# What the fork adds: a log to rebuild it anywhere

Everything this fork does that upstream OpenBot doesn't, written so it can be rebuilt in another app: what Elias gets, why, how it works independent of OpenBot's code, where it lives here, and how to check it. Same format as Codync's `docs/fork/FEATURES.md`.

**Agents: every fork change adds or updates its entry here in the same commit.** Newest last. Build tooling and fixes for this Mac count too.

Skipped features, to pick up later: [LATER.md](LATER.md).

---

## 1. No auto-updates

- **Get:** the app never checks for, downloads or installs an upstream release.
- **Why:** an official release would silently replace the fork's build and every change in this file (Codync lesson, its entry 2).
- **How (portable):** one compile-time switch that the updater's enable condition reads first; keep the upstream updater code so merges stay simple. With the switch off the app uses its own "updates unsupported" path (as for unpacked builds), so no electron-updater module loads.
- **Here:** `updatesAllowed = false` in `src/main/fork.ts`, read as `forkUpdatesAllowed &&` at the top of `updatesEnabled` in `src/main/application-services.ts`, and as `!forkUpdatesAllowed ||` in `createRecoveryUpdater` (`src/main/newer-database-recovery.ts`, upstream #4983, merged 2026-11-04): when the fork opens data that a newer release migrated, it shows upstream's "update required" dialog and does not install upstream's release. `app-update.yml` still ships (unused).
- **Check:** `grep -n 'forkUpdatesAllowed &&' src/main/application-services.ts`; Settings shows updates as unavailable; no `~/Library/Caches/openbot-updater/pending/update.zip` appears after an upstream release.

## 2. Dock launcher: one click builds `main` if it changed, installs, opens

- **Get:** **OpenBot Launcher** in the Dock (like the Codync, Ghostex, FigCraft and EffectCraft launchers). Click: if `main` has a new commit since the last install, a small window builds it (Stop / close / ⌘Q cancel the whole build), replaces `/Applications/OpenBot.app` and opens it; otherwise it just opens OpenBot. Never quits or replaces a running OpenBot.
- **Why:** Elias's apps all rebuild from `main` this way; agents commit without building, and the next launch builds once.
- **How (portable):** a shell script plus a tiny Swift AppKit window that runs it and shows the log's last line (only dyld's `Reason:` / `Referenced from:` / `dyld[` notes are skipped; indented progress lines stay). Stamp file = commit last installed; a `mkdir` lock so one build runs; the build runs in its own process group (`set -m`) so Stop kills every child; build functions are never called inside `&&`/`||` (there `set -e` is silently ignored). Install by moving the bundle (old one moved aside first, then deleted: one copy only). A build made while the app runs (`--build`) is kept with its commit as the next install, so the next click with the app quit installs it at once instead of building again. The launcher is signed with the same Developer ID as the app, so after the first install macOS lets it replace the app without the App Management grant.
- **Here:** `scripts/fork/app.sh`, `scripts/fork/launcher.swift`, `scripts/fork/install-dock-app.sh`. Since 20 Nov 2026 the launcher is a starter inside `/Applications/OpenBot.app` (main executable `OpenBot`, Electron renamed `OpenBot-app`, same signature identity), so the Dock shows one icon; it `execv`s into Electron when `main` is unchanged. State and log in `build/fork-launcher/`.
- **Check:** quit OpenBot, make a commit, click: builds, installs, opens. Click again with no new commit: opens at once. With OpenBot open: `scripts/fork/app.sh --build`, quit, click: installs in a second.

## 3. Fast launcher builds: this Mac only, no timestamp, patch what changed

- **Get:** full build ~1¾ min (103 s measured, 2026-11-03); a commit that only changes the app's JavaScript or resources ~40 s (measured by hand on a clone: electron-vite 7 s, unsigned electron-builder 28 s, swap 1.3 s, sign 3.4 s, verify 0.9 s); a docs-only commit builds nothing. Of upstream's last 60 commits, 37 patch, 18 full, 5 nothing.
- **Why:** Codync lessons (its entry 5): universal builds, slow release profiles and per-file timestamp calls made rebuilds 5½ min.
- **How (portable):** build only the machine's architecture (`--arm64`). Nothing native compiles here (voice runtime, remote desktop runtime and Cua driver are pinned downloads that each skip when present), so no LTO profile to override. Sign without a secure timestamp (`-c.mac.timestamp=none`). `bun install` only when the lockfile, a `package.json` or a patch changed (hash stamp). Diff the installed commit against the new one and sort each path by what it is compiled into: `src/`, `packages/`, the Vite config, `scripts/agent-runtime-lock*` (imported by `src/main`) → the asar; `resources/` → plain files in Resources and the asar (`src/` imports `resources/agent-import/*/SKILL.md?raw` and `resources/plugin-catalog/catalog.json`); tests and e2e/smoke scripts, docs, other apps (mobile, API, bots), CI, lint tooling (`tools/`) and the launcher → nothing; dependencies, runtime locks, `electron-builder.yml`, the fork entitlements and anything unknown → full build. A patch clones the installed app (APFS `cp -Rc`, instant), swaps in `app.asar`, `app.asar.unpacked` (its native addon re-signed) and the `Info.plist` that carries the asar hash (`ElectronAsarIntegrity`, from an unsigned electron-builder run with the same flags), re-signs the outer bundle and checks `codesign --verify --deep --strict`; any failure falls back to a full build. Packaging needs no network: electron-builder gets the cached Electron zip (`-c.electronDist`) when its SHA-256 matches `node_modules/electron/checksums.json`; otherwise it downloads `SHASUMS256.txt` from GitHub on every build, so a GitHub release-server outage (504, then a hang, 8 Nov 2026) stopped the build. A new Electron version with no cached zip still downloads as usual.
- **Here:** `changes`, `patch`, `steps`, `install_deps`, `electron_dist` in `scripts/fork/app.sh`.
- **Check:** `build/fork-launcher/build.log`: first line names the parts (`full`, `( js)`, `( res js)`, `none`), last lines `built in N s` and `installed`.

## 4. Signed with Elias's Developer ID: macOS permissions survive rebuilds

- **Get:** Screen Recording, Accessibility, Microphone and Automation are granted once and stay granted across rebuilds.
- **Why:** ad-hoc or unsigned builds look like a new app to macOS each build, so grants vanished (Codync lesson, its entry 6).
- **How (portable):** sign every build with one stable certificate. `codesign` gets the full name `Developer ID Application: mohamed elias hamadouche (GQQBT37P3D)` (the short name is ambiguous: an Apple Distribution certificate has it too); electron-builder refuses the prefix (`Please remove prefix "Developer ID Application:"`) and wants the short name, picking the Developer ID certificate itself, hardened runtime, the app's entitlements; no notarizing. Drop entitlements that need the original team's provisioning profile (`com.apple.developer.associated-domains`, `com.apple.developer.team-identifier`): without the profile macOS refuses to start an app that claims them. Set `ElectronTeamID` to the new team. Nested binaries signed by a third party (Cua driver) keep their own signature. Grants made for the original signature (team `ZTRDTUL87R`) must be removed (−) and granted again once; the keychain item "OpenBot Safe Storage" asks once ("Always Allow").
- **Here:** `identity`, `entitlements`, `builder_flags` in `scripts/fork/app.sh`; `scripts/fork/entitlements.mac.plist`.
- **Check:** `codesign -dv /Applications/OpenBot.app` shows `TeamIdentifier=GQQBT37P3D`; after a rebuild Computer Use still works without a new prompt.

## 5. Same app identity and data as the release, with a rollback

- **Get:** the fork opens Elias's existing data, agents (Rasta, Booty, Shelf) and settings; the official release can be put back.
- **Why:** the fork replaces the installed release in place.
- **How (portable):** keep the bundle id (`app.openbot.desktop`) and product name, so a packaged run's userData stays `~/Library/Application Support/OpenBot` and the agent workspaces stay in `~/OpenBot`. Before the first install over a build signed by another team, zip that app (`ditto -c -k --keepParent`) and copy its database with `sqlite3 .backup` (upstream `main` migrates the schema forward; an older release refuses a newer database).
- **Here:** `backup_official` in `scripts/fork/app.sh` → `build/official-<version>.zip`, `build/official-<version>-openbot.db`. Rollback steps in [README.md](README.md#data-signature-rollback).
- **Check:** after the first install the agents and chats are there; both backup files exist.

## 6. The `$` list shows the agent's own skills and commands

- **Get:** typing `$` in an agent's box lists, after OpenBot's skills and `~/.agents/skills`, every skill its CLI loads: for Claude all of `~/.claude/skills` (editing-harvest, effectcraft-motion…, ~84 with a `SKILL.md`), the `.claude/skills` of its project folders, and, once a session of the agent started, what Claude itself says it has: plugin skills (`figma:figma-use`, `voicemode:converse`…) and Claude Code's built-in commands (`compact`, `context`, `init`, `review`…, badge **Command**); for an ACP agent (Codex ACP, Gemini, OpenCode…) the commands it lists itself. MCP servers stay in the list. Picking a skill makes the agent use it; picking a command as the first thing in the message runs it (`$compact keep the API notes` = `/compact keep the API notes`).
- **Why:** the box said "This agent has no skills" though Claude agents load all 113 of Elias's skills (Codync lesson, its entry 10 / ADR 0007).
- **How (portable):** list per agent, on request, what the engine itself has: its skill folders in the user's home (provider's skill folders, `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` for Claude) and in each project folder, plus the agent's last command list (kept in memory per agent; a change makes the open lists read again): an ACP agent's `session/update` `available_commands_update` (it arrives outside turns too, so read it before the "is a turn active" check), and for Claude, which runs on the Agent SDK and sends no ACP update, the query's `supportedCommands()` at each session start (also after an idle release), filtered again at `system/init` (its `terminal_slash_commands`) and replaced by `system/commands_changed`. Left out of Claude's list, as claude-agent-acp does: `clear cost keybindings-help login logout mcp output-style:new release-notes todos`, terminal-only commands, and the `agents:` / `openbot:` plugin skills (OpenBot's own, already listed). Disk skills come first, so they show before the first session; names already listed are left out. A skill's tag id is `agent:<name>`; the provider reads it as `/<name> (skill)`: Claude Code runs a skill the user names as `/<name>` with its Skill tool, and ACP agents know their commands by that name. A Claude Code built-in command (`SlashCommand.builtin`) is `agent-cmd:<name>` and reaches the provider as a bare `/<name>` (no " (skill)", which Claude would read as arguments); at the very start of the message, with the blank before it dropped, Claude Code runs it, and OpenBot's Claude client already sends a message that starts with `/` without its memory/teammate blocks. Elsewhere in the text it stays words for the model. So built-ins stay in `$` (decided from the code): a plain user message reaches `turn/start` unwrapped; a reply-to, a teammate message or a handoff wraps the text, and then the command does not run.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-agent-skills.ts` (`claudeCommandsForList`, `expandAgentCommandTags`); `listInstalledForChatTags` in `src/main/skill-marketplace-service.ts` (last constructor argument from `application-services.ts`); `available_commands_update` in `#sessionUpdate` of `src/backend/acp-client.ts`, and `#forkEmitCommands` in `src/backend/claude-client.ts` (after `#startThread`, `init` / `commands_changed` in `#handleMessage`) → `openbot/agentCommands/updated` → `agentCommands` in `src/backend/agent-service.ts`; tag text in `displayMessageReferences` of `src/backend/agent/delivery-content.ts`; `AGENT_COMMAND_ID_PREFIX` in `packages/contracts/src/ipc-skills.ts`; badge in `pickerOptionBadge` (`packages/ui/src/features/conversation/composer-picker.ts`, icon `SquareSlash`, i18n `composer.picker.badge.command`, English only).
- **Not done:** the command list is in memory only, so after an OpenBot restart plugin skills and built-ins come back with the agent's first session; a built-in later in the text does not run; a user skill named like a built-in hides the built-in in the list (Claude runs the built-in).
- **Check:** in Shelf's box type `$edit`: editing-harvest, editing-cut… appear (badge Workspace, place `~/.claude/skills/…`). Pick editing-harvest, send "what does this skill do": Shelf calls its Skill tool for editing-harvest. After one message to Shelf, `$figma` lists `figma:figma-use`… and `$comp` lists `compact` with badge Command. Send `$compact` alone: the chat shows the compaction (context bar drops), no "(skill)" answer.

## 7. Waits: an agent sleeps until something is done, then carries on in the same chat

- **Get:** an agent can say "wake me when this render is written / this process exits / that agent finishes / this URL answers", end its turn, and get woken in the same chat and provider session, knowing everything it knew. The wait shows above the composer (upstream's open-waits block) with Cancel; a macOS notification when it ends; the wake shows in the chat as a routine-style marker "⏳ Wait over: <note>".
- **Why:** long EffectCraft renders made agents poll (tokens) or stop and forget. Upstream 0.59/main has waits (`await_event`), but only a routine run or a script event wakes them: the agent must make a routine and a detached script. Codync lesson (its entry 3 / ADR 0004): plain code watches, the wake goes to the existing session.
- **How (portable):** tool `wait_until {note, file+settleSeconds | pid | agentId | url, timeoutMinutes ≤ 1440}`, exactly one condition; it returns at once and the agent ends its turn. The wait is an ordinary open wait (so `list_waits` lists it and `close_wait` or the user's Cancel cancels it, with no wake); what is watched goes to a JSON file next to the database, so waits survive a restart. Every 2 s plain code checks: file exists and `size:mtime` unchanged for `settleSeconds` (a file already there must change first), `kill(pid, 0)` fails, the other agent was busy (a turn or a queued message) and now is not (idle at the start means its next work), the URL answers < 500 (checked every 10 s). On fire or timeout: the wait is closed, a notification, and a delivery with a routine sender (`routineId` `fork-wait`, no real routine) is queued to the agent's own chat; its text is the whole prompt ("[OpenBot: ⏳ wait over] … not a message from the user", note, what was watched, result, the request it continues). A routine run never wakes a watched wait. Refused in channel/messaging threads, like `await_event`. The wake runs in the agent's own provider session: upstream runs every routine-sender delivery in a fresh ephemeral session with a transcript excerpt (`DrainScheduler.#runsInOwnSession`), so the fork excludes `fork-wait` there (fixed 2026-11-03; before, the woken agent only had the excerpt). The wake's routine name ("⏳ Wait over: <note>") is cut to the contract's 80 characters: a longer one failed to decode as a chat message, so the wake failed with "Invalid conversation message." (fixed 2026-11-03; a wake that a build before the fix stored still reads, because upstream's `routineNameInLimit` in `legacy-conversation-message.ts` (#4922) now cuts every routine name on read and replaced the fork's own `withShortRoutineName`; the queue (`publicSender` in `mailbox-store.ts`) cuts it too: one such row made the phone refuse Rasta's whole queue with "Queue unavailable / Request failed.", fixed 2026-11-04).
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent-waits/fork-wait-conditions.ts` (`ForkWaitConditions`); routed from `AgentWaits.handleTool` (`useConditions`, filter in `wakeForRun`); built in `src/backend/agent-service.ts` (wake via `RoutineScheduler.enqueueHandoff`, boot-recovery `quietRoutineDelivery` exception, `forkNotify` from `src/main/application-services.ts`); tool in `src/backend/openbot-tools.ts`; prompt line in `developer-instructions.ts`; wake text in `deliveryPromptInput` (`delivery-content.ts`). File: `~/Library/Application Support/OpenBot/fork-wait-conditions.json`.
- **Not done:** no separate `cancel_wait` (upstream's `close_wait` cancels); the card is upstream's open-waits block, not a card in the timeline; no wait by process name.
- **Check:** tell an agent "the secret word is heron; wait_until the file /tmp/heron.txt, then tell me the word"; it ends its turn and the wait shows above the box. `echo hi > /tmp/heron.txt`: within ~7 s a notification, "⏳ Wait over" in the chat, and the agent says "heron". A second wait cancelled with its Cancel: no wake.

## 8. Project folder as the agent's home ("Work here")

- **Get:** in an agent's Settings › Folders, each added folder has **Work here** (the Workspace row too, to go back). The agent's sessions then start in that folder, so Claude loads the project's `CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/skills`, `.claude/settings*.json` and hooks, as when you run `claude` there. The agent's own folder (`~/OpenBot/Agents/<id>`) stays its home for OpenBot's files.
- **Why:** agents always started in `~/OpenBot/Agents/<id>` and got project folders only as extra directories, so Shelf needed an AGENTS.md line "cd into motion-kit first and follow its CLAUDE.md". Codync's bots have a working folder.
- **How (portable):** an optional `workFolder` on the agent, which must be one of its added folders (removing that folder, or a folder that is gone, falls back to the workspace). The session's `cwd` is the work folder on start, resume, each turn and history reads; the writable roots stay the same (they include it). Changing it restarts the agent's sessions like a folder change. Because the CLI no longer sees the agent's own instruction file, that file's text goes into the prompt, and the prompt says "you work in the project folder X; your own folder is Y". Claude resumes a session only from its cwd's project folder (`~/.claude/projects/<sanitized cwd>/<id>.jsonl`), so before a resume the transcript is copied there when it is elsewhere, with its `<session>/subagents/` folder (entry 42): the same session goes on, with its memory.
- **Here:** `agentSessionCwd` in `src/backend/agent/workspace-sandbox.ts`, used in `thread-lifecycle.ts` (`#startParams`, `threadParams`, `#instructionsFile`), `drain-scheduler.ts` (`turn/start`), `boot-recovery.ts`; `workFolder` in `AgentSummary` (`packages/contracts/src/ipc-agents.ts`), `src/backend/agent-store.ts` (update, read, copy), `standingProfileChanged` (`profile-save.ts`), prompt line in `developer-instructions.ts`; `carryClaudeTranscript` (`claude-history.ts`, called from `#startThread` in `claude-client.ts`); IPC `agent:folders:work-folder` (`setAgentWorkFolder`: contracts, preload, `agent-folder-handlers.ts`, `setWorkFolder` in `src/main/agent-folders.ts`, preview mock); UI `FolderRow` / `AgentFoldersPage` in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/AgentFolders.tsx`, i18n `agentSettings.folders.workHere*` (English only).
- **Not done:** project `.mcp.json` servers still don't load (upstream's `strictMcpConfig`: OpenBot's MCP panel is the only door); local agents only (not on a joined server); other providers get the cwd but their transcript move isn't handled.
- **Check:** Shelf › Settings › Folders › Add folder… `/Users/elias/Developer/motion-kit` › Work here. Ask Shelf "what is your cwd, and what does your CLAUDE.md say?": `/Users/elias/Developer/motion-kit` and the motion-kit rules; it still remembers the earlier chat. Then remove the "Workspace: … cd into it first" line from `~/OpenBot/Agents/agent-7c6c0058-…/AGENTS.md`.

## 9. Claude Code's MCP servers in the MCP panel ("From Claude Code")

- **Get:** the MCP servers panel lists every user-level server of Claude Code (`~/.claude.json` `mcpServers`: cua, figcraft-app, hindsight, linear, motion-library, motion-library-remote, pencil, quickshotx, reelscope) with a **From Claude Code** badge and "Edit in ~/.claude.json". All agents get them (Claude, Codex and the ACP providers read the same list). No Edit or Remove; the switch works and stays as set. Test and Sign in (linear) work.
- **Why:** OpenBot starts Claude with `strictMcpConfig: true` ("the MCP panel is the only door"), so agents saw none of Elias's Claude Code servers, and retyping them would drift.
- **How (portable):** keep the panel as the one list and add the mirrored servers to what it lists, not to its table. Each read of the list stats `~/.claude.json` (`$CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR/.claude.json` when set) and converts the top-level `mcpServers` again when its mtime or size changed: `stdio` → command/args/env, `http` → url/headers; `sse` and other types are skipped (the panel has no SSE client). Each entry goes through the panel's own normalize/validate; a bad one is skipped and logged by name only. Ids are `claude-code-<name>` (hand-made rows are `mcp-<uuid>`), which is what makes them read-only: save and remove refuse them ("This MCP server comes from Claude Code. Edit it in ~/.claude.json."). A hand-made row with the same name wins: the mirrored one is left out and logged. The list stays within the panel's 32 servers. Env values and headers are never written anywhere by OpenBot: they live in memory and are registered for log redaction like stored rows. The on/off switch is kept by name in `fork-claude-code-mcp-off.json` beside `openbot.db`. A `fs.watchFile` poll (3 s, stat only, not persistent) re-reads the file; when the `mcpServers` result changes, every agent gets a fresh provider session at its next turn, as after a panel save. `~/.claude.json` is only read, never written. Project `.mcp.json`, `projects.<path>.mcpServers` and plugin servers are left out.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-claude-code-mcp.ts` (`ClaudeCodeMcpMirror`); `list`, `get`, `save`, `remove`, `setEnabled`, `watchClaudeCode` in `src/backend/mcp-server-store.ts`; `watchClaudeCode` / `close` in `src/backend/agent/mcp-gateway.ts`; constructed in `src/backend/agent-service.ts`, watch started in the "restore channel links" startup step; `CLAUDE_CODE_MCP_SERVER_ID_PREFIX` / `isClaudeCodeMcpServerId` in `packages/contracts/src/ipc-mcp-servers.ts`; subtitle "From Claude Code", note and no Edit/Remove in `CustomServerPage` of `packages/ui/src/features/marketplace/MarketplaceAppPage.tsx` (upstream #10423 moved custom MCP servers from Server settings into Marketplace › Apps; merge of 18 Nov 2026); i18n `mcp.panel.fromClaudeCode*`, `error.backend.mcpServerFromClaudeCode` (English only).
- **Not done:** `${VAR}` expansion in values (none of Elias's entries use it); the open panel does not refresh by itself after a `~/.claude.json` change (reopen it).
- **Check:** Marketplace › Apps › a Claude Code server's page (Server settings › MCP servers before the 18 Nov 2026 merge): **From Claude Code**, no Edit or Remove; Test (and Sign in for linear) work. Switch hindsight off, quit and reopen OpenBot: still off. Ask an agent "which MCP tools do you have?": cua, reelscope… are listed. Add a server to `~/.claude.json` with `claude mcp add -s user …`: the next turn of an agent has it; reopen the panel to see it.

## 10. `$` in channels: the members' skills, with who has each

- **Get:** typing `$` (or + › skill) in a channel lists every skill of its members, each once, with the members that have it (`editing-harvest · Shelf`, `figma:figma-use · Shelf, Boty`). Picking one that a single member has also puts `@Shelf` at the start of the message, so the channel gives it to Shelf only; when several have it, only the skill goes in and the user adds the `@`. The member that gets the message uses the skill; a member without it reads the tag as plain text. No built-in commands (`/compact`) and no MCP servers in a channel's `$` list.
- **Why:** the channel's `$` said "This agent has no skills" (upstream turned the picker off there: "a skill belongs to one agent"). Upstream #4781 (merged 2026-11-04) now lists the members' skills too, but with no owners and with CLI commands; the fork keeps its list. Same as Codync group chats (its entry 10 "Groups", ADR 0007).
- **How (portable):** the list is the union of each member's own `$` list (entry 6), merged by skill id, with the members that list it as owners; built-in commands are left out, since they run only at the very start of a session's prompt and a channel turn starts with the channel's own text. A pick of a one-owner skill adds a mention of that owner in front of the draft unless the draft's opening address already names it, because a channel routes only by the mentions at the start of a message (`leadingAddress`). On the host, when a member's channel packet is built (also the delta and a correction steered into a running turn), the request is a person's message and it tags a skill that this member's own `$` list has, the packet starts with `The user picked your /<name> skill for this message. Use it.` (`/<name>` for a CLI skill as in a 1:1 chat, the skill name for the others), because the user's text is inside the packet and a `/<name>` there does not run by itself. A member that cannot list its skills gets no line.
- **Here:** `channelSkills` in `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/fork-channel-skills.ts`, on upstream's per-agent loading in `stores/skills-store.ts` (#4781: `skillsByAgent`, a failed member keeps its last list), where `showLoadedSkills` calls `channelSkills` in a channel; `ComposerSkill.owners` in `packages/ui/src/data.ts`; `skillOwnersText` in `composer-picker.ts` (i18n `composer.picker.skillOwners`, English only); `addressSkillOwner` in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/ComposerEditor.tsx`; `pickSkill` and `mcpServers` in `ConversationComposer.tsx`. Host: `pickedSkillsLine` in `src/backend/fork-agent-skills.ts`; `#pickedSkills` and hook `skillsOf` in `src/backend/channel-service.ts` (`prepare` and the steer path); `forkChatSkills` in `src/backend/agent-service.ts`, set in `src/main/application-services.ts` to `SkillMarketplaceService.listInstalledForChatTags`.
- **Not done:** with no member skills the empty list still says "This agent has no skills"; a pick does not remove other members already addressed at the start; plugin skills of a member show only after that member's first session (as in entry 6).
- **Check:** in a channel with Shelf (Claude) type `$edit`: editing-harvest, editing-cut… with `· Shelf`. Pick editing-harvest: the draft becomes `@Shelf @editing-harvest `. Add "what does this skill do" and send: only Shelf answers, and it calls its Skill tool for editing-harvest. Type `$comp`: no `compact`.

## 11. `/goal`: an agent keeps working until the goal is done

- **Get:** in an agent's own chat, `/goal <goal>` (or `$` › goal) makes it work turn after turn without you: each turn does real work, then the next starts by itself, in the same provider session. A line in the chat per turn ("🎯 Goal turn 3 of 25: <next step>"). It ends when the agent reports completed / needs-input / blocked, at the turn limit (default 25, `/goal --turns N <goal>`, max 200), when you press Stop, or with `/goal stop`. `/goal continue` gives it the same number of turns again. A notification when it ends or needs you. Teammates: the agent can hand parts to other agents with `send_message` and report "waiting"; their answers start its next turn.
- **Why:** Elias picked it from OpenMausBot ("a team keeps working until the goal is done"). Upstream's plan continuation stops after 8 turns and only while a plan has open steps.
- **How (portable):** the message is rewritten into a goal prompt (adapted from OpenMausBot's lead prompt, Apache-2.0): goal, "turn n of N", do the next concrete piece of work, never claim completion without the deliverable, end each turn with the tool `goal_status {status: continue|waiting|completed|needs-input|blocked, next | detail}`. A tool, not OpenMausBot's hidden `<openmaus-goal>` text block, so nothing has to be cut from the streamed answer. At the end of each own-chat turn: `continue` → the turn continues the way a plan does (same deliveries requeued, same session, the goal prompt with "Your next step: …" as the note), unless the turn failed, was stopped, the agent asked the user in the UI, has an open wait, or another message waits in the queue (then the goal pauses and goes on at the next turn). No `goal_status` → one reminder turn, then blocked. While a goal runs, every other prompt in the agent's own chat (your message, a teammate's answer) ends with a goal footer that asks for `goal_status`. State per agent in `fork-goals.json` beside the database (a restart pauses the chain; the next message or `/goal continue` resumes).
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-goals.ts` (`ForkGoals`, `goalPrompt`, tool schema); `continueGoal` + `claim` in `src/backend/agent/plan-continuation.ts`; the turn-end hook (`goalTurn`) in `src/backend/agent/turn-lifecycle.ts`; the command and footers in `src/backend/fork-commands.ts`, called from `deliveryPromptInput` (`delivery-content.ts`); tool in `openbot-tools.ts`, routed in `openbot-tool-router.ts`; built in `agent-service.ts` (`#forkLine` writes the chat lines with the plan-continuation item type); `src/backend/fork-hub.ts` holds the instance; i18n `status.agent.goal*` (English only).
- **Lines:** every goal line is written with a turn's own snapshot, so the chat shows it at once: an end line ("✅ / ⏸ / ⛔") with the turn that ends the goal (`takeLine`), a command line ("🎯 Goal resumed", "replaced", "⏹ stopped") with the turn that the `/goal` command starts (`takeStartLines`, at `turn/started`). A line that no turn takes within 60 s is appended on its own (fixed 2026-11-03: appended lines showed only after a reload). A long line ("🎯 Goal waits: <detail>") wraps inside the marker (`.chat-action-marker-plan-continuation` in `packages/ui/.../styles/messages.css`); before, it ran past the chat's right edge.
- **Update after the call (fixed 2026-11-06):** the chat shows only the text after a turn's last tool call as its answer. Claude (`claude-client.ts`, `#flushNarration`) and ACP fold the text before a call into the "N steps" row; Codex marks it commentary itself. So the goal prompt, the footer, the `/goal` status prompt, the tool text and every `goal_status` result now say: call `goal_status` when the work is done, then write the update. Before, they said "update, then `goal_status` as your last action", so on Claude every goal update was hidden in the steps and only a one-liner after the call showed (Shelf, 6 Nov: "Waiting for your pick: 🅰 or 🅱" with the explanation folded away; the phone push of feature 39 sent only that line too). Not done in the provider: a `goal_status` that keeps the text before it as the answer. The boot re-import of Claude history (`claude-history.ts`, `reconcileClaudeHistoryMessages`) would then disagree with the rows already stored and give old goal turns a second bubble.
- **Not done:** `/goal` in a channel (own chat only); no goal card with a Stop button (Stop the turn, or `/goal stop`); a teammate that never answers leaves the goal waiting (send any message or `/goal continue`).
- **Check:** in Shelf's chat: `/goal --turns 4 Write /tmp/goal-test.txt with the numbers 1 to 3, one per turn, then report completed`. Lines "🎯 Goal turn 2 of 4…", the file has 1 2 3, then "✅ Goal completed". Start another and press Stop: "⏹ Goal stopped." A `needs-input` turn: its question shows as the answer bubble, not only inside "N steps".

## 12. `/setup`, `/learn`, `/memory`

- **Get:** `/setup [job]`: the agent asks ≤ 4 questions (job, when, which apps, which folder), says what it will set, then sets itself up with OpenBot's own tools (profile, AGENTS.md rules, skills, routines, API keys, Composio connect links) and ends with what you still do by hand. `/learn [source]`: turns this chat (or a folder or URL) into a skill in OpenBot's skill library, installed for that agent. `/memory`: lists the latest memory changes and undoes one when you ask. All three are in the `$` list.
- **Why:** Elias picked /setup and /learn from OpenMausBot; /memory is the chat side of entry 13's journal.
- **How (portable):** a message that starts with the command is rewritten into an instruction prompt before the provider sees it (a provider would take an unknown `/name` as its own command). /setup and /learn prompts are adapted from OpenMausBot (`server/setup-mode.ts`, `server/skill-learn.ts`, Apache-2.0) to OpenBot's tools: `update_profile`, the workspace AGENTS.md (edits show with Undo), `create_skill` (SKILL.md folder in the workspace → local library + install), `create_routine`, `request_api_key`, Composio's `COMPOSIO_MANAGE_CONNECTIONS`. The rewritten prompt stays in the session history, so the answers turn after a /setup question still has it.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-commands.ts` (`FORK_COMMANDS`, `forkUserPrompt`, prompts); `$` list entries first in `listAgentOwnSkills` (`src/backend/fork-agent-skills.ts`).
- **Not done:** the "Set me up" card on a new agent; learned skills go to OpenBot's library, not `~/.claude/skills`.
- **Check:** `/setup a bot that summarizes my GitHub notifications every morning`: up to 4 questions; answer; it changes its profile/AGENTS.md and makes a routine. After a chat about a task: `/learn`: a new skill in Settings › Skills.

## 13. Memory: recall per message, journal with Undo, nightly tidy

- **Get:** (1) every message you send in an agent's own chat starts with up to 4 of its saved memories that share words with it (its "always" memories are in its prompt already). (2) Agent settings › Memories › **History**: every change (added / changed / forgotten, by the agent, you, the review, the nightly tidy, an undo) with **Undo**; `/memory` and the tools `memory_journal` / `memory_undo` do the same from the chat. (3) After 03:00 once a day, each idle agent with ≥ 5 memories is tidied: contradictions (later wins unless the earlier is the correction), duplicates merged, passed plans forgotten; never a memory you wrote or chose; at most 20 % forgotten per pass; the changes show in its chat as a memory line.
- **Why:** Elias picked them from OpenMausBot. Upstream already has essential/searchable memories, a search tool and a background review, but the agent must think to search, changes cannot be undone after the fact, and nothing tidies.
- **How (portable):** recall = the upstream FTS5 bm25 memory search with the message's content words (stop words out, ≤ 16), a hit needs 2 matching terms when the message has ≥ 5, block ≤ 6000 chars, put in the user message (not the system prompt: the cache stays), wrapper text adapted from OpenMausBot's `recall.ts`. Journal = snapshot diff: `AgentMemories.stateChanged` (the one place every change passes) diffs the memory texts against the last seen ones and appends lines to `fork-memory-journal/<agentId>.ndjson` (0600, last 500); the actor comes from the call site (`nextActor`). Undo goes through the normal create/update/delete. Tidy = one generation without tools on the review's background model (`MemoryReview.forkGenerate`), prompt adapted from OpenMausBot's `memory-tidy.ts`, answered as the review's JSON operations and applied with `applyReview` (its checks: locked memories, current text). The journal keeps the text of forgotten memories so undo works (local only; upstream purges a forgotten memory's events).
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-memory.ts` (`ForkMemory`); hooks in `src/backend/agent/agent-memories.ts` (`nextActor`, `sync` in `stateChanged`, `searchFor`), `forkGenerate` in `memory-review.ts`; recall via `forkUserPrompt` (`fork-commands.ts`); IPC `agent:fork-memory-journal` / `agent:fork-memory-undo` (contracts `ipc-agent-memories.ts`, `ipc-endpoints.ts`; preload `decodeMemoryJournal`; `src/main/ipc/memory-handlers.ts`, `parseUndoAgentMemoryChange`; `AgentService.forkMemoryJournal` / `forkUndoMemoryChange`; preview mock); UI `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/MemoryJournalList.tsx` in `AgentMemoriesView`, port in `memories-port.ts`, `AgentMemoriesSettings.tsx`; i18n `memory.journal.*` (English only). State `fork-memory-state.json` (last tidy date). Test switch: start the app with `OPENBOT_FORK_TIDY_NOW=<agentId>` (`open -a OpenBot --env OPENBOT_FORK_TIDY_NOW=…`) and the first check (1 min after start) tidies that agent at any hour, without changing the last tidy date.
- **Not done:** channel memories (agents only); OpenMausBot's topic files and "organize" step (OpenBot has essential/searchable instead); a tidy button.
- **Check:** tell Shelf "remember that my sister lives in Lyon", then in a new message "what should I bring when I visit my sister?": the prompt starts with the recalled Lyon memory (Settings › Diagnostics trace or ask "what was recalled?"). Memories › History shows "Added by the agent"; Undo removes it.

## 14. Composio: ~1,000 apps for every agent

- **Get:** every agent has Composio's tools (Gmail, Calendar, GitHub, Notion, Slack, Drive…): it searches the right tool, runs it, and for an app not yet connected gives you a Composio connect link. Apps connected in Ghostpoint work here too (same Composio user). The MCP panel shows a read-only **composio** row (switch works). For Elias only, not shared.
- **Why:** Elias asked for Composio ("just for myself").
- **How (portable):** Composio Tool Router (REST v3.1): `POST /tool_router/session {user_id, manage_connections: on, workbench: off, multi_account: explicit selection}` → `mcp.url` (must be on `*.composio.dev`); the session id + URL (no key) are kept in `fork-composio.json` and reused while `GET /tool_router/session/:id` knows them. The key comes from the Keychain service `COMPOSIO_API_KEY` via `/usr/bin/security` at start (`ak_` → `x-api-key`, `uak_` → `x-user-api-key`), stays in memory, is registered for redaction, and goes only in the MCP server's headers. The row is an extra server of the Claude Code mirror (entry 9), so it gets the same read-only handling and on/off file; when the session is ready, agents get it at their next turn. A prompt line says how to use the meta tools and to ask before sending, posting, deleting or paying. User id `ghostpoint-<mac user>` = Ghostpoint's.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-composio.ts` (`ForkComposio`, `COMPOSIO_INSTRUCTIONS`); `extra` + `refresh` in `src/backend/fork-claude-code-mcp.ts`, `refreshClaudeCode` in `mcp-server-store.ts` / `mcp-gateway.ts`; started in `agent-service.ts` after the mirror's watch; prompt line in `developer-instructions.ts`.
- **Marketplace:** upstream's catalog has a Composio listing; the session row matches it (same name, URL on `composio.dev`), so Marketplace › Your apps shows Composio as Connected with its logo and prompts. Rows that come from elsewhere (Composio, Claude Code) get no "Change key" / sign-in button there (`readOnly` in `MarketplaceAppConnection`, set in `marketplace-controller.ts`): the save would refuse them (fixed 2026-11-03).
- **Not done:** a Connect UI in Settings (links come from the agent in the chat); per-agent app filters; the row shows the "From Claude Code" badge.
- **Check:** Marketplace › Apps: a **composio** server. Ask Shelf "what's on my Google Calendar tomorrow?": it uses COMPOSIO_SEARCH_TOOLS then COMPOSIO_MULTI_EXECUTE_TOOL and answers. "Connect my Gmail": it gives a connect link.

## 15. Compact before the cache goes cold, or keep it warm (`/keepwarm`)

- **Get:** a Claude agent's own chat that is quiet for 55 minutes, with 100k tokens or more of context, is compacted by itself while its prompt cache is still warm. The chat shows the routine-style marker "🗜 Compact before the cache goes cold (164k tokens)" and Claude's compaction line. `/keepwarm [2h | 90m | 1h30m | 3 | off]` (`$` list too; default 2 hours, max 12) keeps the whole chat instead: while it is quiet, a ping 50 minutes after its last turn keeps the cache one more hour; the chat shows only a collapsed "♨️ Keep the cache warm (until 15:40)" routine row. After the window, the compaction rule takes over again. An idle Claude process stays open 60 minutes (upstream 10). Elias's Claude Code mod `cache-tax` is off in OpenBot's Claude processes (it stays on in Claude Code).
- **Why:** Claude keeps the cache one hour. After that, the next message writes the whole context again (Rasta, 4 Nov 2026: 164k tokens, $1.64 at list price against $0.08 warm). The `cache-tax` mod then dropped the message once ("Prompt dropped by a hook: cache-tax…"): fine in a terminal, wrong in OpenBot, where a routine, a wait's wake, a `/goal` turn or a teammate never sends it again. Its own `/keepwarm` runs inside the `claude` process, which OpenBot closed after 10 idle minutes. Elias chose "between keep-warm and compact", from 100k tokens, then the `/keepwarm` window and the 60-minute process life (4 Nov 2026; on a 64 GB Mac the extra RAM is at most two idle processes, ~0.5 GB).
- **How (portable):** `ForkCacheCompact` notes when a turn of the agent's own chat ends (`turnEnded`, from `TurnLifecycle`). A check each minute: quiet ≥ 55 and < 60 minutes, not busy (turn, unresolved delivery, compaction), Claude (`contextCompaction === "native"`), and the last reported context ≥ 100k (`ContextCompaction.snapshot`). Then a delivery with a routine sender (`routineId` `fork-compact`, no real routine) whose text is the whole prompt: `/compact <what to keep>`; Claude Code runs a prompt that starts with `/compact` as its command, and OpenBot adds no context blocks before a `/` prompt. It runs in the agent's own session, like the wait wake. One try per quiet stretch. Quiet for ≥ 60 minutes (the Mac slept): nothing, the cache is cold already. Keep-warm: `/keepwarm` is rewritten (like `/goal`) into a one-line confirmation prompt and sets a window end per agent (in memory). Inside the window, quiet ≥ 50 and < 60 minutes → a delivery with `routineId` `fork-keepwarm` whose prompt asks for exactly the quiet routine marker `[[no-update]]`; the delivery counts as a quiet routine run (`quietRoutineDelivery`), so the answer folds into the routine row. The ping turn is a new turn end, so the next ping follows 50 minutes later. Idle release: `CLAUDE_THREAD_IDLE_RELEASE_MS` 60 minutes, so a ping or compaction reaches a running process; `CLAUDE_IDLE_THREAD_LIMIT` (2) is unchanged. The mod is off through the query's flag settings (`enabledPlugins: { "cache-tax@claude-code-mods": false }`).
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-cache-compact.ts` (`ForkCacheCompact`, `keepWarm`, `parseKeepWarm`, prompts, `isForkCacheRoutine`); built in `src/backend/agent-service.ts` (`forkHub.cacheCompact`, `#forkCacheTurn` enqueues via `RoutineScheduler.enqueueHandoff`, `#forkKeepWarmDelivery` in both `quietRoutineDelivery` hooks); `/keepwarm` in `src/backend/fork-commands.ts`; `turnEnded` call in `src/backend/agent/turn-lifecycle.ts`; prompt pass-through in `delivery-content.ts`; own-session exception in `drain-scheduler.ts` (`#runsInOwnSession`); flag settings and the idle release in `src/backend/claude-client.ts`. Test switch: `open -a OpenBot --env OPENBOT_FORK_COMPACT_QUIET_MINUTES=2` (ping after 1 quiet minute, compaction after 2).
- **Not done:** Codex and the other providers; a setting to turn the compaction off; a window survives no app restart; after an app restart a chat counts only from its next turn. The phone chat's blank space below a short last turn is upstream's "latest message to the top" space (mobile `anchoredEndSpace`, 19 Oct 2026), not this feature.
- **Check:** in Rasta's chat `/keepwarm 2h` → one line "warm until …". Leave it 50 minutes: a collapsed "♨️ Keep the cache warm" row, no answer bubble; Claude's transcript shows the ping with a cache read of the whole context. Two hours later with ≥ 100k tokens: the 🗜 compaction.

## 16. Contrast slider (all windows; more solid glass)

- **Get:** Settings › General › Appearance › **Contrast**, 0–100 %. 0 = upstream's look. Upward, the gray text (secondary, muted, readable) moves toward the primary text and the lines get stronger, in the opaque and the transparent window. In the dark palette the backgrounds also get darker: at 100 % the canvas and sidebar are #111 and the frame #070707, as Codync's dark window. With **Transparent window** on, it also makes the glass tint more solid: 100 % = a solid window like Codync's dark one. Each step shows at once; the release saves it.
- **Why:** Elias, 4 Nov 2026: the glass let too much of the desktop through next to Codync's high-contrast window, and he wanted to set it himself. First version worked only with Transparent window on; he asked for it in the opaque window too (same day). Codync itself has no slider (only Light/Dark/System on solid colors).
- **How (portable):** one CSS variable `--window-glass-contrast` (0–1) on the root. Glass: every clear tint of the window gets `color-mix(token calc(p + (100 % − p)·c), transparent)`, where p is upstream's alpha (frame 20 %, card 30 %, canvas and sidebar 25 %, surfaces and controls 35–55 %; since upstream #5161, 6 Nov 2026, the surfaces in the window are clear too, so the slider has to cover all of them to reach a solid window). The tints and the solid values for poppers are set again on `body`, so they use the darker fork backgrounds. Text and lines: a custom property cannot read itself, so the root copies the palette's values under `--fork-base-*`, and `body` sets each token to `color-mix(text-primary k·c, base)` (secondary k = 70 %, muted and readable 55 %, border 10 %, strong border 14 %). Backgrounds (dark palette only, `:root:not([data-openbot-color-scheme="light"]) body`): `color-mix(#000 k·c, base)`, canvas and sidebar k = 35 % (#1a1a1a → #111), frame 46 % (#0d0d0d → #070707), surface and control 20 %. The glass tints are set on `body` too, so they use the darker frame and canvas. Codync colors sampled from Elias's screenshots, 4 Nov 2026: he said max "doesn't get as dark as Codync". The value is a main-window preference of this computer, so it stays in browser storage (`openbot.fork.glassContrast`) and is applied before the first paint; no IPC or main setting.
- **Here:** `src/renderer/src/fork-glass-contrast.ts` (`syncGlassContrast`, `useGlassContrastChoice`); called in `src/renderer/src/index.tsx`; row in `src/renderer/src/features/settings/SettingsGeneralTab.tsx` (`SliderField` from `@openbot/ui`); glass in `src/renderer/src/styles/window-glass.css`, text and lines in `src/renderer/src/styles/fork-contrast.css`; i18n `settings.appearance.glassContrast.*` (English only).
- **Not done:** other windows (picture-in-picture browser, helper windows) keep upstream's contrast; joined web/phone clients; light-palette backgrounds; the macOS blur strength itself (native, not CSS).
- **Check:** Transparent window off, drag Contrast to 100 %: gray previews and labels in the sidebar get almost white, lines show, and the sidebar and chat get as dark as Codync (#111). Turn it on: at 100 % the desktop no longer shows through. Quit and reopen: the value stays.

## 17. Sidebar folders: Codync's smooth open/close, no dark header band in the glass window

- **Get:** clicking a folder or section header opens and closes its rows like Codync: height and fade together, 300 ms on a spring. With **Transparent window** on, a folder header (e.g. "Editing") is clear like the rest of the sidebar instead of a dark square band; there it scrolls with its rows instead of sticking.
- **Why:** Elias, 4 Nov 2026 (screen recording of Codync's "Editing" section + OpenBot screenshots): OpenBot's fold felt abrupt (upstream: height 160 ms, fade 120 ms, so the rows vanished before the space closed), and the folder header showed as a dark band over the glass.
- **How (portable):** the fold is a one-row grid (`grid-template-rows: 1fr ↔ 0fr`) plus opacity; both get the same duration and Codync's spring as a CSS `linear(…)` easing (16 stops, slight overshoot). Reduced motion keeps upstream's "no transition". During a drag (`.agent-list[data-sidebar-dragging]`) the fold keeps upstream's 160/120 ms: the drag engine measures its drop targets again only on the fold's `transitionend`, so the 300 ms fold made dragging rough while a folder sprang open (Elias, 4 Nov 2026). The sticky folder header painted the opaque sidebar color (`--openbot-bg-sidebar`), which equals the card only when the window is opaque; in glass mode it is `transparent` and `position: static` (it does not stick, so no row scrolls under it), and a drop-target header gets only the accent tint. Not `backdrop-filter`: the first version (99dd4d2ac) blurred the rows under the sticky header, and in the transparent macOS window that left old frames on screen: the last chat showed through the new one and a closed trace panel stayed half-visible. Turning Transparent window off removed every ghost (checked with cua-driver on the installed build, 4 Nov 2026).
- **Upstream #5161 (merged 6 Nov 2026)** blurs the sticky folder header in the glass window (`backdrop-filter` in `window-glass.css`). The fork rule sets `backdrop-filter: none` on it, because that blur left the ghosts described above.
- **Here:** `src/renderer/src/styles/fork-sidebar.css`, imported last in `src/renderer/src/styles.css`. No markup change.
- **Not done:** Codync's chevron + row count on folder headers (OpenBot keeps its folder icon); the drag preview of a row (the outlined row while dragging) is upstream's and unchanged.
- **Check:** glass on, a folder with rows: its header has no dark band; click it twice: rows fold and unfold smoothly, sections below glide. Switch chats and close a trace panel: no ghost of the last view stays. Scroll a long folder: its header scrolls away with it (glass only).

## 19. Own iPhone build (EAS → TestFlight), next to the App Store app

- **Get:** an "OpenBot Fork" app on Elias's iPhone with the fork's mobile changes (e.g. entry 18's "% used"), installed from his own TestFlight. The App Store OpenBot stays as it is.
- **Why:** Elias, 4 Nov 2026: fork changes to `apps/mobile` never reach the App Store app (upstream's signed build); he chose EAS + TestFlight over Xcode-over-USB.
- **How (portable):** `app.json` stays upstream's; `app.config.js` passes it through `fork-ios.js`, which renames every upstream iOS id: bundle `com.eliasx45.openbot` (+ `.ShareExtension`, `.ExpoWidgetsTarget`, `.NotificationService`), App Group `group.com.eliasx45.openbot`, Apple team `GQQBT37P3D`, name "OpenBot Fork", no `applinks:openbot.run` (Elias's team cannot claim that domain). Owner and EAS project come from `apps/mobile/fork-ios.json` (`{owner, projectId}`, no secrets); without it OTA updates are off, so the fork never loads upstream's JavaScript. The two TS files that named the App Group read it from `extra.forkAppGroup` (`src/shared/lib/app-group.ts`).
- **Here:** `apps/mobile/fork-ios.js`, `apps/mobile/app.config.js`, `apps/mobile/eas.json` (`submit.production.ios`, fork-only values), `apps/mobile/src/shared/lib/app-group.ts`, `push-notifications-native.ios.ts`, `share/model/share-extension.ts`.
- **Limits:** push notifications do not arrive (the account service sends with upstream's APNs key for upstream's bundle id); links from outside the app (`openbot://`, QR codes scanned with the Camera app) may open the App Store app instead: use the in-app scanner; the "update available" card points to the App Store; a TestFlight build lasts 90 days.
- **Steps:** (1) done 4 Nov 2026: Expo account `elias-1`, CLI logged in. (2) done: project `@elias-1/openbot` (`0566f7c1-01c9-41e6-b0c2-3303d924422c`) in `fork-ios.json`; `eas init` cannot write to a dynamic config, so `fork-ios.json` with only `owner` lets it run once, then the printed id goes in. (3) done: App Store Connect API key "EAS" (Admin, Key ID `Z6HB82959X`, issuer `23d99afe-f709-4772-8975-f0cd556326ae`), file `~/.appstoreconnect/private_keys/AuthKey_Z6HB82959X.p8`; no Apple password is typed anywhere (Elias's choice). (4) done: the 4 bundle ids + App Groups/Push through the API; on developer.apple.com (signed-in Chrome, cua) the App Group `group.com.eliasx45.openbot`, its link to the 4 ids and Communication Notifications on the app id: Apple's public API cannot create an App Group, link one, or set `USERNOTIFICATIONS_COMMUNICATION`, so EAS's capability sync fails with the key and must be off (`EXPO_NO_CAPABILITY_SYNC=1`). (5) done: EAS made the distribution certificate and the 4 profiles (stored on Expo); push setup answered No (a push key needs an Apple ID sign-in, and pushes cannot reach the fork anyway); first cloud build `6da79cf0-6e91-4d78-9f73-0e5b8ebb1c47` (build 10) queued 4 Nov 2026. (6) done: Elias accepted the updated License Agreement; app record "OpenBot Fork" (Apple id `6830840733`, SKU `openbot-fork`) made on App Store Connect (the API cannot create apps). (7) `apps/mobile/eas.json` › `submit.production.ios`: `ascAppId` plus the local key path, Key ID and issuer, so `eas submit` neither asks for an Apple ID to find the app nor makes a new Admin key stored on Expo (it asked both). Submission `1d6e4ea7-777e-40e2-9dae-d4abbc7162d4` scheduled 4 Nov 2026. The upload runs on Expo's servers, so the CLI sends them the key for that job (not checked whether Expo keeps it; to be safe, revoke key "EAS" on App Store Connect when TestFlight work stops). (8) TestFlight on the phone.
- **Build command (this Mac):** `EXPO_NO_CAPABILITY_SYNC=1 NODE_OPTIONS="--network-family-autoselection-attempt-timeout=3000 --dns-result-order=ipv4first" EXPO_ASC_API_KEY_PATH=… EXPO_ASC_KEY_ID=Z6HB82959X EXPO_ASC_ISSUER_ID=… EXPO_APPLE_TEAM_ID=GQQBT37P3D EXPO_APPLE_TEAM_TYPE=INDIVIDUAL npx -y eas-cli@27.4.1 build -p ios --profile production`. Why each part: Node's default 250 ms connect attempt fails to reach `api.appstoreconnect.apple.com` from this network while `curl` works, and EAS hung 9 min then failed "register bundle identifier"; bun's 7-day minimum release age blocks a new `eas-cli`, so `npx`; old cached eas-cli sent an invalid capability request. `--non-interactive` refuses to create a certificate, so the first run answers the Y/n questions (an `expect` script did it, 4 Nov 2026).
- **Check:** TestFlight shows "OpenBot Fork"; it signs in with the same email code and shows the plan-limit rings as "% used".

## 20. Chat videos play when the MP4 index is at the end

- **Get:** an MP4 in a chat (e.g. an agent's `H1-glitch-hit-before-after.mp4`) plays and seeks, also when it was exported without `-movflags +faststart`.
- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026: a video from Vegapunk stayed at 0:00 with no duration. Its `moov` index was after `mdat`, and the `openbot-attachment` protocol sent only the whole file from byte 0 (status 200, no `Accept-Ranges`), so Chromium could not jump to the index.
- **How (portable):** `fileResponse` takes the request's `Range` header. One `bytes=a-b`, `a-` or `-n` range answers 206 with `Content-Range` and only those bytes; a range past the end answers 416; no header, multi-range or a bad value send the whole file as before. Every answer has `Accept-Ranges: bytes`. Only the local attachment protocol passes the header (avatars and logos keep 200).
- **Here:** `src/main/file-response.ts`, `src/main/session-configuration.ts` (`openbot-attachment`), `src/main/renderer-permissions.ts`.
- **Not done:** `openbot-remote-attachment` (attachments from another server) still downloads the whole file and answers 200.
- **Full screen:** the video's full-screen button did nothing (Elias, 5 Nov 2026): the permission handler refused `fullscreen`. `renderer-permissions.ts` now allows `fullscreen` for the trusted renderer's main frame only; frames (visual replies) and other pages stay refused (`renderer-permissions.test.ts`).
- **Check:** open Tapa Lead's chat, press play on `H1-glitch-hit-before-after.mp4` (Update from Vegapunk, 11:16 AM): it shows 0:11 and plays; drag the bar to seek; the full-screen button fills the screen and Esc leaves it.

## 21. Listen to a Codex live voice in Settings

- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026: picking a Codex live voice (Spruce, Ember, Vale…) in Settings → Voice played nothing, so he could not hear the voices before a call.
- **How (portable):** picking a voice, or the new **Listen** button on the live-voice row, plays a sample. The renderer opens a WebRTC call with no microphone (`live-voice-preview.ts`); `voice.previewLive` starts Codex realtime v3 with that voice on upstream's live-voice relay (#5230, merged 6 Nov 2026: a separate toolless Codex process, not the agent's provider), with the Codex sign-in of the first Codex agent or, with none, of any agent (no chat, no startup context, no relay rule, nothing added to any chat). When the call connects, `voice.speakLive` sends the sample sentence (`settings.voice.speech.sample`) through Codex App Server's experimental `thread/realtime/appendSpeech`. The call ends when the voice's turn is done (or after 20 s), with `thread/realtime/stop`, and the relay process stops. A sample ends any other live session. It needs Codex live voice on, a Codex sign-in and one agent.
- **Here:** `packages/contracts/src/ipc-voice.ts` + `ipc-endpoints.ts`, `src/preload/index.ts`, `src/main/ipc/voice-inputs.ts` + `voice-handlers.ts`, `src/backend/agent/live-voice.ts` (`preview`, `speak`, `#open`, `LiveSession.preview`), `src/backend/agent-service.ts`, `src/renderer/src/features/settings/live-voice-preview.ts` + `SettingsVoiceTab.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/preview/mock-voice.ts`.
- **Check:** Settings → Voice → Live voice: pick Vale. Within ~3 s you hear "Hello. This is how your replies will sound." in that voice; the button shows Stop while it plays and Listen after. Pick Ember: the voice changes. Listen replays; Stop cuts it. No agent chat gets a new message.

## 22. CI on the fork: knip, translations, context budget, no Discord post or Cloudflare deploy

- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026: CI failed on the fork's `main` after the push. `knip` found 9 exports in `fork-*.ts` that nothing outside their file uses. The upstream "Announce mobile release on Discord" workflow also ran, and it failed because the fork has no `DISCORD_MOBILE_RELEASE_*` secrets.
- **How (portable):** fork modules export only what other files import. An upstream workflow that needs upstream secrets runs only when `github.repository == 'nightly-labs/openbot'` (the pattern of `release-regression.yml` and `provider-cli-canary.yml`).
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-agent-skills.ts`, `fork-cache-compact.ts`, `fork-composio.ts`, `fork-goals.ts`, `fork-memory.ts`; `.github/workflows/mobile-release-announcement.yml` (`detect` job; `announce` needs it, so it skips too; upstream #5306, merged 6 Nov 2026, renamed the job "Find the posts that are due" and added a schedule, and the fork condition stays on it).
- **Cloudflare:** CI's `deploy-production` job (upstream's account Worker; needs the private home-page-beam repository and production secrets) has the same `github.repository == 'nightly-labs/openbot'` condition. "All required checks pass" does not need it, so a skip is not a failure.
- **Translations:** the fork's own text is English only. Every key that a locale does not translate is listed for that locale in `packages/i18n/untranslated.json` (the screen shows English), as `docs/i18n.md` says. A new fork key needs the same: run `bun run i18n:check`, then add each "is not translated" key there (or translate it). `status.agent.goalTurnNoStep` ends with "." so that its English no longer also matches `status.agent.goalTurn`.
- **Context budget:** fork tools and instructions (goals, Composio, memory, skills) add text to every agent. `tools/agent-context/budget.json` holds the fork's counts; after a fork change that adds agent text, set the new counts from `bun run check:agent-context`.
- **Check:** the next push to `main`: the CI "Check" job passes (unused files, translation catalogs, agent context budget), and the Discord workflow shows its jobs as skipped.

A pick puts a folder chip in the draft; the message stores `@[Cyber](section:<sectionId>)` (new tag kind `section`, strict name rule like tab/thread). The message shows a folder chip (no click action). When the prompt is built, the host reads the section from its own sidebar layout and the provider gets `"Cyber" (sidebar section sectionId <id>; agents: Shelf <agentId>, …)` (up to 20, `, N more`), `; no agents yet`, or `(deleted sidebar section)`; a repeat in the same message is the name only. A teammate's tag and an older app read the name. A deleted folder's chip in a draft turns into the outline of an unavailable tag.

The message shows a Cyber folder chip; the agent's prompt has `"Cyber" (sidebar section sectionId …; …)` and it can create/assign agents there without asking which folder. Rename the folder, reopen the draft with a tag: the agent gets the new name. Delete a folder that a draft tags: the chip turns grey.

## 24. ⇧⌘T opens a closed thread tab again

- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026: he closed a side-thread tab by accident and wanted "a Command-Shift-T, just like in the browser".
- **How (portable):** each close of a thread tab (×, Close others, Close to the right, side panel ×) puts the thread on a per-window stack (last 25, this session only). ⇧⌘T (Ctrl+Shift+T on Windows/Linux) in an agent's chat takes the newest closed tab of that agent and opens it again where it was: chat tab (it becomes the open tab) or side panel tab. Archived, deleted or already-open threads are skipped. Archive and Delete do not go on the stack. A key press that another handler took (`defaultPrevented`) is left alone, so the full browser view keeps its own ⇧⌘T (it listens in capture phase and stops the event).
- **Here:** `rememberClosed`, `reopenClosed`, `isReopenTabShortcut` and the `keydown` listener in `src/renderer/src/features/agent-threads/agent-thread-tabs.ts`; `close` / `closeSide` record, the internal `closeTab` / `closeSideTab` (used by archive and delete) do not.
- **Check:** in Bot Bot's chat open two thread tabs, close one with ×: ⇧⌘T opens it again as the open tab. Close two, press ⇧⌘T twice: both come back, newest first. Move one to the side panel, close it there: ⇧⌘T puts it back in the side panel. Archive a thread: ⇧⌘T does not bring it back. Open the full browser view, close a browser tab: ⇧⌘T reopens the browser tab, not a thread.

## 25. The chat's scroll rail keeps its glow at both ends

- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026 (screenshot): at the bottom of a chat the reading head's dot and glow on the right-edge scroll rail were cut off flat ("cutoff of the glow here too"). The same cut hit the top end (head at the start of the first section).
- **How (portable):** a scrolling box (`overflow-y: auto`) clips at its padding box, and the head and the active bar glow up to ~15px past the list's ends (and a blurred shadow fades a little further). The track reaches out further than that with a negative block margin and pads it back (`margin-block: -24px; padding-block: 24px`), so the glow fits inside the clip while the list, the bars and the layout around them stay put. The code that scrolls a long list to keep the current section in view subtracts that padding, so a section is not tucked under it.
- **Here:** `.chat-scroll-rail-track` (`--chat-scroll-rail-bleed`) in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/styles/scroll-rail.css`; the keep-in-view effect in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/ChatScrollRail.tsx`. The public site's reading rail (`apps/auth-api/src/styles.css`, `.reading-progress-list`) does not scroll, so it never clipped.
- **Not done:** the glow's right side still ends at the transcript scroller's own edge (the rail sits in its side padding).
- **Check:** open a chat long enough for the rail, scroll to the very bottom: the dot at the end of the last bar shows its full round glow below it. Scroll to the very top: the dot at the start of the first bar shows its glow above it. In a chat with more sections than the rail holds, step through sections: the current one stays fully in view.

## 28. Hover follows the pointer: no dead gaps, no moving targets

- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026 (two screen recordings): "a lot of racing condition here on hoverover": the "show steps" chevron after the live status line jumped away from the pointer each time the status text changed length; the hover fill of sidebar rows, the pinned group tiles, the header thread tabs and the left rail tooltips stayed on the item the pointer had left.
- **How (portable):** (1) a control that sits after changing text gets a hit area that does not move: the toggle's `::after` covers the whole status line (`inset: 0` on the positioned line), so the pointer stays on it while the chevron moves. (2) Items in a list with gaps take the gap as hit area: a transparent `::after` reaches into the gap at `z-index: -1` inside the item's stacking context (a transform, a z-index or `isolation: isolate`), so it is under the item's own parts and only the gap gets it. The pointer goes from one item straight to the next: no frame with no hover, no arrow cursor, no closing tooltip. (3) Gaps inside a `-webkit-app-region: drag` header get no mouse events at all (the window manager takes them), so the tab row is `no-drag`. (4) A rail tooltip's old label starts to fade when the pointer leaves its button, not when the next panel is placed some frames later; the handoff fades on from the current opacity.
- **Root causes found:** hover is plain CSS `:hover` with `--openbot-duration-hover: 0ms`; Cuelume only plays sounds and does not drive hover. The recordings show 2–3 captured frames (~70–110 ms) from the pointer entering an item to its fill, at ~100% CPU: that part is the main thread being slow to draw the next frame, not app logic. The gaps, the drag area and the moving chevron made it visible as flicker.
- **Here:** `.agent-steps-toggle::after` in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/styles/agent-steps.css`; `.agent-threads-header` (`no-drag`) and `.agent-threads-tab::after` in `styles/agent-threads.css`; `.window-rail-button::after` in `packages/ui/src/features/window/page-rail.css`; `.sidebar-agent-item`, `.sidebar-person-item` and `.sidebar-pinned-item` `::after` in `src/renderer/src/styles/app-shell.css`; `holdInPlace` / `handOff` in `packages/ui/src/menu-motion.tsx`.
- **Not done:** the side panel's tab row (its tab list fills the header, so `no-drag` would stop the window drag there).
- **Check:** while an agent works with steps, keep the pointer still on the chevron through several status changes: the chevron keeps its hover fill, one click opens the steps. Move the pointer slowly down the sidebar rows and across the pinned tiles: the fill goes from one row to the next with no frame of no fill and no arrow cursor. Move across the header thread tabs and up out of them into the header: the hover goes off at once; the empty header still drags the window. Move down the left rail: the tooltip label of the left button fades at once and the panel moves to the next.

## 29. The pointer no longer flips between arrow and hand

- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026 (recordings): with the pointer still on a button or link, it flipped between the arrow and the hand every few frames while the hover fill stayed on. "u and me are fighting for the cursor."
- **Cause:** the Computer Use rim overlay (one transparent, floating, click-through window per display, covering the whole desktop, on screen whenever built) was click-through with `forward: true`. Forwarded moves reached its page, which is `pointer-events: none` with nothing under the pointer, so it set the arrow on every move while the window below set its own cursor.
- **How (portable):** a click-through overlay that never reacts to the pointer uses `setIgnoreMouseEvents(true)` without `forward`; forwarding is only for a surface that must notice hover.
- **Here:** `createComputerUseHighlightWindow` in `src/main/main-window.ts`.
- **Not done:** the Dynamic Island windows keep `forward: true` (they need moves to open on hover). On a display without a notch the collapsed island window (58pt tall) overlaps the top of the chat header, so the top half of the thread tabs under it may still flip.
- **Check:** with OpenBot running (rim overlays built), rest the pointer on the "show steps" chevron while an agent works, on a sidebar row, on a link: the hand stays, no flicker to the arrow. Computer Use still draws the rim and agent cursor and nothing on the overlay takes a click.

## 30. The selection toolbar opens over file chips and code, and takes the keyboard

- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026 (two recordings): the "Describe edits · Quote · Explain · Improve" toolbar did not open for a selection over a file chip (`ALG3 TD1.pdf`) or a line with code spans (`Shared`, `:1230`), and when it opened he had to click in "Describe edits" before he could type.
- **Cause:** `messageTextSelection` refused any selection that touched `a, button, [role="button"], .message-citation`. A file chip (`LocalFileLink`) is a `<button>`, so every selection over a chip failed. Chromium (tested headless with the real message CSS) selected plain `<code>` spans with no problem. The code-span cases in the recording are not explained by this check (unconfirmed).
- **How (portable):** refuse only selections that touch form fields (`input, textarea, select, [contenteditable="true"]`). Links, chips, code and citations are message text. The chip's badge is `user-select: none`, so `Selection.toString()` gives the chip's label only. A chip click that ends a text selection does not open the file. A selection made with the pointer focuses the instruction field (`preventScroll`). The selection keeps its cloned range and the CSS highlight, so typing does not change what the edit refers to. A keyboard selection keeps the focus, so Shift and the arrow keys still extend it.
- **Here:** `EDITABLE_SELECTOR`, `captureSelection(fromPointer)` and `focusInstruction` in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/SelectionActions.tsx`; `selectionTouches` in `LocalFileLink`, `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/MarkdownMessageText.tsx`.
- **Check:** in an agent message, drag over an inline code span, over a PDF chip, and across both: the toolbar opens with the caret in "Describe edits". Type at once: the text goes into the field and the highlight stays. Quote puts exactly the selected text (chip label, no "PDF" badge) in the composer. Esc closes the toolbar. A plain click on a chip opens the file with no toolbar. A plain caret click does not move the focus.

## 25. Agents download files to a path they choose

- **Get:** `openbot_browser.download({ tabId, url, saveTo?, overwrite?, timeoutMs? })`. The agent saves a file from a site, signed in as the tab, into a folder or file path it picks, and gets `{ path, size, status, mimeType }` back.
- **Why:** before, a file came only from a click in the page and always landed in `~/OpenBot/Downloads`; the agent had to move it and could not use the tab's sign-in from a shell.
- **How (portable):** start the URL as a download of the tab's own web contents (Electron `webContents.downloadURL`, so the session cookies apply), and match it in the session's one `will-download` handler by tab and first URL. Only a matched agent request may set the path; page downloads keep the old rule (Downloads, new name). An existing folder keeps the server's sanitized name and adds " (2)"; another path is the file itself, its folder must exist, and an existing file fails unless `overwrite`. Relative paths fail. A Workspace only agent may write only its sandbox roots plus Downloads. The same 2 GB / 4 active / no-secret-tab limits apply; a timeout (default 120 s) or a cancelled call cancels the download.
- **Here:** `src/backend/browser-downloads.ts` (path rules), `download` handler and `will-download` in `src/backend/browser-host.ts`, schema in `src/backend/browser-tools.ts`, roots in `src/backend/agent/openbot-tool-router.ts`, label `browser.action.download` (14 locales), guidance line in `src/backend/agent/tool-guidance.ts`, budget in `tools/agent-context/budget.json`.
- **Check:** `bun run test:browser -- --scenario=download-tool` (writes `.openbot-build/browser-download-tool.json`).

---

## 31. A file name in a code span is a chip only when the file exists

- **Get:** `` `notes.md` ``, `` `~/Downloads/a.pdf` `` or a shared file shows as a clickable file chip only when the click would open it. A name with nothing behind it (such as a Google Drive name `` `ALG3 TD1.pdf` ``) stays plain code: no chip, no "Nothing exists at …" error.
- **Why:** any code span with a known extension became a chip, and nothing checked the file, so clicks on Drive or web names failed.
- **How (portable):** the code span shows as code first, then asks main once, in a batch, which named files resolve, with the same resolver and options as the click (`resolveWorkspacePath`: Full access → anywhere, Workspace only → inside the workspace; bare names in the workspace, the message's folder, or the thread's file history; shared files through `resolveSharedFile`). Every refusal answers false, so a Workspace only agent cannot tell "outside" from "missing". Answers are cached per message; a streaming message asks nothing until it ends. A joined server or the browser client has no check: its names stay chips as before.
- **Here:** `agent:check-file-chips` (`checkFileChips` in `packages/contracts/src/ipc-endpoints.ts`, `parseCheckFileChips` in `src/main/ipc/agent-inputs.ts`, handler in `src/main/ipc/attachment-handlers.ts`, `AgentService.checkFileChips`, `workspacePathResolves` in `src/backend/workspace-paths.ts`), batching in `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/message-file-chips.ts`, `MentionedFileChip` in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/MarkdownMessageText.tsx`. Agent guidance line in `src/backend/agent/developer-instructions.ts` (budget raised by hand, +154 bytes, in `tools/agent-context/budget.json`); upstream #5312 (merged 6 Nov 2026) moved "full path for files outside your folder" into the attach line, so the fork line now says only "Put a file name in backticks only for a real local file; write names you mean as text without backticks."
- **Check:** an agent writes `` `ALG3 TD1.pdf` `` (missing), a real full path like `` `~/Downloads/<file>.pdf` ``, and the bare name of a file in its folder: the first is plain code, the other two are chips that open the file.
