## 32. Import browser sign-ins into browser profiles; an agent can use several profiles

- **Get:** Settings › Browser profiles › **Import from your browser**. Pick one or more profiles of Chrome, Brave, Arc, Edge, Chromium or Vivaldi on this Mac, and keep **All sites** or **Only sites I select**. After Touch ID, each one becomes a named browser profile with that browser's cookies, so agents are already signed in. A linked profile shows "From Google Chrome “…” · synced …" and a **Re-sync** button. In Agent settings › Advanced › **More profiles**, an agent gets extra profiles; the browser tool `open` takes `profile: "<name>"` and `status` lists `browserProfiles`, so the agent picks the account the task needs. On a login wall, the sign-in card has **Use Chrome's sign-in**: it copies that one site from the linked browser profile and reloads. A tab that lands on a login wall (a host such as `accounts.`/`login.`, a path such as `/login`, `/signin`) does this on its own, once per tab and site every 5 min, and reloads only when the browser had something new; there, and with the button, the browser's copy wins over a newer OpenBot change, which is the site signing OpenBot out (Elias, 22 Nov 2026: Google and Linear went stale after the one import of 6 Nov). A failed automatic read (Keychain, missing profile) stops the automatic copies until the next start, so no Keychain question pops up by itself.
- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026: import once for all bots, choose the profiles, several accounts per bot chosen by context, re-sync on a login wall and by hand, newest wins. Passwords go through the existing Vault › Import from CSV (Chrome's export is read as is); the sheet says so.
- **How (portable):** read the browser's `Cookies` SQLite (a temp copy, `node:sqlite` read-only with `readBigInts`) and decrypt `v10` values: Keychain "<Browser> Safe Storage" via `/usr/bin/security`, PBKDF2-SHA1 (`saltysalt`, 1003 rounds, 16 bytes), AES-128-CBC with 16 spaces as IV; at DB meta version ≥ 24 drop the first 32 bytes (SHA-256 of the host). Partitioned cookies are skipped. Write with Electron `session.cookies.set` into the profile's partition. Newest wins per cookie: a note `{chrome, local}` per cookie, `local` set from `cookies.on("changed")` (own writes and non-explicit removals ignored); a cookie newer in OpenBot is kept, one removed in Chrome is not removed here. Importing a browser profile that is already linked syncs that profile again (no "(2)" copy). A failed first sync deletes the new empty profile. Cookie values are never logged.
- **Here:** `src/main/chrome-import.ts` (service, `isSignInUrl`, `autoSignIn`; test `src/main/chrome-import.test.ts`; data in `<userData>/fork-chrome-import/links/<profileId>.json` and `agent-profiles.json`, no DB migration), wiring and Touch ID (`OPENBOT_FORK_SKIP_TOUCH_ID=1` skips it) in `src/main/application-services.ts`, handlers in `src/main/ipc/agent-admin-handlers.ts` (this computer only), contracts `packages/contracts/src/ipc-chrome-import.ts` + `agent:admin:*` endpoints, `profile` in `src/backend/browser-tools.ts` and `#agentTabSession` in `src/backend/browser-host.ts`, UI `packages/ui/src/features/browser/BrowserChromeImport.tsx`, `BrowserProfilesPanel.tsx`, "More profiles" in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/AgentSettingsPanel.tsx`, card button in `ConversationPrompts.tsx` / `ConversationBrowserTakeover.tsx`, keys `browser.chrome.*`, `agentSettings.browserProfile.more*`, `error.backend.chromeImport*`.
- **Sign-in follows the site (8 Nov 2026):** Elias: "Why is it that it's not logged in? … should be lgged in". Tatiana Lead opened Instagram without `profile`, so the tab was in its default profile; "Your Chrome" (an extra profile with Instagram's `sessionid`) was never used, and the card had no **Use Chrome's sign-in** because the tab's profile was not linked. Now: `open` without `profile` picks the agent's first extra profile that is linked and holds imported cookies of the URL's site (`BrowserHost` option `siteProfile` → `ChromeImportService.profileWithSite`, read from the link's cookie notes). A default that holds that site itself stays the default. On a login wall in a session that is not linked (the default, the agent's own or the shared one), the card button shows when one of the agent's profiles is linked and holds the site. It copies Chrome's cookies of that one site into the tab's session (`copySiteToTab`, no notes, `BrowserHost.tabCookies`) and reloads. Limit: a default that is a named profile with a manual sign-in of the same site but no Chrome link loses to the linked extra profile. Name `profile` to keep it.
- **Not done:** remote servers (Chrome runs on this Mac only). Passwords are not read from the browser. Not tested: the Touch ID prompt itself, and the first Keychain prompt of Brave/Arc/Edge.
- **Check:** `bun run dev`, then `bun scripts/fork-chrome-import-e2e.ts [--chat]` (writes `.openbot-build/fork-chrome-import-e2e.json`): lists profiles, imports google.com of two Chrome profiles, a tab reaches myaccount.google.com signed in, re-import reuses the profile, re-sync keeps newer cookies, and with `--chat` a Codex agent opens the profile named in the message and reports its account.

## 33. Drop files on a sidebar agent to attach them in its chat

- **Get:** drag a file, a screenshot (Finder, QuickShotX) or an image from the open chat onto another agent's row, a channel row or a pinned tile ("Editors", "Bot Bot"). The row lights up while the files are over it. On drop the files go into that chat's message box as a draft (not sent), the chat opens as on a click, and the cursor is in its message box. Several files work. On the open chat's own row, the drop is the same as a drop on its message box. Section and folder headers take no files.
- **Why:** to hand a screenshot to another agent, Elias had to open that chat first and drag again.
- **How (portable):** the drop keeps the one import path of the message box. The row only says "I take files" (a data attribute) and names its chat. The preload already imports files dropped on the conversation; it now also takes a drop on a marked row. Imports start in the same task as the drop, and the open conversation gives each one a target: normally the open chat, now the chat of the row when one was named just before. The chat opens in the next task, so the conversation that takes the imports is still there; the upload chips and drafts live in the shared controller, so the next view shows them. A sidebar reorder carries no files and is not an external drag, so the drag engine keeps it.
- **Here:** `packages/ui/src/features/sidebar/stores/file-drop-store.ts` (`createFileDropTarget`), used in `SidebarAgentRow.tsx`, `SidebarChannelRow.tsx`, `SidebarPinnedGroup.tsx`; `onDropFiles` in `sidebar-types.ts`; `dropFilesOnChat` in `src/renderer/src/features/sidebar/WorkspaceSidebar.tsx`; `resources.sidebarDropTarget` in `conversation-controller.ts`, read on `started` in `conversation-scope.ts`; `isConversationDropTarget` in `src/preload/index.ts`; `.agent-row-file-drop-target` in `src/renderer/src/styles/app-shell.css`. Desktop only: the browser client passes no `onDropFiles`.
- **Limits:** with no chat open (settings page, routines), an import that starts has no conversation to take it. A drop from Finder does not bring the OpenBot window to the front.
- **Check:** in chat A, drag a Finder PNG onto agent B's row: the row lights up; on drop B opens with the file chip in its message box and the cursor there. Do it again with an image from A's chat, with three files, and on A's own row. Drag a sidebar row to reorder it: it still moves.

## 34. Hovering a PDF file chip shows its page

- **Get:** hover a PDF chip in a chat message (a code span that resolved, entry 31) and, after 300 ms, an opaque frosted peek (320 px wide) shows page 1 and "Page 1 of N" under it. `` `notes.pdf#page=3` `` is a chip too and its peek shows page 3 (the last page when the file is shorter). Keyboard focus on the chip opens it too. It closes 250 ms after the pointer or focus leaves; a click closes it and opens the file as before. A name with no file stays plain code with no peek.
- **Why:** to see which document a chip names without opening it. Feel copied from Ghostex Learn's PDF cite peek (`learn_cite.rs`: hover delay, hide delay, frosted bubble).
- **How (portable):** the shared chip takes an optional `previewFileChip(file, page, signal)` reader; absent (joined servers, browser client) means no peek. The reader asks the existing file preview (`previewWorkspaceFile` with `exactPath`, or `previewSharedFile`, at most 32 MB), releases any preview page it got, and draws the page with pdf.js (the renderer the mobile app already uses for PDFs) into a canvas, kept as a `data:` WebP (the renderer CSP allows `data:` images, not `blob:`). The page is read only when the peek first opens; a close before the answer aborts the drawing and forgets the entry, so the next hover reads again. Answers are cached per message, chip path and page (LRU, 32 MB of pictures) while the chat is open; a file that does not draw gets no peek again. pdf.js loads lazily, with its worker as a Vite asset.
- **Here:** `FilePagePeek` (Kobalte `Tooltip`, new) in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/FilePagePeek.tsx`; `fileChipPage` and the peek in `MentionedFileChip` in `MarkdownMessageText.tsx` (its `onOpen` passes upstream's `asWritten` from #5312, merged 6 Nov 2026, so a chip that joins the wrong folder still opens the file as the agent wrote it); prop pass-through in `MessageRendering.tsx` and `ChatMessageRow.tsx`; `.message-file-peek*` in `styles/message-content.css`; reader in `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/message-pdf-pages.ts`, wired for the local server in `ConversationTimeline.tsx`; key `chat.file.pagePeek` in all 14 catalogs; `pdfjs-dist` (catalog, already used by mobile) added to the root `package.json`. No new IPC channel.
- **Check:** in a local chat, post `` `/full/path/a.pdf` ``, `` `/full/path/a.pdf#page=2` ``, the bare name of a PDF in the agent's workspace, and `` `Made Up.pdf` ``. Hover each: the first three show page 1, page 2 and page 1 with "Page x of N"; the last is plain code with no peek. A hover shorter than 300 ms shows nothing.

## 35. Selection notes: "Describe edits" waits in the composer, then all notes go in one message

- **Get:** select text in a message (or a file in the side panel), type in "Describe edits", press Enter or the arrow. Nothing is sent: the toolbar closes, the selection clears, and a card above the message box shows "1 comment" with the start of the quote and the note. Do it on more passages: the notes pile up ("3 comments"). × removes a note; a click on a note edits it (Enter or a click away saves, Esc cancels). Type more text if you want and press the composer's Send: one message goes to the agent with every note in order (quote, then note), then the typed text. The notes are per chat, stay when you switch chats and after a restart, and clear after the send. ⌘Enter (Ctrl+Enter off macOS) in "Describe edits" still sends at once, as before. Enter (or ⇧Enter) with "Describe edits" empty quotes the selection, exactly as Quote does: the quote chip goes into the message box, where you write about it, and it is sent with that message (alone, too). The Enter that ends an input method composition does not quote; an empty ⌘Enter does nothing, as before.
- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026 (recordings `rec.mp4`, `rec-2.mp4` = Claude desktop): one message per note made the agent answer each passage alone; Claude's desktop app collects notes first.
- **How (portable):** the draft of each chat gets a list of notes `{id, quoteId, quote, note}` beside its text. The toolbar adds to the list in place of sending. At send, each note becomes the quote marker that Quote already puts in the composer (`@[text](quote:<message id or file quote id>)`), a blank line and the note; then the typed text. No new wire format: the provider already expands each quote marker to a Markdown block quote (a file quote gets its "From `path`, lines …" heading), and the user's bubble shows each quote as a chip. The send clears only the notes it sent. A note does not wait while the composer edits a queued message (that edit keeps only its text) or when the message would pass the length limit: then it sends at once as before. Explain, Improve, Shorten, Tone and Grammar still send at once (same path as before), and Quote still puts a quote chip in the text.
- **Here:** `SelectionNote` and `serializeSelectionNotes` in `packages/contracts/src/selection-instruction.ts`; `onQueueNote`, `addNote`, `sendsNow` in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/SelectionActions.tsx`; the card `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/ComposerSelectionNotes.tsx` (CSS `.composer-selection-notes` in `styles/composer.css`); `selectionNotes` on `ComposerDraft` (`conversation-types.ts`); `queueSelectionNote`, `removeSelectionNote`, `editSelectionNote` and the serialization in `submitMessage` (`stores/composer-actions.ts`); `clearSubmittedDraft` and `composerHasContent` in `stores/composer-store.ts`; storage in `composer-draft-storage.ts`; i18n `chat.selection.addNote`, `chat.selection.sendNow*`, `composer.notes.*` (all locales). Desktop only: mobile keeps its own immediate send. Empty Enter → Quote (Elias, 23 Nov 2026: "Enter without typing anything … the agent will just understand that I quoted"; before, `addNote` returned on an empty field and nothing happened): `addNote` calls `onQuote`, `compositionEnter` skips the IME Enter; test `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/SelectionEmptyEnterQuote.test.tsx` (5 of its 10 cases fail before).
- **Limits:** a side thread's message box has no notes (the toolbar adds to the agent's own chat). Edit on a failed send puts its notes back as cards; after a restart, the text of a failed send comes back as plain text (quote chips and notes), not as cards.
- **Check:** add 3 notes on different passages; remove one; switch chats and back (2 remain); type extra text and Send: the agent gets quote 1, note 1, quote 2, note 2, the text, in that order. ⌘Enter in "Describe edits" sends at once. Quote / Explain / Improve behave as before.

## 36. "From iPhone" in the + menu: a photo from the iPhone joins the message

- **Get:** in any chat of this Mac's own server, + › **From iPhone** opens macOS Continuity Camera's menu at the pointer (Take Photo, Scan Documents, Add Sketch on the nearby iPhone). The photo joins the draft as an image, the same way as a pasted image, so the agent reads it. Not on a joined server or the web.
- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026: the photo of paper work from the Ghostex Learn harness ("📷 From iPhone"), for every agent (Owl, his study tutor, first).
- **How (portable):** Electron's window cannot be the Services requestor, so a small helper app (`OpenBot iPhone Photo.app`, Swift, LSUIElement) does it with the recipe proven in Ghostex (`GpuiContinuityCamera.m`): a 1×1 clear key window at the pointer whose view is first responder and answers `validRequestorForSendType:returnType:` for `NSImage.imageTypes`; an EMPTY `NSMenu` popped with `popUpContextMenu:withEvent:forView:` (AppKit adds the iPhone items itself); `readSelectionFromPasteboard:` writes a JPEG (0.85). It prints `{"outcome":"picked|dismissed|noDevice|timeout|failed"}`: an empty menu that closes in < 0.15 s means no iPhone; after a choice it waits up to 120 s for the photo. Main reads the file, deletes it, and returns the bytes; the renderer adds them with `pasteFiles`. A toast says "Take the photo on your iPhone…" and, on failure, what to check.
- **Here:** `native/macos/iphone-photo/main.swift`, built by `bun run prepare:iphone-photo` (`scripts/prepare-iphone-photo.ts`) into `.openbot-build/iphone-photo/`, copied to `Resources/iphone-photo` (`electron-builder.yml`; also in `package`, `scripts/fork/app.sh` and the release workflow). `src/main/iphone-photo.ts`, `takeIphonePhoto` (`agent:admin:take-iphone-photo`) in `packages/contracts/src/ipc-endpoints.ts` + `ipc-iphone-photo.ts`, handler in `src/main/ipc/agent-admin-handlers.ts`, preload, mock. UI: `onIphonePhoto` in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/ComposerAddMenu.tsx`, `takeIphonePhoto` in `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/ConversationComposer.tsx`. Keys `composer.add.iphonePhoto`, `composer.iphonePhoto.*`.
- **Trap:** the helper's window is borderless, and a borderless `NSWindow` never becomes key unless a subclass overrides `canBecomeKey`. Without it the menu lists the iPhone but every item is grey (seen 5 Nov). The helper also waits until it is active and its window is key before it opens the menu, and logs `active/key` to stderr (the dev log).
- **Every message box (5 Nov, Elias: "i want threads to have the button too"):** the main chat, a thread tab, the side thread and an open task on the Tasks page (`onIphonePhoto` on `AgentThreadView`; one renderer helper, `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/iphone-photo.ts`). The Tasks page's start box has no + menu, so no button there.
- **Focus:** when the helper ends, main calls `app.focus({ steal: true })`, so OpenBot's window takes the keyboard again (not for a helper a newer click replaced).
- **Log (8 Nov 2026, Elias: "Yes, add the log"):** each menu writes one line to `<userData>/logs/iphone-photo.log` (`~/Library/Application Support/OpenBot/logs/`): time, seconds, the helper's outcome, its exit, and its stderr (`active=… key=… firstResponder=…`), plus a line when a picked photo file is empty. In the packaged app the helper's stderr goes nowhere else, so this is the only trace of a failure. Rolls over to `.1` at 256 KB; no photo data. In `src/main/iphone-photo.ts` (`logRun`).
- **Check:** `OPENBOT_FORK_IPHONE_PHOTO_TEST_IMAGE=<jpg> bun run dev`, then `bun scripts/fork-iphone-photo-e2e.ts` (`.openbot-build/fork-iphone-photo-e2e.json`): the helper's outcomes, the main call, and + › From iPhone puts the photo in the main chat's draft and in a new thread's draft. Live: iPhone near, unlocked, same Apple Account, Wi-Fi and Bluetooth on; + › From iPhone › Take Photo, shoot, Use Photo: the photo shows in the draft (passed 5 Nov 2026, Elias's iPhone).

## 37. A request that waited while you were at the Mac goes to the phone when the Mac is idle

- **Get:** an agent question, an approval or a browser step that came while Elias was at the Mac (so the phone stayed quiet) goes to the phone after the Mac has had no input for 2 minutes, if nobody answered it. Answered on the Mac first, or the phone app on screen: nothing goes out. Replies and failed turns are unchanged.
- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026: Tapa Lead asked a question at 9:18:57 PM while he was at the Mac; he left at ~9:24; the phone got later replies but never the question, still unanswered at 9:29. Upstream drops a request that is skipped because the owner is at the computer.
- **How (portable):** in `PushNotificationService.#deliver`, a request (`heldRequest`: `prompt`, `approval`, `browser-takeover-requested`) skipped by `atHostComputer` or recent use elsewhere goes into `Registration.waiting` (one per request, at most 20). One 15 s interval runs only while something waits: when the Mac idle time (upstream's `createComputerIdle` in `src/main/computer-idle.ts`, #5315 merged 6 Nov 2026: `powerMonitor.getSystemIdleTime` on macOS, OpenBot window input on Wayland) and the use elsewhere are both ≥ `WAITING_REQUEST_IDLE_MS` (2 min), the request leaves the list and, if the runtime snapshot still lists it (or the snapshot is incomplete), `#deliver(event, registration)` runs again, which checks screen, mute, level, hidden agent and Live Activity as usual. `#releaseAnswered` (`agent-input-resolved`, also for approvals, and `browser-takeover-resolved`) and `#dropHeld` (phone on screen, removed) clear it. Dev: `OPENBOT_DEV_AWAY_SECONDS` also sets the idle time.
- **Here:** `src/main/push-notifications.ts` (`WAITING_REQUEST_IDLE_MS`, `#keepWaiting`, `#sendWaiting`, `requestStillWaits`), options `hostIdleSeconds` / `idleRequestAfterMs` in `src/main/host-service.ts`, set in `src/main/application-services.ts`. Doc: `docs/architecture/channels-and-messaging.md`.
- **Check:** iPhone app registered and in the background. At the Mac, make an agent ask a question (ask_user); keep using the Mac 10 s, then leave it untouched 2+ min: the phone gets the question once. Again, but answer on the Mac before 2 min: nothing on the phone.

## 38. Agents show their reasoning while they work

- **Get:** Claude agents stream a summary of their thinking, so a long run of tool calls with no text still shows what the agent is doing.
- **Why:** Elias, 5 Nov 2026: "are agents working/moving or stuck? feels like bugs". Vegapunk's row showed "Now the R4 gaps…" from 22:35 while it worked until 23:00. 697 of 716 thinking blocks in 2 h were empty. In SDK mode the CLI asks the API to omit thinking. OpenBot already renders `thinking_delta`.
- **How (portable):** `ClaudeClient` adds the CLI flag `--thinking-display summarized` to `extraArgs`. Tested 5 Nov 2026 (CLI `-p`, stream-json): with the flag, the thinking block has text; with only `showThinkingSummaries: true` in the settings, or no setting, it is empty. Running agents get it only after OpenBot restarts their query.
- **Here:** `src/backend/claude-client.ts` (`extraArgs`).
- **Check:** send an agent a task that needs several tool calls. Thinking text shows in its chat while it works, and new `thinking` blocks in its transcript `.jsonl` have text.

## 39. "Goal needs you" reaches the phone

- **Get:** when a `/goal` stops for Elias's answer (`needs-input`) while he is at the Mac, and he leaves without answering, the phone gets the agent's reply (the question) once the Mac has had no input for 2 minutes. A message to the agent before then starts the goal again, and nothing goes out.
- **Why:** Elias, 6 Nov 2026: "Today since yesterday, it didn't update me." Bot Bot's Handy goal reported needs-input at 23:10 with two questions in its reply. He was at the Mac, so the reply push was skipped, and feature 37 sends later only question cards, approvals and browser steps. Before this, "goal needs you" was only a Mac banner (`forkNotify`).
- **How (portable):** `PushNotificationService.#keepWaiting` also keeps a completed turn in the own chat of an agent with an active goal (`#goalReply`, one per agent, the newest replaces it). At send time `requestStillWaits` → `goalWaitsForUser`: send only while that goal is `paused` (it waits for the user). It reads the goal through `forkHub.goals`.
- **Here:** `src/main/push-notifications.ts` (`#goalReply`, `GOAL_REPLY`, `goalWaitsForUser`).
- **Check:** iPhone registered, app in the background. At the Mac, `/goal` an agent with a task that ends in a question (e.g. "pick a name for X: ask me"). When "Goal needs you" shows, leave the Mac untouched 2+ min: the phone gets the reply once. Again, but answer within 2 min: nothing on the phone.

## 40. Goals build every option, then ask with a question card

- **Get:** a goal agent that hits a choice for Elias does not stop. It builds each option (at most 3) side by side (code: a branch each; edits: a copy each, the original untouched), finishes the rest of the goal, then asks once with a question card (`ask_user` / `ask_ui`) that says what each option is, where it is, how to try it, and its pick. It asks first only for real-data changes, publishing, spending or what cannot be undone.
- **Why:** Elias, 6 Nov 2026: "isn't there a way where I can let them continue working and do the options/alternatives, all of them … and put them in PR and wait for me?" The Handy goal stopped overnight on two questions in plain text.
- **How (portable):** two lines in the goal prompt (`goalPrompt`). A question card also reaches the phone after 2 idle minutes (feature 37), where text in a reply did not.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-goals.ts` (`goalPrompt`).
- **Check:** `/goal` an agent with a task that has a real choice (e.g. "make 3 versions of the X card, I pick one"). It builds all of them without stopping, then shows one question card listing them.

## 41. A goal is done only when its result is shown

- **Get:** a `/goal` agent can report `completed` only with `proof`: the paths of the checked result (render, clip, screenshots, files), or `none: <why>`. The goal prompt says "done means shown": for a video or an animation, render, check frames and timing, fix, check again (at most 3 rounds), then show it in the update. A clip up to 2 minutes goes in whole; a longer one as a highlights reel of what changed (3–5 s per part, with audio, timecode labels) plus the full render's path.
- **Why:** Elias, 6 Nov 2026, picked "a done goal must include a render" ("doesn't have to show me the entire rendered thing in the chat if it's more than 2 min"). His Vision note's Self-Refine pass (27 Oct): nothing reaches him before the agent has looked at its own frames.
- **How (portable):** `proof` in `goalStatusToolSchema`; `handleTool` refuses `completed` without it; one line in `goalPrompt`.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-goals.ts`.
- **Check:** `/goal` an agent with "render a 5 s test clip of anything and finish". Its last update shows the clip, and `goal_status` was called with `proof`. Calling `completed` without `proof` returns the "completed needs proof" error.

## 42. Agents continue after a restart

- **Get:** when OpenBot restarts (quit, crash, Dock rebuild) while an agent works in its own chat, the agent continues by itself, in the same provider session, so it knows what it did. For about 10 s after the start, the open-waits block above the composer shows "🔄 Resumes after the OpenBot restart in a few seconds. Cancel keeps it stopped." with Cancel. Then the chat shows the routine-style line "🔄 Resumed after restart" and the agent gets one wake: "[OpenBot: 🔄 restart] Your last turn was interrupted at 15:12 on 6 Nov (Europe/Berlin) by an OpenBot restart — this is not a message from the user. … check what already happened … Never repeat sends, posts, payments … Then continue your work. The request it continues: …". A Claude agent also gets the background subagents that the restart stopped, by agent ID, and is told to continue each with `SendMessage` instead of launching it again. Stop works on the wake turn as on any turn. `/autoresume off` turns it off for every agent (`/autoresume on`, `/autoresume` shows the setting).
- **Why:** Elias, 6 Nov 2026: restarts (rebuilds of the fork, crashes) left every working agent "interrupted", and he had to write "continue" in each chat, as Claude Code desktop does by itself ("You were interrupted after the app restarted, continue your work"). Before: `recoverPersistedTurns` marked the turn interrupted (`turn.interrupted-by-restart`), expired open questions and marked background subagents stopped; nothing ran again. A background subagent dies with the CLI (exit 137), but its transcript stays (`<config>/projects/<cwd>/<session>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl` + `.meta.json`), and after `--resume` the parent's `SendMessage` to that agent ID continues it with its context (Elias checked it with Claude Code 2.1.319).
- **How (portable):** (1) While running, a heartbeat writes `aliveAt` each minute (and at stop) to a JSON file beside the database. (2) At boot, before recovery settles a thread, the agent's own chat is noted when it had a running turn, unsettled deliveries, background subagents still running, or a goal between two turns; the note is keyed by the interrupted turn, so the same turn is never noted twice. Not noted / skipped: channel, messaging and task threads (only the own chat); a turn the user stopped or that ended (not running); a turn with an open question (`ask_ui`, `ask_user`, AskUserQuestion); a turn of a routine run or of OpenBot's upkeep (`fork-compact`, `fork-keepwarm`; routine runs keep the conservative reconcile rules); a message that waits in the queue (it runs instead: the newest intent of the user, and never two turns). A long downtime is no skip (Elias, 6 Nov 2026: "it should continue"): when the app was down more than 2 h (`RESTART_RESUME_LONG_DOWNTIME_MS`, from `aliveAt`), the wake also says how long and the time now, and asks the agent to check each time-sensitive step (a send or post planned for a time, a deadline, "today") and to skip it and tell the user rather than do it late. (3) After start, each note opens a wait (Cancel) and a pump fires the notes one at a time: 10 s after the start, then 5 s apart (rate limits). At fire it skips when the wait was cancelled, the agent is deleted, hidden or has a new chat, another turn started in that chat since the restart (the user wrote), a message is queued, the goal stopped, or the turn had completed; it waits up to 10 min for the provider to be ready and signed in, then writes one quiet line ("🔄 Not resumed after the restart: codex was not ready…"), no loop; no active provider session → one quiet line. Else it closes the wait and queues a delivery with a routine sender (`routineId` `fork-restart-resume`, no real routine, name "🔄 Resumed after restart", within the 80-character contract limit), like the wake of `wait_until`: its text is the whole prompt, it runs in the agent's own session (excluded in `#runsInOwnSession`), its answer is never quiet (`quietRoutineDelivery`), and a routine run does not wake its wait. While a goal runs (`fork-goals.json` state `working`), the wake carries the goal footer, so the turn end continues the goal chain instead of leaving it paused. (4) Crash loop: a wake whose own turn is interrupted by the next restart is recognised by its run id (`restart-resume:<try>:<key>`) and gets one more try ("🔄 Resumed after restart (second try)", "This is the last automatic try"); after that it stays interrupted (`RESTART_RESUME_MAX_ATTEMPTS` 2). A wake already queued when the app stops just runs after the start, like any queued message. (5) Subagent IDs: the background `Agent` result's `agentId` is stored on the subagent (`ConversationSubagent.providerAgentId`, optional); for a subagent without it (a foreground one cut off mid-run) the wake reads the session's `subagents/*.meta.json` (`toolUseId` → agent ID). A work-folder move (entry 8) copies the `subagents/` folder with the transcript, so `SendMessage` finds it there. Codex and the other providers get the wake without the subagent part (their subagents cannot be continued).
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-restart-resume.ts` (`ForkRestartResume`: `capture`, `start`, `#fire`, `restartResumeText`, `restartResumeTitle`); hook `restartInterrupted` in `src/backend/agent/boot-recovery.ts` (`recoverPersistedTurns`); built in `src/backend/agent-service.ts` (`#restartResumeHooks`, enqueue via `RoutineScheduler.enqueueHandoff`, `start` in the "arm routines" step, `dispose` in `stop`, `turnStarted` in `#emit`, the `quietRoutineDelivery` exception); `forkHub.restartResume` (`fork-hub.ts`); prompt pass-through + goal footer in `delivery-content.ts`; own-session exception in `drain-scheduler.ts`; wait filter in `agent-waits.ts` (`wakeForRun`); `/autoresume` in `fork-commands.ts`; `providerAgentId` in `packages/contracts/src/ipc-conversation-subagents.ts` and `foldClaudeSubagentResult` (`subagent-updates.ts`); `claudeSubagentAgentIds` + the `subagents/` copy in `carryClaudeTranscript` (`claude-history.ts`), `forkSubagentIds` (`agent-client.ts`, `claude-client.ts`); i18n `status.agent.restartResume*` (English only). File: `~/Library/Application Support/OpenBot/fork-restart-resume.json` (`enabled`, `aliveAt`, the notes with state and reason). Test switches: `OPENBOT_FORK_RESTART_RESUME=0` (off), `OPENBOT_FORK_RESTART_RESUME_GRACE_MS`, `_STAGGER_MS`, `_PROVIDER_WAIT_MS`, `_SETTLE_WAIT_MS`.
- **Tests:** `bun scripts/restart-resume-e2e.ts` (real agent service, temp profile, fake Codex app-server, eight simulated restarts; 19 cases; `.openbot-build/restart-resume-e2e/report.json`); `src/backend/fork-restart-resume.test.ts` (wake text, title, run id, Claude agent ID, meta lookup, `subagents/` carry); `src/renderer/src/fork-restart-resume-projection.test.ts` (the wake is a routine line, not a user bubble).
- **While OpenBot runs:** a Claude process that ends with background subagents (crash, a close for a change) gets the same wake at once: entry 47.
- **Fixed in #113:** an agent on its own provider process (a second provider account, a confined workspace) was never woken (the wake waited 10 min for a client that only a turn starts, then wrote "…was not ready"), and a message the user wrote before the wake replaced it silently, so the agent never learned what the restart stopped. See entry 113.
- **Not done:** task threads (the agent tasks lane): not resumed. The wake path queues into the own-chat lane only (`enqueueHandoff`); a task wake needs a lane-targeted delivery, and task lanes also hold routine side threads (outward runs) and threads the user may have closed. No Settings switch: `/autoresume` and the file's `enabled` (the fork has no settings page of its own yet). No line for the quiet skips (queued message, stopped, question).
- **Limits:** a Stop pressed in the last moment before a crash, before the interrupt reached the provider, still counts as running (the stop lives in memory). The 2 h long-downtime note counts from the last heartbeat (up to 1 min early). A first start after installing this build has no heartbeat yet and uses the newest message time instead. Wakes of a crash loop run at most twice per interrupted turn; a wake that the next start finds already queued runs without the grace time.
- **Check:** give Shelf a long task that starts a background subagent ("start a background subagent that writes /tmp/rr-1..5.txt one per 20 s, then wait for it"). While it works, quit OpenBot and open it again. Within ~10 s: the "🔄 Resumes after the OpenBot restart" wait with Cancel, then "🔄 Resumed after restart"; Shelf checks /tmp, says the subagent was stopped, calls `SendMessage` to its agent ID, and the files continue from where they stopped. Again, but press Cancel on the wait: nothing runs. `/autoresume off`, restart while it works: the turn stays interrupted.

## 43. Database load test, and the queries that slowed down as data grew

- **Get:** a load test that seeds a synthetic `openbot.db` at 1x, 10x, 100x and 1000x of a three-month user, runs the app's own read and write paths on it, and reports p50/p95, query plans and significance (`perf/REPORT.md`, chart `perf/chart.html`). Four fixes from it: deleting a side thread and the whole-snapshot write no longer read every turn and activity ever stored; restart recovery no longer reads every turn; the routine-flow "answer of a turn" read no longer reads the whole chat; the memory list and search tools and the memory settings no longer read every selection once per memory.
- **Why:** Elias, 6 Nov 2026: find which queries fail at scale, fix them, prove it with numbers. SQLite runs on the main process, so a slow statement freezes the app.
- **How (portable):** seed raw rows with realistic fan-out (agent -> chats -> turns -> messages, with one event, receipt and activity per message, provider sessions, attachments, memories, routines and runs), clone the seed per run (APFS clone), run each operation warmed up and repeated, inside `BEGIN`/`ROLLBACK` for writes, and capture every prepared statement for `EXPLAIN QUERY PLAN`. List every foreign key whose child columns no index leads with: SQLite reads the whole child table once per deleted parent row. Compare two code versions in one process (each checkout loads as its own module graph, with its own `effect`), each on a clone of the same seed, timing every repeat in both in alternating order: on a busy Mac, separate runs of each version were confounded (an unchanged query came out 5x apart). Mann-Whitney U with Holm, bootstrap CI of the median ratio. Fixes: index every cascade child key (partial when most rows are NULL: a partial `WHERE col IS NOT NULL` index serves an FK action), a partial index for "running" rows, a unary `+` on the first ORDER BY term when SQLite picks the time index over a selective one, and a keyed read instead of filtering a full list per item.
- **Here:** `scripts/db-load-test.ts` (`--compare=<checkout of the code before>`), `scripts/db-load-test-report.ts`; migration 59 (`V59_THREAD_CHILD_INDEX_SQL` in `src/backend/openbot-database-schema.ts`, test `src/backend/thread-child-index-migration.test.ts`); `readTurnAssistantMessages` in `src/backend/database/conversation-queries.ts`; `getSelection` in `src/backend/agent-memory-store.ts`. Not fixed, measured in the report: message search reads every message (an FTS5 trigram prototype is in the report), an unread badge of a chat never opened counts its whole history on each sidebar refresh.
- **Check:** `node_modules/.bin/tsx scripts/db-load-test.ts --label=check --scales=1,10 --reps=30`: `write.deleteSideThread` shows no `SCAN projection_turns` or `SCAN projection_thread_activities`. Upgrade risk: migration 59 builds four indexes in one transaction at the first start of a build that has it; an older build then refuses the database (newer version).

## 45. Voice notes: an agent answers with a spoken MP3 (Deepgram Aura-2), like a voice memo

- **Get:** ask any agent for "a voice note", "a voice memo" or "answer by voice". It calls `openbot.send_voice_note({ text, language? })`, and an MP3 of its words in the voice of **Settings → Voice ▸ Voice notes** is attached to its reply and plays in the chat on the Mac and on the phone. Its text reply says the same words. The Voice notes row has a dropdown of the 91 Deepgram Aura-2 voices ("Orion, male · English (US)"), English first, and Listen; picking a voice plays a sample. Elias's voice: Orion. A note in another language (`language: "fr"`) uses a voice of that language with the same gender (Orion → Hector). `/voicenotes off` turns them off for that agent (the tool then refuses and tells it to answer in text), `/voicenotes on` turns them on again, `/voicenotes` shows the setting. On by default. The audio card in the chat is 480 px wide (was 360), so the player's progress bar has room.
- **Why:** Elias, 6 Nov 2026: "do we have the reply as voice memo like grok bot has?" (Grok Bot has voice memos, OpenMausBot has `send_voice_note`, Codync has only voice calls) → "port it". The first version used the local Supertonic 3 voice; on the first try Bot Bot never called the tool and made the note with macOS `say` instead, and Elias found the voice "too robotic" and the player "too small". He heard Supertonic, four Aura-2 voices and `say` side by side and chose Deepgram Aura-2 Orion with a dropdown to change it, and asked to drop the local model if not needed (it took ~350–400 MB and ~3 cores while it spoke).
- **How (portable):** a tool with `text` (1–1000 characters, as OpenMausBot) and an optional `language` (`VOICE_NOTE_LANGUAGES`). It checks the agent's switch, reads the key from the macOS Keychain (`/usr/bin/security find-generic-password -s DEEPGRAM_API_KEY -w`, else the `DEEPGRAM_API_KEY` variable; read at each call, never logged), and posts the text to `https://api.deepgram.com/v1/speak?model=<voice>&encoding=mp3` (30 s timeout). Deepgram answers the MP3 itself, so nothing runs on the Mac: no model, no encoder. MP3 because it is the one audio type every attachment surface takes. The MP3 is written to a new scratch folder `~/OpenBot/Shared/.voice-notes/note-*/voice-note-<date-time>.mp3` (a folder the attachment gateway accepts, never a project folder), attached with the gateway, message id and dedup of `attach_files_to_response`, and the scratch folder is removed after. Refusals (off, too long, empty, no key, 401/403 key refused, 402 credit gone, 429 busy) are correctable tool failures with a sentence the agent can act on. The tool is a visible (direct) tool, not only in the catalog, and its description says never to make a note with `say` or another text-to-speech program. The voice and the agents with notes off are in `fork-voice-notes.json` beside the database. Settings reaches it through three IPC requests in the `voice` group: `getVoiceNotes` (voice, key found), `setVoiceNoteVoice`, `previewVoiceNote` (sample sentence ≤ 300 characters → MP3 bytes, played from a blob URL). The voice list is `VOICE_NOTE_VOICES` (id, gender, region), taken from Deepgram's `GET /v1/models` on 6 Nov 2026.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-voice-notes.ts` (`ForkVoiceNotes`: `record`, `discard`, `enabled`, `setEnabled`, `voice`, `settings`, `setVoice`, `preview`; Deepgram request, Keychain key, `voiceFor`); voices, languages, settings types in `packages/contracts/src/ipc-voice.ts` (fork section at the end); endpoints in `packages/contracts/src/ipc-endpoints.ts`; handlers in `src/main/ipc/voice-handlers.ts` (through `forkHub.voiceNotes`), parsers in `src/main/ipc/voice-inputs.ts`, test group in `define-ipc-group.test.ts`; decoders in `src/preload/app-decoding.ts` + `src/preload/index.ts`; preview mock in `src/renderer/src/preview/mock-voice.ts`; store (`setVoiceNoteVoice`, `playVoiceNote`, `stopVoiceNoteSample`) in `settings-voice-store.ts`; the row in `SettingsVoiceTab.tsx`; i18n `settings.voice.voiceNotes.*` (English only, `untranslated.json`); tool definition in `src/backend/openbot-tools.ts`, visible in `src/backend/agent/tool-catalog.ts`; dispatch, correctable failure and uninterruptible attach in `src/backend/agent/openbot-tool-router.ts`; `forkHub.voiceNotes` (`fork-hub.ts`), built in `src/backend/agent-service.ts`; `/voicenotes` in `src/backend/fork-commands.ts`; card width in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/styles/attachments.css`; `PRIVACY.md` (the text goes to Deepgram); counts in `tools/agent-context/budget.json`.
- **Tests:** `bun scripts/voice-note-e2e.ts` (real Deepgram API with the Keychain key: default voice Orion, the voice survives a new instance, an English note in the chosen voice and a French note in Hector as MP3s ffprobe can read, a Settings sample, refusals for too long, empty and off, the switch is per agent, scratch folder removed; `.openbot-build/voice-note-e2e/report.json` and the MP3s). 6 Nov 2026: passed, ~2 s per note. `bun run test:desktop -- src/main/ipc/define-ipc-group.test.ts` passed.
- **Not done:** a voice per agent (one voice for all). Other engines (ElevenLabs, OpenAI). A key field in Settings (the key lives in the Keychain). An agent on a remote host makes the note on that host, with that host's Keychain.
- **Check:** Settings → Voice ▸ Voice notes: the dropdown shows "Orion, male · English (US)"; Listen plays the sample; pick Thalia: the sample plays in her voice; pick Orion again. In Bot Bot's chat: "send me a voice note saying the intro is ready" → a wide `voice-note-….mp3` card plays the sentence in Orion's voice, and the steps show `send_voice_note`, not `say`. `/voicenotes off`, ask again: text only. `/voicenotes on`.

## 46. A refresh waits for background subagents

- **Get:** a background subagent keeps working when OpenBot refreshes the agents' tools (an MCP server saved, a `~/.claude.json` change, the cua-driver daemon coming or going, a skill installed). When it ends, the lead reports by itself as before.
- **Why:** Tapa Lead, 6 Nov 2026: Elias had to ask "whats the progress w the subagent?". Its background SFX subagent showed "Stopped" after 18 min. At 20:03 Bot Bot added the Windows cua MCP server; the refresh that followed closed every idle agent's Claude process, and the subagent died with it in the middle of a request, so no "done" notification ever reached the lead. A refresh waited only for a running turn, and a lead whose turn ended reads as idle while its subagent works.
- **How (portable):** the client says whether a session holds work that outlives its turn (`AgentClient.holdsBackgroundWork`; Claude: the CLI's `background_tasks_changed` count, which the SDK calls the reliable level signal). `applyPendingRuntimeRefresh` defers such a thread like a busy one: the refresh mark stays, and the next delivery start of that agent spends it once the work ended. The turn that the subagent's finish starts runs on the old session, with the old tools.
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent/thread-lifecycle.ts` (`applyPendingRuntimeRefresh`, upstream's `#runsBackgroundWork`); `src/backend/agent-client.ts` (`holdsBackgroundWork`); `src/backend/claude-client.ts`.
- **Upstream (merge of 18 Nov 2026):** upstream #10335 (#8484) made a skill or setting refresh wait for background work too, but closes the session at once after an MCP change ("revoked MCP access must leave the session at once"), and waits only for Claude. The fork keeps its rule: every refresh, an MCP change included, waits for every provider's background work (`thread-lifecycle.ts`). So an MCP server that is removed or signed out stays in a session that runs a background subagent until that work ends.
- **Tests:** `bun scripts/provider-session-reuse-e2e.ts`, case `claude-refresh-waits-for-background`: a stand-in `claude` reports a background task, `refreshAllAgentRuntimes()` runs, the process stays (it exited before the fix); the task ends, the next turn closes it and resumes the same session.
- **Not done:** ACP and Codex report no background work, so nothing changes for them. A user message while the subagent works still runs on the old tools.
- **Check:** in an agent's chat: "start a background subagent that runs `sleep 120` and then says done". While it runs, save any MCP server in Settings. After 2 min the subagent shows done and the lead writes by itself.

## 47. Stopped subagents continue

- **Get:** when a Claude process ends while OpenBot keeps running (the `claude` CLI crashes, or OpenBot closes it for a settings, account or tool change) and a background subagent still worked, the chat shows "🔄 A background subagent stopped when its Claude process ended. The agent continues it in a few seconds. Cancel keeps it stopped." for about 10 s, then the line "🔄 Stopped subagent continues", and the lead gets one wake: the subagents by agent ID, "continue each with SendMessage, do NOT launch it again", check what is done, never repeat sends. When it is not continued, a line says why, so you know: a new message came first, the chat has a new provider session (context reset, account or provider change), 3 stops in the last hour, Claude did not start again in time, or `/autoresume` is off. Each of them ends with what to write ("continue the subagent"). A subagent that had already finished when the process died (only its "done" notification was lost) is not continued: the wake gives its final answer and says "Do NOT continue or relaunch them — use their answers as if they had reported".
- **Why:** Elias, 6 Nov 2026, after Tapa Lead's SFX subagent stopped at 20:03 and he had to ask for its progress: "should we make it send a message to the bot to continue if it gets stopped like that?" … "w all edge cases needed"; then "cant we know if the subagents work is done will it still ping it and restart it for no reason..?". Entry 42 does this only after an OpenBot restart; entry 46 stops the tool refresh from killing them, but a crash or another close still did, silently. A crashed process also left its subagents "running" in the chat.
- **How (portable):** the Claude client, when it closes a process (`#closeRuntime`) or its stream ends unexpectedly (`#fail`, now also for every other process of the client), reads the background subagents that still run (`runningBackgroundSubagents`), marks every subagent stopped, and sends `openbot/fork-background-stopped` (cause `closed` / `crashed`, the session, the turn, id, title and `SendMessage` id of each). Not while OpenBot quits (`stop`): entry 42 does it at the next start. `TurnLifecycle` passes it to `ForkRestartResume.captureStopped`: own chat only (channel, messaging and task threads, a deleted agent: nothing), one note per turn (`subagents:<turn>`, the same key as entry 42, so a stop that is still pending merges and a quit before the wake runs is woken at the next start). It reuses entry 42's wait, pump, Cancel, provider wait and enqueue, with `trigger: "process"`: a turn started since the stop or a queued message → line, no wake (a new message is the user's newest intent and never two turns); the session must be the one the subagents ran in (`SendMessage` finds them only there); at most 3 woken stops per agent per hour (`LIVE_WAKES_PER_HOUR`), and a turn whose continued subagents stop again gets one more try (attempt 2), then a line. `#tookOver` became the time of the last own-chat turn start, compared with each note's capture time, so a live note is not skipped by a turn from before it. Just before the wake, `#fire` asks the client for each subagent's outcome (`forkSubagentOutcomes` → `claudeSubagentOutcomes`): the last 256 KB of `<session>/subagents/agent-<id>.jsonl`; when its last record is an assistant message with `stop_reason: "end_turn"`, text and no `tool_use`, the subagent is done and its text (up to 1500 characters, 6000 for all) goes in the wake. A `tool_use`, a `tool_result` or an `api-request` at the end means mid-step: it is continued. A missing or unreadable transcript also means continue.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-restart-resume.ts` (`captureStopped`, `#skipLive`, `FORK_BACKGROUND_STOPPED_METHOD`, the `process` texts in `restartResumeText` and `restartResumeTitle`); `src/backend/claude-client.ts` (`#stopSubagentsOf`, `#fail`); `src/backend/agent/subagent-updates.ts` (`runningBackgroundSubagents`); `src/backend/agent/turn-lifecycle.ts` (the notification case); i18n `status.agent.subagentResume*` (English only); `src/backend/claude-history.ts` (`claudeSubagentOutcomes`, `finishedSubagentResult`); `AgentClient.forkSubagentOutcomes`.
- **Tests:** `bun scripts/subagent-stop-resume-e2e.ts` (real agent service, temp profile, a stand-in `claude` that starts background subagents and is killed and writes subagent transcripts; 9 cases: crash wakes in the same session with the agent ID and the subagent shows stopped, Cancel, a message first, context reset, `/autoresume off`, the hourly limit, a foreground subagent gets no wake, a quit notes nothing live, a subagent that finished before the crash gets its answer handed over, not continued; `.openbot-build/subagent-stop-resume-e2e/report.json`). Also run: `restart-resume-e2e.ts` (19 cases), `provider-session-reuse-e2e.ts` (4), `fork-restart-resume.test.ts` (11).
- **Not done:** Codex and ACP agents (only Claude reports background subagents that outlive a turn). A subagent continued with `SendMessage` is not tracked as a subagent, so if it stops again, no wake (the hourly limit is the guard for loops). Not in CI: like entry 42's test, the script is not a `package.json` script.
- **Also (#83):** OpenBot's own stop of a Claude turn (the quiet end of a no-update turn) also ends background subagents in the CLI; it is reported with cause `interrupted` and wakes the agent the same way.
- **Check:** in an agent's chat: "start a background subagent that writes /tmp/sr-1..6.txt, one per 20 s". While it works, kill its `claude` process (`ps` → `kill -9 <pid>`). Within ~10 s: the "🔄 A background subagent stopped…" wait with Cancel, then "🔄 Stopped subagent continues"; the agent calls `SendMessage` and the files continue. Again, but write any message before the 10 s end: the line "…not continued, because a new message came first".

## 48. A channel opens with its last page, not empty

- **Get:** going back to a channel (a pinned tile such as "Editors", a channel row, a search result) shows its transcript at once. New messages come in a moment later, without the view going empty first.
- **Why:** Elias, 7 Nov 2026 (screen recording, Bot Bot → Editors): "why is it flickering or going empty and then back to normal like that?". Upstream's controller holds one page only: opening a channel set it to `null` and waited for `listChannels` + `readChannel`, so the view drew empty for ~0.3 s each time.
- **How (portable):** when the reader leaves a channel (opens another one, an agent, or closes it), a snapshot of its page goes into a small cache (last 12 channels, oldest dropped). Opening a channel puts its cached page on screen and still starts the read; the read merges with it through upstream's `mergeChannelPage` (joins or replaces on a sequence gap), so nothing old hides new messages. The cache is cleared on a scope change (another account or server) and a channel's entry is dropped when the channel is gone.
- **Here:** `leftPages`, `leavePage`, `takeLeftPage` in `src/renderer/src/features/channels/channels-controller.ts` (`open`, `close`, the scope effect, `read`).
- **Tests:** `bun run typecheck:renderer`, Biome. Not run in the app yet (night queue).
- **Check:** open a channel with history, click an agent, click the channel again: the messages are there on the first frame (record with QuickShotX: no empty frame between).

## 49. Chat videos show their first frame before Play

- **Get:** a video file in a chat (an agent's `before-after.mp4`, a file you sent) shows its first frame in the player at once, not a grey box with `0:00`.
- **Why:** Elias, 8 Nov 2026 (screenshot of the Editors channel): "Unless I click on the actual play icon, it stays as a gray gradient … i like for the thumbnail to be actually shown". Upstream gives every player with a host URL `preload="none"`, so Chromium reads no byte until Play.
- **How (portable):** a file on this computer (`openbot-attachment:` URL) gets `preload="metadata"`: the protocol answers byte ranges, so Chromium reads only the start (and the `moov` box) and paints the first frame. A remote server's file (`openbot-remote-attachment:`) keeps `none`, because that protocol downloads the whole file on each request.
- **Here:** `mediaPreload` in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/AttachmentCards.tsx`.
- **Tests:** Biome, `bun run typecheck:renderer`. Not run in the app yet (night queue).
- **Check:** open a chat with an agent's MP4 that was never played: the player shows a frame of the video and its length.

## 50. Drop an image on an agent's or channel's picture

- **Get:** drag an image from Finder onto the picture in agent or channel settings, or onto the editor's "Upload image" button: it becomes the picture, as choosing it in the file picker does. The picture or button shows a green outline while a file is over it.
- **Why:** Elias, 8 Nov 2026 (screenshot of the Editors channel's picture editor): "drag drop into icon not working … clicking and choosing from finder does work tho". Upstream has no drop handler there, so the desktop preload's window `drop` listener took the file as a chat attachment (over the settings panel), or nothing happened (over the editor popover).
- **How (portable):** `createAvatarImageDrop(onFile)` gives `onDragEnter/Over/Leave/Drop` and an `active` signal. On a drag with files it calls `preventDefault` and `stopPropagation`, so the preload's window listener never sees the drop; it uploads the first image file through the same path as the file picker (`normalizeAvatarFile`). Wired on the avatar trigger and the upload button of both editors; ignored while an upload is running. The chat overlays already have `pointer-events: none` or stop at the settings panel, so they do not cover the picture.
- **Here:** `packages/ui/src/avatar-image-drop.ts`, `AgentSettingsPanel.tsx`, `channels/ChannelPictureEditor.tsx`, `.avatar-image-drop-active` in `src/renderer/src/styles/app-shell.css`.
- **Tests:** Biome, `bun run typecheck:renderer`. Ran in an isolated dev instance (sandbox HOME, CDP `Input.dispatchDragEvent` with a real file, which goes through Chromium's native drag path and the preload): a PNG dropped on the channel picture, on the channel editor's Upload button and on an agent editor's Upload button each became the picture; nothing was attached to the chat. Not yet a Finder drag on the installed build.
- **Check:** in channel settings drag a PNG from Finder onto the picture: it shows a green outline, then becomes the picture; the message box gets no attachment. Same on the "Upload image" button and in agent settings.

## 50. Channel handoffs are one line: "Tapa Lead asked Gotham ▸"

- **Get:** in a channel (desktop, web client and phone), when a member gives work to another member, the chat shows one row: the sender, "asked" (or "handed over to"), the member, the time, and a chevron. The chevron opens the full brief under the row. Click a name to open that agent's chat.
- **Why:** Elias, 8 Nov 2026 (4 screenshots of Editors): "it looks like it sent the message that I wanted to send to Gotham to me". Upstream posts every `channel_assign` / `channel_transfer` as a full bubble `"<recipient>: <brief>"` from the sender, as a reply to the person's request, so it showed a "You" quote and read like an answer to him. The brief did reach Gotham.
- **How (portable):** only the desktop view changes. The stored handoff has no mark (author `system`, the sender as member author), so the row is found from the task it started: a child of the message's task (assign) or the same task with a new owner (transfer), whose instruction is exactly the text after `"<name>: "`. That message becomes an outgoing agent-message row (the "Messaged" row of agent chats) with `channelHandoff` set; the reply quote is dropped; the delivery state comes from the task state. The host, the database, the Team API and other clients keep the plain message. On Elias's Editors channel (221 messages, 55 tasks) it finds the one handoff and none of the 28 other agent messages that start with "Name: ".

## 54. Background work stays open and reaches the phone

- **Get:** a group-chat task whose agent started background subagents no longer reads as done when the agent's reply ends: it stays open ("waiting", marked `waitingFor: "subagents"`) until every one of them ended. The agent's report when they end (Claude starts that turn by itself) now shows in the channel under the same task, and its end closes the task; if more subagents still work, the task waits again. When the subagents end and no report comes within 3 min (the report went into another turn), the task closes as done. A stopped or cancelled task is never reopened. Side threads say `subagentsRunning: true` while theirs work. On the phone: "Tapa Lead: A subagent finished: Find B-roll" (or "… stopped with an error: …"), one push for the ends of one agent within 15 s ("2 subagents finished. 1 subagent stopped with an error."), only when nobody used the Mac in the last 30 s; otherwise it goes to the phone after 2 min with no input, as a waiting question does.
- **Why:** Elias, 8 Nov 2026: "i keep asking 'whats the progress'". Measured on his data: all 73 Editors channel tasks were `completed` while 2 Tapa Lead subagents were still `running`, and the answer Claude gave when a background subagent ended was dropped from the channel (a turn with no delivery was nobody's reply), so nobody was told.
- **How (portable):**
  - **Signal:** `TurnLifecycle.#applySubagents` emits `subagents-ended` (local `AgentEvent`) when subagents of a turn that already ended stop running; `subagentsRunning(agentId, turnId)` answers from `#subagentTurns`.
  - **Channel task:** `ChannelService.complete` leaves a task `waiting` + `waitingFor: "subagents"` when its turn's (or an earlier turn's) subagents still run, and does not release its parent. `#bindReportTurn` gives a provider turn with no delivery (`origin: "unknown"`) to that task as a new running assignment (no delivery), so `capture` keeps its rows. `#subagentsEnded` + a 3 min timer close a task whose report never came. In memory only; `recover` closes waiting tasks at start (a restart stops every subagent) and a report turn with no delivery.
  - **Why `waiting`, not `running`:** a running assignment holds the agent's channel lane, so "what's the progress?" would queue behind the subagents. `waiting` keeps the task open in every view (the UI already counts it as active and Stop covers it) while the agent stays free to answer.
  - **Phone:** `PushNotificationService` groups `subagents-ended` per agent for `ENDED_SUBAGENTS_GROUP_MS`, keeps it like a waiting request when the owner is at the Mac (`hostComputerInUse` says yes for it whenever there was input in the last 30 s) and sends it after `WAITING_REQUEST_IDLE_MS`. Text from `notificationForAgentEvent` at level "all", with the agent's own notification switch; no Live Activity shortcut. Stopped subagents are not news.
- **Here:** `src/backend/channel-service.ts`, `src/backend/agent/turn-lifecycle.ts`, `src/backend/agent-service.ts`, `src/backend/agent-tasks/agent-tasks.ts`, `src/main/push-notifications.ts`, `src/main/application-services.ts`, `packages/team-client/src/agent-notifications.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/ipc-agent-events.ts` (`subagents-ended`), `ipc-chat-channels.ts` (`waitingFor`), `ipc-agent-tasks.ts` (`subagentsRunning`), `notification.subagentFinished*` / `notification.subagentsFinished` / `notification.subagentsFailed` in all 14 locales.
- **Tests:** Biome; `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, `typecheck:i18n`, contracts and team-client typechecks. `src/backend/fork-channel-subagent-wait.test.ts` (real database and channel service: waits, takes the report turn, waits again, a cancelled task stays cancelled, closes after the grace time); `src/main/fork-subagent-push.test.ts` (grouping, stops ignored, held while at the Mac and sent after 2 min idle, the texts). Not run: a live Claude background subagent in a channel end to end, and a real phone push.
- **Check:** in a group chat ask the lead for something it hands to a background subagent: after its reply the task still shows as open; when the subagent ends, the lead's report shows in the channel and the task closes. Leave the Mac alone: the phone gets "<Agent>: A subagent finished: <title>".

## 56. A group chat says when its agent is finishing another chat first

- **Get:** a message to a group chat whose agent is busy in another chat no longer shows nothing. The working line reads "Tapa Lead · finishing another chat first, this one is next", or, when others work too, "Chief is working … Tapa Lead: finishing another chat first". The composer's Stop button was the only sign before.
- **Why:** Elias, 8 Nov 2026: "sometimes i send it a message in a group chat and no response, is that normal? what's the reason? investigate and fix". Measured on his Editors channel (read-only copy): "progress ? and whta tasks are running?" at 01:44:35 UTC was routed at 01:44:41. Tapa Lead's turn started at 01:46:56, the moment its turn in its own 1:1 chat ended (turn 2271bbbd, which made a "Morning brief" routine); the reply came 37 s later. Over 84 of his Editors messages, the wait before the turn started was 5 s median, over 2 min 4 times, and 19.5 min at worst. All that time the channel showed no worker, because `channelWorkers` only named owners of running tasks.
- **How (portable):**
  - An agent runs one turn at a time across all its chats. `ChannelService` keeps a task queued while `hooks.busy(owner)` (`AgentService.#runsTurn`). That design stays: running turns in parallel would share one session, folder and memory.
  - `channelWorkers` now also returns the owner of each queued task that is not already working and not hidden, with `queued: true`.
  - `ChannelActivityIndicator` shows the queued text instead of the animated label when everyone it names is queued, and adds a muted "{names}: finishing another chat first" when only some are.
- **Here:** `src/renderer/src/features/channels/channel-conversation-rows.ts`, `packages/ui/src/features/channels/ChannelActivityIndicator.tsx`, `styles/subagents.css`, `channel.activity.queuedLabel` / `queuedNames` in every locale.

## 57. Long turns stay visible: read receipts, ⌘↩ queues, honest trace, no empty nudges, big chats compact

- **Get:**
  - A message sent while an agent works (steered into its turn) shows "⏳ Waits for the current step" beside the bubble, then "👀 Seen 02:48" once the model has read it. Claude, Codex and the ACP agents (Gemini/Antigravity, OpenCode, Devin, custom ACP agents) report the read; Grok, the Pi agents (Pi, Inception, DeepSeek, OpenRouter) and Muse do not yet, so there the mark stays "Waits" until the turn ends and then goes away.
  - A message that goes into the running turn (plain ↩ with "Steer agents while they work", or Steer) shows in the chat at once. It no longer shows in the queue panel above the composer for a moment first. Elias, 8 Nov 2026: "it appears slightly above the chat very quick for a couple miliszeconds and then it gets sent". `queueSend` in `composer-actions.ts` sends it with `queued: false`, so it takes the "starting" path: the chat keeps its pending row and the panel hides it for 3 s. If the host queues it after all, the panel shows it after that grace.
  - ⌘↩ (Ctrl+Enter off macOS) while the agent works queues the message instead: it waits and starts the agent's next turn, even when "Steer agents while they work" is on. Plain ↩ still follows that setting. Only when ↩ is the send key.
  - The trace no longer shows Claude's thinking as one bar across the whole turn marked "Slowest". Each burst of thinking is its own bar, ending at its last word. "LLM calls" is the number of model requests Claude made.
  - "The plan has open steps. The agent continues" no longer fires when the answer asks the user to act ("Approve 3–5 and I'll commit them", "Still waiting: your approval…") or while the agent's background subagents still run.
  - A Claude chat over 300k tokens is compacted 20 s after a turn ends, when nothing else waits ("🗜 Compact a big chat (550k tokens)"), not only after 55 quiet minutes.
- **Why:** Elias, 8 Nov 2026, about Gotham: "is this normal? considering it had work to do but it didn't report back to me?" Measured: one 20-minute turn (01:45–02:05 UTC) did everything, but his two messages sent at 01:47 got no sign until the answer at 02:05 (Claude read them at 01:48:52, after a 3-minute commit hook). Earlier, two plan nudges only made it say "Waiting…" and "Still waiting…". The trace's "Slowest 20m 6s" was the thinking item; the real time went to 4 tool steps of 2.5–3.5 min each (typecheck hooks at load 200). Each of 30 requests carried 510k–550k tokens ($3.88, 15.9M tokens), because the chat never went quiet for 55 minutes. Then: "cmd enter should queue my messages instead of steering (so they get sent when the bot responds the next time)". Elias approved all fixes and the 300k limit.
- **How (portable):**
  - Read receipt: `ClaudeAgentClient` keeps each steer's uuid (`runtime.unreadSteers`). The CLI stamps `user_message_uuids` on each assistant message; a steer uuid there emits `openbot/steer-read`. `TurnLifecycle` calls `MailboxStore.noteSteerRead` (in memory only, no stored row changes, max 500) and `emitQueue`. The projected user message gets `delivery.steered` / `delivery.readAt` (`steerReceipt`, both projection paths); `toAgentMessage` makes `receipt.seenTime`; `ChatMessageRow` shows it (`.message-receipt`, always visible). A restart drops the receipts.
  - ⌘↩: `SendMessageInput.queue: true` (decoded in `parseSendMessage`); `AgentService.sendMessage` skips `#steerWhenBusy`. `ComposerEditor.onQueue` (⌘/Ctrl+Enter while `sendShortcut` is `enter`), `queueOnModEnter` in `ConversationComposer`, `queueComposer` in `composer-actions.ts`, the flag through `onSendMessage`. Only the local host gets the flag; a remote host keeps its own mode.
  - Trace: `TurnTraces` keeps steps in an array; a thought or message with an event after another step starts a segment `<id>#n`, and the segment before ends at its own last event; an open thought ends at its last event at `take`. `ClaudeAgentClient` emits `openbot/model-call` once per new model message id; `TurnTraces.countModelCall` → `ConversationTurnTrace.modelCalls`; the turn span carries it and `TraceWaterfall` totals use it in place of counting model children.
  - Nudges: `waitsForUser` also matches `ASKS_USER_TO_ACT` (a line that starts with approve/confirm/tell me/let me know/say/reply/pick/choose/decide, or names "your approval/go-ahead/ok/sign-off/confirmation/decision/answer/choice/call/input/review"), after list markers are removed. `#planContinues` returns false while `#subagentTurns` has the agent.
  - Big chats: `ForkCacheCompact.turnEnded` arms a 20 s check (`#compactBig`): Claude, not busy, no keep-warm window, context ≥ `FORK_COMPACT_BIG_TOKENS` (300k) → the same `/compact` delivery, labelled "Compact a big chat". The end of that compaction turn arms no new check.
- **Here:** `src/backend/claude-client.ts`, `src/backend/agent/turn-lifecycle.ts`, `turn-traces.ts`, `plan-continuation.ts`, `mailbox-sync.ts`, `src/backend/mailbox-store.ts`, `src/backend/fork-cache-compact.ts`, `src/backend/agent-service.ts`, `src/main/ipc/agent-inputs.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/ipc-conversation-messages.ts` / `ipc-conversation-trace.ts` / `ipc-conversations.ts`, `packages/ui/src/data.ts`, `ChatMessageRow.tsx`, `ComposerEditor.tsx`, `TraceWaterfall.tsx`, `styles/messages.css`, `src/renderer/src/app-message-projection.ts`, `turn-steps.ts`, `features/conversation/{ConversationComposer.tsx,conversation-context.tsx,conversation-scope.ts,conversation-types.ts,stores/composer-actions.ts}`, i18n `chat.receipt.waiting` / `chat.receipt.seen` (English only).
- **Tests:** `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, `typecheck:i18n`, Biome on each changed file, `fork-restart-resume-projection.test.ts`. Scratch runs: `waitsForUser` on the 4 real Gotham lines (all now wait) and 4 lines that must not; a simulated turn through `TurnTraces` (thinking 0–34 ms, tool 34–234 ms, thinking #2, answer; modelCalls 2). Not run in the app: night queue.
- **Check:** in a Claude agent's own chat, ask for a task with a 2-minute shell command. While it runs, send a message with ↩: "⏳ Waits for the current step", then "👀 Seen …" after the command ends. Send another with ⌘↩: it shows in the queue and starts a new turn after the answer. Open the trace (the "N steps" row): thinking bars are short, LLM calls ≈ the requests. For the compaction, a chat over 300k shows "🗜 Compact a big chat" 20 s after a turn.
- **Fix, 24 Nov 2026 (Gemini, OpenCode, Codex):** a message steered into a Gemini turn kept "⏳ Waits for the current step" for the whole turn, also after the model had read and answered it. Vegapunk (Antigravity 1.5.16, mode "steer"), turn `9e2d6f01` from 10:17:40Z: messages steered in at 10:29:18Z, 10:34:34Z and 10:36:57Z, answered 10–50 s later, the mark never changed. Cause: only `ClaudeAgentClient` emitted `openbot/steer-read`; the ACP client and the Codex client never did.
  - **How:** `AcpAgentClient` keeps the turn's steers in `AcpTurn.unreadSteers` (delivery id → delivered). A steer by prompt (Antigravity, Devin) goes in just before its `session/prompt` and comes out again when the CLI refuses it; a `_session/steering` steer (OpenCode, custom ACP agents) goes in when the agent answers that it took it. The steer is delivered at the next end of a model step: a tool call that ends (`completed`/`failed`), or the agent's echo of a user message (`user_message_chunk`; Antigravity echoes a prompt when it puts it into the conversation, seen for wake prompts in #10569, not yet seen live for a steer). It is read at the first model output after that: thought or answer text (not blank, not the flavor's noise or retry lines), a new tool call, or a plan. `openbot/steer-read` then goes through the same `TurnLifecycle` → `MailboxStore.noteSteerRead` path as Claude. Not the moment the CLI takes the steer (Antigravity ends the open prompt at once, OpenCode answers `injected` at once): the model has not seen it then; it sees it at its next request, after the running step. `CodexAppServerClient` keeps the `clientUserMessageId` of each `turn/steer` (dropped when the request fails or the turn completes); Codex (0.136.0 and later; the managed one is 0.172.0) echoes it as `clientId` on the `userMessage` `item/started` that it sends when it drains the steer into the history for the next model request, and that item emits `openbot/steer-read`.
  - **Limits:** an ACP agent that does not echo the steer and whose model answers it with no tool call in between gets no read until the turn ends (then the mark goes away, as before). An ACP model that was writing when the steer was echoed can show "Seen" a few seconds early, at the rest of that answer. A Codex steer whose request timed out gets no read.
  - **Here:** `src/backend/acp-client.ts` (`unreadSteers`, `#noteSteerReads`, `#isModelOutput`, `endsModelStep`, `#steerByPrompt`, `turn/steer`), `src/backend/app-server-client.ts` (`#awaitSteerRead`, `#noteSteerRead`).
  - **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-steer-read.test.ts` drives the real clients over scripted fakes (`scripts/provider-fakes/agents/steer-read.ts` for ACP, `codex-steer-read.ts` for Codex) and checks after each step: Antigravity steer by prompt (no read at the CLI's take, a tool update, the tool end or the echo; read at the next thought; a second steer while the model writes is not read by that text or the next tool call, only after that tool ends); OpenCode `_session/steering` (same, no echo); Codex (no read for the turn's own input, other items or a `userMessage` with no `clientId`; read at the steer's `clientId`; none for a refused steer or after the turn completed); a steer the CLI refuses gets none. With `main`'s clients 3 of 4 fail, with the fix 4/4 pass. Also `test:antigravity-steer`, `test:opencode-steer`, `test:devin-steer`, `test:custom-agent-steer` pass, `bun run test:related` for both clients (5 files, 22 tests), `typecheck:node`, Biome. Not run: a live Antigravity or Codex turn in the app.
