## 59. Claude accounts sign out one at a time: Account 1 has its own sign-in, each agent waits only for its own account

- **Get:** Claude Account 1 (the provider's own sign-in) no longer shares its sign-in with the terminal `claude`, neptune, Ghostex or the night run, so they cannot sign it out. An agent on Account 2 keeps working, and has no "Sign in required" banner, while Account 1 is signed out, and the reverse. After the first install with this, sign Account 1 in once (Settings › Providers › Claude, in the Chrome profile of its account).
- **Why:** 8 Nov 2026: at 10:43, with OpenBot quit, the `~/.claude` sign-in (Keychain `Claude Code-credentials`) was cleared to blank tokens. The CLI does that after a refresh gets `invalid_grant` (`tengu_oauth_refresh_token_marked_dead_invalid_grant`): the server changes the refresh token at each refresh, and many CLIs used that one sign-in. Signed out also on 7 Nov 07:38 with the app open. Then OpenBot held the queued messages of every Claude agent, also of agents on Account 2, whose sign-in worked: the drain and the banner read only the provider's own sign-in. Upstream #5187 (read the stored sign-in instead of `auth status`) is in and does not cover this.
- **How (portable):** (1) In the packaged app, the own sign-in runs with `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR` = `<userData>/fork-claude-home`: a folder with a symlink to each entry of `~/.claude` except `.claude.json`, `.config.json`, `.credentials.json`, `.credentials.lock` and `backups`, made again at each start (new entries are linked, entries the CLI made there stay). CLAUDE.md, settings, hooks, skills, plugins and the saved sessions (`projects`) stay the same; on macOS the CLI names the Keychain item after the folder, so the sign-in is separate. The variable goes in `claudeEnvironment` and in the Claude sign-in/sign-out command environment; an account's folder still wins (it comes later). Development keeps `~/.claude`. (2) `ProviderRuntime.signInRequiredForAgent`: an agent on an account after the first waits only while that account's last check is `signed-out` (no check yet does not hold it); the drain (`drain-scheduler.ts` both places) and `providerReady` use it. An account that leaves `signed-out` wakes the drain of its provider (`accountSignedIn` host hook). The composer banner follows the agent's account; its button opens the provider settings, where each account has its own sign-in.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-claude-home.ts` (prepared in `src/main/application-services.ts`), `claudeEnvironment` in `src/backend/claude-client.ts`, `CLAUDE_COMMAND_ENV` in `src/backend/provider-drivers.ts`; `signedOut` and `accountSignedIn` in `src/backend/agent/provider-accounts.ts`; `signInRequiredForAgent` in `src/backend/agent/provider-runtime.ts`; `src/backend/agent/drain-scheduler.ts`, `src/backend/agent-service.ts`; `signInRequired` in `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/ConversationComposer.tsx`.
- **Tests:** `bun scripts/fork-claude-home-e2e.ts` (fake `~/.claude`: links, skipped sign-in entries, second start, the sign-in command's environment; real CLI with the real `~/.claude` as source: settings linked, `auth status` signed out) passed 8 Nov 2026, `.openbot-build/fork-claude-home-e2e/report.json`. `bun run test:changed` (66 files) passed; `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, Biome. Not run in the app: night queue.
- **Check:** after the install, `~/Library/Application Support/OpenBot/fork-claude-home` holds links (`ls -la`), and Account 1 asks for a sign-in once; sign in hakifx91. Then the Keychain has `Claude Code-credentials-5edb7839` (that folder's item) and `Claude Code-credentials` (the terminal's) is not changed by it. Sign Account 2 out: an agent on Account 1 still answers; an agent on Account 2 shows the banner, and its button opens Providers.

## 60. Question cards show math and a hint ladder

- **Get:** an agent's question card (`ask_ui`: form, choice, quick replies, confirm title) shows LaTeX math typeset, not `x^n`. Form, choice and quick-reply cards can carry up to 3 hints, hidden until Elias taps 💡 **Hint**; each tap opens the next one (a nudge, then the method or formula, then the first step). A tutor bot (Owl) can ask "run the 3 steps" and still give the formulas to the one who forgot them, without giving the answer.
- **Why:** 8 Nov 2026, Owl's card "Does f_n(x) = x^n converge uniformly on [0,1[?". Elias: "Formulas not written in math ascii/whatever" and "it din't show me the actual formulas/thigns to do to check each step". Card text was plain text by contract (only `confirm.preview` was Markdown) and a card had nowhere to put the method. Elias chose "Math + hints".
- **How (portable):** (1) Card text stays plain text; the renderer cuts out math with the chat's own delimiters (`$…$`, `\(…\)`; a single `$` before a digit or a space is not math, so "$5 and $10" stays text) and typesets only that part with KaTeX (`trust: false`). No Markdown or HTML is read in titles or labels. (2) An optional `hints: string[]` (1–3, ≤2000 characters each, safe Markdown with math) on form, choice and quick_replies, rendered under the card body with a "Hint" / "Hint 2/3" button; opened hints stay after the answer. The fallback questions (for a client that does not know cards) leave hints out. (3) The tool description tells every agent to write math as `$…$` and what hints are for.
- **Here:** `hints`, `readHints`, `uiBlockSpecWithoutHints` in `packages/contracts/src/ui-blocks.ts`; `uiHintsSchema` and the `ask_ui` description in `src/backend/openbot-tools.ts`; `UiMathText.tsx` and `UiBlockHints.tsx` in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/ui-blocks/` (used by `UiBlockCard`, `UiFormBlock`, `UiChoiceBlock`, `UiQuickReplies`); `renderMarkdown` passes the chat's Markdown renderer from `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/UiBlockPrompt.tsx`; styles in `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/styles/ui-blocks.css`; keys `uiBlock.hint.*` in all 14 languages.
- **Phone and web client:** the released Team API wire `ui-blocks-v1` is frozen, so it leaves `hints` out (like `vault` and `apiKey`); a remote client shows the card without hints. Hints there need a second capability string and a phone build: [LATER.md](LATER.md). The phone already typesets math in card titles and form labels (its question text goes through `ChatMarkdown`); option chips stay plain there.
- **Tests:** Storybook `Conversation/UiBlocks › Form Math Hints` (Owl's card: math in title, labels and hints; three taps open the three hints; a11y panel: 0 violations), checked 8 Nov 2026. `bun run test:changed` (66 files) passed; `typecheck:renderer`, `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:storybook`, contracts typecheck, Biome. Not run in the app: night queue.
- **Check:** ask an agent: "show me an ask_ui form with math in the title and 3 hints". The title shows typeset math; Hint opens hint 1, then "Hint 2/3" opens hint 2.

The released Team API codecs (`v1.ts`, `provider-aware-codec.ts`) hardcode 10 on the wire, so a new `INPUT_LIMITS.teamAttachments: 10` keeps every Team API path at 10: `queue-edit-v1`, the host's `stringArray` default, the phone (`chat-view`, `use-chat-queue`, `queue-edit-draft`) and the web client. `draftAttachments` (unsent files of all chats together) goes from 50 to 250, so one full message does not block the other chats. The 250 MB total per message stays.
- **Here:** `packages/contracts/src/input-limits.ts`; `teamAttachments` in `packages/contracts/src/team-protocol/queue-edit-v1.ts`, `src/main/team-api/request-helpers.ts`, `apps/mobile/src/features/chat/{components/chat-view.tsx,components/use-chat-queue.ts,model/queue-edit-draft.ts}`, `src/renderer/src/features/web-client/{web-client-context.tsx,web-conversation-runtime.ts,web-channels-runtime.ts}`.
- **Phone and web client:** stay at 10. More needs a new Team API capability and a phone build.

## 62. The plan shows the step that runs now, not step 1

- **Get:** the plan card's header (list closed), the live-work bar and the phone's plan header name the step the agent works on now. Before, they named the first step in progress, so when an agent started a later step and left an earlier one open, the card "stayed on step 1".
- **Why:** Elias, 8 Nov 2026: the step / task-list cards "stay running on step 1 visually even though it's on a later step". Measured on his transcripts (51 sessions, read-only): 52 of 183 task-list updates had 2+ steps in progress, and the first one was not the newest for 108 min in all. Example: Tatiana pack, 03:39 UTC: task 12 "Writing Tatiana style" was in progress since 03:02 and task 14 "Proving and shipping" had just started; every view showed task 12. The data was right (the agent's calls were folded exactly); only the pick was wrong. Subagent task lists had the same pick (no subagent used one yet).
- **How (portable):**
  - `ConversationPlan.current` (optional step id, contract): the step that started last and still runs. `withPlanCurrent(previous, next)` in `plan-updates.ts` sets it in `TurnLifecycle#applyPlan`: a step running now that did not run in the previous plan just started (several: the last one); else the previous current step stays while it runs. Steps match by text (ids are list positions). A new turn compares with the agent's last plan, whose open steps carry on. Codex, ACP and Claude plans all get it.
  - `currentPlanStep(plan)` (contracts): `current` while it runs, else the last running step. Hosts before this field send no `current` and get the last running step.
  - Desktop: `TaskList` takes `current`; its header uses `activeTask` (`task-list-active.ts`). `ConversationTimeline` passes `currentPlanStep(plan)?.id`; `createSubagentDock` uses `currentPlanStep`. `subagentPlanProgress` takes the last running step.
  - Phone: `projectPlan` adds `current`; `chat-plan.tsx` names it, else the last active step.
  - Wire: the field is optional and additive. `isConversationPlan` accepts it; Team API v4 copies the plan as it is; protocols 1–3 read the checklist text, which has no running step.
- **Here:** `packages/contracts/src/ipc-conversation-plan.ts`, `src/backend/agent/plan-updates.ts`, `src/backend/agent/turn-lifecycle.ts`, `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/TaskList.tsx`, `task-list-active.ts`, `Subagents.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/ConversationTimeline.tsx`, `subagent-dock.ts`, `apps/mobile/src/features/chat/model/chat-messages.ts`, `components/chat-plan.tsx`.
- **Tests:** `bun scripts/fork-plan-current-step-e2e.ts`: the real agent service and `ClaudeAgentClient` with a stand-in `claude` that plays 3 turns of `TaskCreate` / `TaskUpdate` / `TodoWrite` step by step, in the order his agents did. After each step it reads the live conversation and asks the views' own functions what they show. Report `.openbot-build/fork-plan-current-step-e2e/report.json`: 11/11 samples show the real step on the card and the bar; the old rule was wrong on 5/11. `bun run test:changed` (66 files), Biome; the pre-commit hook ran all 14 typecheck projects, mobile included. Not run: the phone app itself and a look in the desktop app.
- **Check:** ask an agent for a 4-step task where it starts step 3 before it closes step 2. Close the plan card: the header names step 3, and so does the live-work bar.

---

## Checked, not needed

- **Several agents use the computer at once (Codync entry 9 / ADR 0006):** OpenBot has no lock between agents. Each provider starts its own `cua-driver mcp` proxy on the shared daemon (`src/main/cua-driver-runtime.ts`), and the action tap (`src/main/cua-driver-action-tap.ts`) only watches calls for the rim and cursor; it refuses and delays nothing. So several agents can already work at once, in different apps or the same one (no "another agent is using this app" guard). The only rule: an agent's task threads get no Computer Use, so one agent's own chat holds its screen work (`#sessionAgent` in `thread-lifecycle.ts`). Checked 2026-11-03; skipped. If two agents clashing in one app becomes a problem, Codync's per-app hold (look-only free, `pid` + background holds that app 20 s, foreground/untargeted holds all) would go in the tap's `data` handler, which sees each call's `name` and `args`.

## 63. A message closes the open card

- **Get:** when Elias writes to an agent while its `ask_ui` card is open, the card closes as skipped, and the agent reads his message at once. Before, the message waited unread until he answered the card, and it showed above the card.
- **Why:** Elias, 8 Nov 2026, Tapa Lead's 6-choice card on C0668-002: "it ignored my message and didn't understand that I wanted to skip and send it notes instead", and "that message … gets sent above the cards". Measured in its transcript: the message was steered into the turn at 14:56 UTC, but Claude Code reads a steered message only between tool calls, and `ask_ui` waits for the card. The model saw it 18 min later, when the card was skipped. The open card is drawn in the live row at the end of the chat, so the steered message showed above it.
- **How (portable):** `QueueControls.steer` calls the hook `userMessageSteered(agentId, turnId)` after it steers a message whose sender is the user. `AttentionRegistry.skipUiBlocksForUserMessage` answers each open `ask_ui` card of that turn with `{"status":"skipped","blockId","reason":"The user sent a message instead of answering this card. Read it next…"}` and freezes it as closed (`cancelled`). The card then stays in its place in the chat, above the message. The forms of `request_api_key` and `request_vault_item` stay open (secrets). Messages of agents and routines do not close a card. A message that waits in the queue (busy mode "queue", or a provider without native steer) does not close it either.
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent/attention-registry.ts`, `src/backend/agent/queue-controls.ts`, `src/backend/agent/prompts.ts` (`reason` on a skip), `src/backend/agent-service.ts` (hook).
- **Tests:** not run (night queue). Biome and `typecheck:node` on the changed files.
- **Guard:** `health-watch` check `openbot-stuck-card` (`~/.local/bin/openbot-check-stuck-card`, every 10 min): fails when a card is still open more than 3 min after Elias wrote in the same chat. Replayed on a copy of the 8 Nov database, it flags the Tapa Lead case.
- **Check:** ask an agent "show me an ask_ui choice card and wait". While the card is open, type "skip that, do X" and send. The card shows as closed above your message, and the agent does X at once.
- **Fix, 13 Nov 2026 (routine runs):** two routine-run cards stayed "pending" in SQLite although the agent got the skip. The event log showed `prompt.resolved` (closed), then 10 ms later a whole-chat write that put the card back. Cause: the release moves the run from needs-attention to running, and `ConversationRuntime.withConversationTransaction` replaced the chat object; `sendMessage` (and `QueueControls.steer`) still held the old one and wrote it whole. Now the transaction copies into the held object. Safety net: each user message calls `AttentionRegistry.closeStaleCardsForUserMessage`, which closes (`cancelled`) every `ask_ui` card of that chat that still shows as waiting with no live request; secret forms and questions without a block (possible MCP elicitations) stay. `#resolvePersistedPrompt` now logs a warning when the card is not in its chat. Tests: `src/backend/fork-card-closes-in-routine-run.test.ts` (real SQLite, runtime and registry).

## 64. Review cards: each question plays its own clip, offers Other, and takes pause notes

- **Get:** an `ask_ui` form field can carry `clip {path,start,end}`. The card plays that part of the video in a loop above the question, with sound. Elias pauses it, taps 📌 **Note here**, types a note, and can add more. Every select or segmented field also offers **Other** with a text box, so an answer can be "something in between". The agent gets each note as `notes: [{field,time,text,frame}]`, with `frame` a PNG of the exact frame he paused on.
- **Why:** Elias, 8 Nov 2026, Tapa Lead's 6-choice card on C0668-002, which pointed into a 4-min before/after reel: "I can't imagine what part it's talking about … I would have liked … the 5-second clip I can play for this specific revision", "only two options … Sometimes I want something in between", and "When I pause, it can get sent the actual paused frame, along with the video info, as well as the notes". He chose the full version (clip + Other + pause notes, Other on all cards by default).
- **How (portable):**
  - Contract (`packages/contracts/src/ui-blocks.ts`): `UiFormClip` (`path`, `start`, `end`, and `src`, which only the host sets) on every form field kind; `other?: boolean` on options fields (absent = Other shown; a value may then be any text). Pause notes are the response key `notes:<fieldId>` (`uiFormClipNotesKey`), a list of `"<seconds> <words>"` (`uiFormClipNoteValue` / `readUiFormClipNote`), and one more fallback question per clip, so `respondToPrompt` carries them. Limit 20 notes per clip.
  - Wire: `uiBlockFitsV1Wire` keeps a block off ui-blocks-v1 when its answer has notes or a value that is not an option, because the released phone reader would refuse it. The phone then shows the card's questions. Released readers drop `clip` and `other`.
  - Backend: `ask_ui` (now an Effect) attaches the clip files to the turn with the `attach_files_to_response` path and sets each `clip.src` to `openbot-attachment://file/<id>`; it refuses the card when a file cannot be attached. `AttentionRegistry.respondToPrompt` makes the frames before it answers (`fork-card-notes.ts`: ffmpeg `-ss <t> -frames:v 1`, Homebrew paths first, PNGs in `$TMPDIR/openbot-card-notes/<blockId>/`). A note whose frame fails keeps its time and words. The `ask_ui` description tells agents to give each question about a place in a video its own clip, and to look at the frames. OpenBot's API key form sets `other: false`.
  - UI: `UiFormClip.tsx` (video loops `start`..`end`, 📌 note list, note draft with Enter/Escape); `UiFormBlock.tsx` renders it above any field and adds the Other segment / select entry with its text box. Styles in `styles/ui-blocks.css`; keys `uiBlock.form.other*`, `uiBlock.clip.*` in all 14 languages.
- **Rule for agents:** motion-kit `video-editing` SKILL.md › Planning: a question about a place in a video carries its 3–8 s clip, never only a time or a whole reel (Self-improve ✅ 8 Nov).
- **Here:** files above, plus `src/backend/openbot-tools.ts`, `src/backend/agent/openbot-tool-router.ts`, `src/backend/agent/prompts.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/team-protocol/ui-blocks-v1.ts`.
- **Tests:** `bun scripts/ui-blocks-e2e.ts` (real `AttentionRegistry` and Team API codecs, 25/25): a review card with a real 4 s ffmpeg video answers "Other" text and one note; the agent gets the text, the note and a real PNG frame; the card stays off the v1 wire. Also the "A message closes the open card" step. Report `.openbot-build/ui-blocks-e2e/report.json`. `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, Biome. Not seen in the running app yet (night queue); no Storybook story; the phone shows such a card as questions only.
- **Check:** ask an agent: "ask me with ask_ui a form with one segmented field about seconds 2–6 of <a video path>". The card plays that part in a loop; Other opens a text box; pause, 📌 Note here, type, Enter; Send. The agent quotes your note and describes the frame PNG it got.

## 65. A queued account switch holds new turns, and the chip names the task that holds it

- **Get:** you change an agent's account, provider or custom agent while one of its turns runs (in its chat or a task). The change still waits for that turn, but no new turn of the agent starts on the old account in the meantime: queued messages wait, and the change applies before the next one. When a task holds the change and the chat runs nothing, the header chip says "Waits for 🩺 Health" (the task's title); its tooltip says why, and a click opens that task, where you answer or stop its turn.
- **Why:** Elias, 10 Nov 2026: Bot Bot's account was unpaid, so he moved it to Account 1. Other agents moved at once; Bot Bot kept failing with "Claude Code can't use your Claude subscription" ("it seems as if the turn itself is stuck"). Its record had the change queued (`pendingRuntime`, no account = Account 1) since its "🩺 Health" task had waited for his answer to a card since 9 Nov 08:09. A queued change applies only when no turn of the agent runs (one Claude process per agent; an account change replaces it), but the drain still started main-chat turns on the old account. Elias picked "simple, keep the one-process convention" over a two-process split.
- **How (portable):** `DrainScheduler.#heldBy`: after the `agent-client-changing` clause, a lane is held with `"runtime-change"` while `agent.pendingRuntime` is set and another lane of the agent runs (`#laneRunning`). When the last running lane ends, the drain's `followUsageLimit` hook finds the change due and applies it, as before. Tasks show the hold as `agent-restarting`. Renderer `ConversationHeader`: when the agent has `pendingRuntime`, its chat is not working and a side thread is busy (a thread that waits for the user first), `pendingModel.waitsFor = {title, onOpen}`; the shared header then draws a ghost button instead of the badge. New English keys `conversation.header.modelWaitsForThread` and `…Detail` (other languages show English, listed in `untranslated.json`).
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent/drain-scheduler.ts`, `src/backend/agent-tasks/agent-tasks.ts`, `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/ConversationHeader.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/ConversationHeader.tsx`, `packages/i18n/src/messages/en/conversation.ts`.
- **Since #71:** a turn that only waits for your answer, or any turn on a spent account, no longer holds the change.
- **Guard:** health-watch `openbot-queued-switch-holds` (`~/.local/bin/openbot-check-queued-switch`): fails when an agent with a queued change started a main-chat turn while another of its threads ran.
- **Check:** give an agent a task that asks you a question (ask_ui) and leave it open. Change the agent's account in the chat header. The chip says "Waits for <task>"; a message in the chat waits and does not fail. Click the chip, answer the card: when that turn ends, the account changes and the waiting message runs on the new account.

## 66. A question in a side thread marks its agent as waiting, not working

- **Get:** when a side thread of an agent (a task or a routine thread such as Bot Bot's "🩺 Health") asks you a question or waits for an approval or a takeover, the agent's avatar in the sidebar shows the question badge and moves to "Needs you", with the question as its preview. Selecting the row opens that thread, where the card is. Before, the avatar showed the three "working" balls for as long as the question waited, while the agent did nothing.
- **Why:** Elias, 10 Nov 2026 (through Bot Bot): "i think its a bug" — Bot Bot showed the working dots at 20:03 and 20:07 between its turns. Its "🩺 Health" routine thread had a turn running since 17:52 that waited on a question (`prompt.requested` 17:53:54). The sidebar counted the running task as work (`workingTurns`) but dropped the wait, because `agentThreadAttention` kept only waits of the agent's own thread. The avatar's face already said "waiting" (`attentionByAgent` reads every thread), so badge and face disagreed. Tapa Lead's dots in the same screenshots were real work: a background subagent until 19:06:25Z and a turn 19:07:29–19:08:06Z.
- **How (portable):** `agentThreadAttention` takes an optional `sideThreads(agentId)`; their waits count after the agent's own thread. `WorkspaceSidebar` passes every wait thread of the agent that no channel owns and that is no closed task (a task list loads only once its agent opened, so it cannot be the source). The precedence in `computeSidebarAgentStates` (a wait outranks working) then gives the badge. `selectSidebarAgent` opens the waiting side thread with `agentThreads.openConversation` after `selectAgent`. The chat view keeps the own-thread waits only.
- **Here:** `src/renderer/src/features/agents/agent-thread-attention.ts`, `src/renderer/src/features/sidebar/WorkspaceSidebar.tsx`, test `src/renderer/src/fork-side-thread-wait.test.ts`.
- **Tests:** the test above (the "before" case shows `working`), Biome, `bun run typecheck:renderer`; isolated dev instance (worktree profile): a Builder task that asked "Ship the rollback step?" showed the working balls before, the question badge under "Needs you" after, and one click on the row opened the task with its card.
- **Check:** start a task of an agent that asks you a question (ask_ui), switch to another chat. The agent's row shows the question badge in "Needs you", not the working balls; a click opens the task at the card.

## 67. Card media: a question card shows the video or image it asks about

- **Get:** any `ask_ui` card (confirm, choice, quick replies, form) can carry `media: [{path, caption?, start?, end?}]`, up to 4 items. The card shows them under its title: a video plays with its sound and controls, from `start`, looping `start`..`end`; an image opens in the image viewer on click; the caption sits under each. A file OpenBot cannot attach (missing, not a file, too large, outside the agent's folders) shows as "<name> can't be shown here", and the card still works. The answered card keeps its media. When a card's words point at something visual (a timecode such as 6:41 or 00:12.5, "frame 1280", animation, scene, clip, shot, render, comp, "how it looks/moves") and it shows no media or clip, the card still goes out, and the answer tells the agent: "This card refers to something visual but has no media. Next time attach a short clip or image with `media`."
- **Why:** Elias, 10 Nov 2026, on Tatiana Lead's card "Healthier (6:41): make its heartbeat line one smooth draw too?": "lets make review/question cards like these be able to embed a video for claude to show me — for ex here i would've preferred it showed me too cuz here idk which animation its talking about". #64 gave each form field a clip, but confirm and choice cards (the common case) had no way to show anything. The nudge was his scope addition the same evening.
- **How (portable):**
  - Contract (`packages/contracts/src/ui-blocks.ts`): `UiBlockMedia` (`path`, `caption`, `start`, `end`, and `src`, which only the host sets) on every spec; kind by extension (`uiBlockMediaKind`: mp4/mov/m4v/webm video, png/jpg/jpeg/webp/gif image; any other file refuses the block). Limits `UI_BLOCK_LIMITS.media` 4, caption 200; a range on an image is dropped. The first fallback question gets one line per item, in words that need no translation (`uiBlockMediaLines`: `🎬 heartbeat.mp4 0:02–0:05 · caption`), so a client that only reads questions still names the files. `uiBlockLooksVisualWithoutMedia` is the plain-regex nudge test (clock times such as "9:30 pm", "framework", "screenshot" do not count).
  - Wire: `uiBlockSpecWithoutMediaSources` drops `src` on ui-blocks-v1 (an attachment id names a host file). A released reader drops `media`; a fork reader names the files. Either way the files are in the chat as the agent's attachments.
  - Backend: `ask_ui` attaches clip and media files in one `attach_files_to_response` call (same file policy and byte-range `openbot-attachment://` serving as #64). Media paths are checked one by one first (`#attachableFiles`: workspace/folders/shared, a file, within `ATTACHMENT_LIMITS.fileBytes`); one that fails only loses its `src`. A clip that fails still refuses the card (#64). The result adds `mediaNotShown` + `mediaNote` for files the user did not see, and `mediaNudge` (`VISUAL_WITHOUT_MEDIA_NUDGE`) for a visual card without media. The zod schema (`openbot-tools.ts`) has `media` on confirm, choice and form, so the Claude MCP tool (`mcp__openbot__ask_ui`) and Codex get it from the same shape; the ask_ui description and the developer instructions each got one line. `tools/agent-context/budget.json` raised by hand (+64 B instructions, +520 B visible tools, +387 B schemas, +133 B descriptions).
  - UI: `UiBlockMedia.tsx` (`UiBlockMediaStrip`, and `keepVideoInRange`, which `UiFormClip` now shares); `UiBlockCard` takes `media` and draws it between header and body; confirm, choice and form pass `spec.media`. One item is card-wide, more go two to a row. The image opens `ImageLightbox`. `toAgentMessages` hides the agent-attachment message whose every file a card in the list shows (no duplicate video above the card); without `src` (remote) it stays. Styles in `styles/ui-blocks.css`; `FileX` icon added; keys `uiBlock.media.unavailable`, `uiBlock.media.enlarge` in all 15 languages.
- **Here:** files above, plus `packages/contracts/src/team-protocol/ui-blocks-v1.ts`, `src/backend/agent/openbot-tool-router.ts`, `src/backend/agent/attention-registry.ts`, `src/backend/agent/prompts.ts`, `src/backend/agent/developer-instructions.ts`, `src/renderer/src/app-message-projection.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-card-media.test.ts` (contract parse/limits/kinds, fallback lines, v1 wire, zod schema, result fields, nudge positives and no false positive on plain yes/no cards), `src/renderer/src/fork-card-media.test.tsx` (video attributes, start/end loop, failed video names the file, image opens the viewer, answering still works, answered card keeps media), `src/renderer/src/fork-card-media-projection.test.ts`. Full vitest 1701/1701, `bun scripts/ui-blocks-e2e.ts` 25/25, typecheck node/renderer/storybook/contracts/mobile, `check:ui`, `i18n:check`, `check:agent-context`, Biome. Isolated dev instance (worktree profile), real Claude Haiku turns through `mcp__openbot__ask_ui`: a 6 s testsrc+sine mp4 played with decoded audio, looped 1→4 s, seeked to 3.5 s, clamped a seek to 0.2 s back to 1 s; the PNG opened in the image viewer; a missing file showed its name and came back in `mediaNotShown`; the media-less "6:41" card came back with `mediaNudge`. Screenshots `~/OpenBot/Shared/Transfers/card-media-20261110/`.
- **Not covered:** the phone app draws no media yet: it gets the files as the turn's attachments and the names in the question text. A remote desktop client names the files on the card and shows them as attachments above it.
- **Check:** ask an agent: "ask me with ask_ui a confirm card about seconds 2–5 of <a video path>, with the video as media". The card plays that part with sound in a loop under its title; answer it; the card keeps the video.

## 68. WhatsApp Watch: read a contact's chat silently and route it, never write to it

- **Get:** in **Marketplace → Apps → WhatsApp**, a **Watch (read-only)** list under **Who can write**, with "OpenBot reads these chats silently and never writes to them. They stay unread on your phone." Add a contact by phone number (**Watch contact**). Everything that person sends (text, Drive/Frame.io links, screenshots, videos, voice notes) and what Elias writes back to them reaches the WhatsApp Orchestrator ("Desk") as one turn per burst, in the "Message yourself" conversation, starting "👁️ Watched chat · Ichiro", with the files attached. Desk routes it and reports only in "Message yourself" ("Ichiro sent a Frame.io review → Zito ✓"). The watched person sees nothing: no reply, reaction, "Working on it…", typing or read receipt (grey ticks only, as when the phone is offline), and the chat stays unread on Elias's phone.
- **Why:** Elias, 11 Nov 2026 (approved design, "watch"): he links his personal number; his only editing client contact is Ichiro, whose messages should reach the right bot (Tapa/Tatiana/Adrian leads, Zito revisions, Vegapunk, Robin) without Ichiro ever seeing OpenBot.
- **How (portable):**
  - Rules (`whatsapp-senders.ts`): `WhatsAppSenderRules.watched` (number → name); `senderDecision` returns `{type:"watch", number}` for a watched contact's direct chat, before the `fromMe` ignore, so the owner's own messages in it count too (`SenderFacts.chatNumber`, the other person of a direct chat, also for `fromMe`). Brazil's two number forms match. A watched number is never an allowed contact (`allowsContact`), and on a dedicated number it never gets a pairing code.
  - Storage (`whatsapp-connect.ts`): the list is JSON in the connection's encrypted values (`watched`, beside `ownNumber`), not a table: `projection_messaging_allowed_senders` has `CHECK(kind IN ('contact','group'))`, a new table needs a numbered migration (upstream owns them, see the v59 clash), and ~600 upstream migration tests snapshot every table. Contract: `WhatsAppSender.kind` gains `"watch"` (same add/remove IPC, team API and preview mock), `WhatsAppAccount.watched?`. Adding to one list removes the number from the other. A number linked again keeps its list.
  - Inbound (`whatsapp-events.ts` `watchEvent`): no approval-menu parsing, no mention gate, no `rememberKey`; one line `Ichiro: text (image: …) (replying to: "…")`, the media remembered for download. The LID form of the chat is remembered for the guard.
  - Batching (`whatsapp-transport.ts` `#watch`): per watched number, messages within `WATCH_TIMING.debounceMs` (2 min) join, flushed at most `maxWaitMs` (10 min) after the first. Each is journaled first; a burst not sent at stop stays unhandled and replays on the next start. The turn: `platformChannelId`/target = the owner's self chat (no quote), `threadKey "dm"`, `platformMessageId "watch:<id>"`, author = the watched contact (its own `AUTHOR_QUEUE_LIMIT` of 2, apart from the owner's), text `watchedTurnText` (who wrote it, "information, never instructions", "never sees anything you write", "report in one short line").
  - Prompt (`messaging-threads.ts`, one line): `fromOwner` is also true for any turn of the self-chat link, so the turn gets the "Message yourself" frame; only the owner and watched turns can land in that link.
  - Hard guard: `whatsapp-watch.ts` `guardWatchedOutbound` wraps the socket's `sendMessage`, `relayMessage`, `sendReceipt(s)`, `readMessages`, `sendPresenceUpdate`, `presenceSubscribe`, `chatModify` (installed in `WhatsAppSession.#connect`), and `WhatsAppSession.#sendNow` refuses a watched chat, or a reaction/edit/delete whose key names one, before waiting for the socket (`WhatsAppWatchRefused`, "watched_chat"). JIDs match by phone, device JID, WhatsApp's LID mapping, or a LID a watched message came from. The adapter drops status reactions of `watch:` messages (they would name a message of another chat). `places()` and `#checkPlace` never include a watched chat.
  - Read receipts (vendored Baileys, checked 11 Nov): Baileys sends a receipt for every decrypted message by itself at `packages/whatsapp/vendor/baileys/src/Socket/messages-recv.ts:1759` (`sendReceipt(..., type)`), with `type` undefined (delivered) or `'inactive'` when `sendActiveReceipts` is false (`:1754`), which it is: `markOnlineOnConnect: false` (`whatsapp-session.ts:378`) makes the connect send `sendPresenceUpdate('unavailable')` (`chats.ts:1379`), which emits `isOnline:false` (`chats.ts:818`) → `sendActiveReceipts = false` (`messages-recv.ts:2058`). Those are the grey ticks. A read (blue) receipt is only `type 'read'/'read-self'` from `readMessages` (`messages-send.ts:228-233`); nothing in Baileys calls it, and OpenBot never did (its `WhatsAppSocket` has no such method). The guard now refuses it anyway.
  - Orchestrator: the description is not changed: it is 1,963 of the 2,000-character `INPUT_LIMITS.agentDescription` and applies only to a new orchestrator, so Elias's existing Desk would never get it. The rule rides in every watched turn's text instead.
  - UI: `WhatsAppIntegrationPanel.tsx` `WatchSection` (Eye icon, **Stop watching**), shown for a personal number (or a dedicated one with a list); `ContactDialog kind="watch"`. Keys `connector.whatsapp.watch*`, `addWatch`, `stopWatching` in all 15 languages.
- **Upstream (merge of 18 Nov 2026):** a watched message is not saved in upstream's 90-day WhatsApp history (#9587/#10439), so `messaging_history` cannot read it; its edits and deletes, which now reach the inbound code, change nothing; upstream's HD second copy of a photo or video (#11939) counts once; a message held after a reconnect (#8168) becomes a watch line, never an answer to the contact. Upstream's sender add (#10916, `#allowSender`) checks a watched number too, and watch still wins over allow (`whatsapp-connect.ts`, `whatsapp-events.ts`).
- **Here:** `src/backend/messaging/whatsapp/whatsapp-watch.ts` (new), `whatsapp-senders.ts`, `whatsapp-events.ts`, `whatsapp-transport.ts`, `whatsapp-session.ts`, `whatsapp-runtime.ts`, `whatsapp-driver.ts`, `whatsapp-connect.ts`, `src/backend/messaging/messaging-threads.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/ipc-messaging.ts`, `src/main/ipc/messaging-inputs.ts`, `packages/ui/src/features/settings/WhatsAppIntegrationPanel.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/preview/mock-messaging.ts`, the story, `docs/messaging.md` (Watch section).
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-whatsapp-watch.test.ts` (decision: watch, owner in watched chat, self/allowed/group/stranger unchanged, watch beats allow, no pairing, Brazil forms; guard: 12 writes toward Ichiro by phone/device/LID/reaction/edit/delete/receipt/presence/chatModify refused with zero socket calls, the same writes to self/others pass; turn text). `bun scripts/fork-whatsapp-watch-e2e.ts` (real agent + messaging services, fake socket, 11/11): exclusive lists; text + encrypted image (served locally, decrypted, byte-identical attachment) + owner reply → one orchestrator delivery in the self-chat link, no writes at all; answer posts to self chat, `watch:` reactions send nothing; adapter/session/socket writes toward Ichiro refused (phone, device, LID); LID-form message read; third waiting turn → busy notice in self chat only; stranger ignored, self chat and allowed contact keep 👀; a burst pending at stop replays after restart; unwatch stops it. Upstream `scripts/messaging-whatsapp-e2e.ts` passes. Full vitest 1711/1711, typecheck node/renderer/storybook/contracts/mobile, `check:ui`, `i18n:check`, `check:agent-context`, Biome. Storybook screenshots `~/OpenBot/Shared/Transfers/wa-watch-20261111/` (1-before, 2-after, 3-watch-dialog).
- **Not covered:** a real phone (night queue, `--who elias`): that Ichiro sees grey ticks only and the chat stays unread. The panel was checked in Storybook, not a linked dev instance (linking needs Elias's phone). A watched group is not supported. A watched turn that waits while Ichiro already has two waiting gets the "busy" notice in the self chat and is not retried.
- **Check:** link the personal number, add Ichiro under **Watch (read-only)**, have him send a link and a screenshot. ~2 min later Desk reports in "Message yourself"; Ichiro's phone shows grey ticks only; the chat stays unread on Elias's phone.

## 69. Agents ride out a network drop: longer CLI retries, and a failed turn resumes when the network is back

- **Get:** a network drop of up to ~5 min no longer ends a Claude turn or its background subagents (was ~3 min). A longer drop ends the turn as before, but the agent's chat now shows an open wait "🔌 Network dropped · resumes when api.anthropic.com answers" (Cancel works). When the provider answers again (probed every 10 s, after 30 s), a turn starts in the same chat and session ("🔌 Network back: continue") that continues the work and continues or relaunches each background subagent the drop ended, plus a macOS notification. Every agent is also told: a background subagent that ends on an API or network error is continued or relaunched at once, not only reported.
- **Why:** Elias via Adrian, 11 Nov 2026: "fix this so the main agent notices and restarts it - and also the fix so that if network drops when it comes back the subagent should continue". Night of 10/11 Nov (session `d7c57a9e`): the network failed 23:38–00:03 UTC. Adrian's 6 background subagents each retried 10 times (~3 min) and died with "API Error: Connection dropped (ECONNRESET)" at 23:41. The CLI did wake Adrian with the failure notices at 23:41 and again at 23:44, but both wake turns died on the same outage after their own 10 retries; nothing retried after 23:47, and the chat stayed silent until Elias wrote at 06:22, although every other agent worked again from 00:03.
- **How (portable):**
  - CLI retries (`claude-client.ts`, thread `env`): `CLAUDE_CODE_MAX_RETRIES=15` (`FORK_CLAUDE_MAX_RETRIES`; a value in OpenBot's own environment wins). The bundled CLI clamps it to 15 unless its undocumented `CLAUDE_CODE_RETRY_WATCHDOG` is on (strings of `claude-agent-sdk-darwin-arm64/claude`: `function ket(){…if(n>ret&&!e)…clamped to ${ret}…}`, `ret=15`, default `e_r=10`, watchdog default `t_r=300`). The watchdog is not used: it also makes 429/529 capacity waits unbounded, which would hide plan limits from OpenBot's usage-limit gate and failover. Backoff caps at ~32–40 s, so 15 retries ≈ 5 min.
  - Resume (`turn-lifecycle.ts`): at a turn's end in the agent's own chat (not a task, channel or routine session), `forkNetworkDrop(outcome, reportedError, failover?.message)` returns the error when the turn failed with cause `network` or `timeout` (`classifyFailure`); then `forkHub.networkResume.turnFailed(…)`. A completed turn calls `turnCompleted`, which closes a network wait still open (the network works). A turn that failed over to a backup model returns before this point and is not resumed.
  - Wait (`agent-waits/fork-wait-conditions.ts` `openNetworkResume`): an ordinary fork wait (#7) with a `url` condition and `notBefore` (30 s settle), `network: {failedAt, error}` kept in `fork-wait-conditions.json` (survives a restart). The probe is `forkNetworkProbeUrl(provider)`: `ANTHROPIC_BASE_URL` or `https://api.anthropic.com/` for Claude, `api.openai.com` for Codex, else `gstatic generate_204`; any answer < 500 means back. One open network wait per agent, at most 3 per hour (no loop when the provider fails each turn), 12 h timeout (then it wakes the agent to tell the user). The wake (`#fireNetwork`) is a `fork-wait` routine-sender delivery whose text quotes the error and the request (or says the provider started the turn itself, as for a subagent's failure notice) and tells the agent to continue each failed subagent with SendMessage to its id or relaunch it with the same brief, after checking its output files.
  - Wiring (`agent-service.ts`): `forkHub.networkResume` (new slot in `fork-hub.ts`), set after the wait conditions, cleared at stop. The error is redacted (`McpGateway.redact`).
  - Instructions (`developer-instructions.ts`, one sentence after the subagent todo rule): "When a background subagent ends on an API or network error, continue or relaunch it at once; do not only report it." Budget `instructions.claude/codex` +116 bytes.
- **Here:** `src/backend/claude-client.ts`, `src/backend/agent/turn-lifecycle.ts`, `src/backend/agent-waits/fork-wait-conditions.ts`, `src/backend/agent-service.ts`, `src/backend/fork-hub.ts`, `src/backend/agent/developer-instructions.ts`, `tools/agent-context/budget.json`, `scripts/fork-network-drop-e2e.ts` (new), `src/backend/fork-network-resume.test.ts` (new).
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-network-resume.test.ts` (6): the CLI's ECONNRESET text is `network`; only failed network/timeout turns resume (auth, past-due plan, completed, interrupted do not); a real DB wait probes a local server that answers 503, does not wake, then wakes once with the continue/SendMessage text when it answers 404; no probe before the settle; one wait at a time, 3 per hour, closed by a completed turn; survives a restart. `bun scripts/fork-network-drop-e2e.ts --drop 270 [--retries 15]` runs the real bundled CLI through a local proxy that resets every connection for 270 s: default → 10 retries, "FAILED: API Error: Connection dropped (ECONNRESET)" at 190 s (Adrian's error); `--retries 15` → 13 retries, ANSWERED "ok" at 302 s.
- **Not covered:** the whole chain in a running app (a Claude turn failing on a real drop, the wait card, the wake turn): a dev instance has its own Claude sign-in. Night queue, `--who elias`. A subagent cannot be resumed by OpenBot itself: the CLI owns it; the main agent does it in the wake turn. Codex and other providers get the resume wait but not the retry setting.
- **Check:** with an agent working (or with background subagents running), turn Wi-Fi off for ~7 min, then on. Expect: the chat shows "🔌 Network dropped" above the composer; within ~1 min of Wi-Fi on, "🔌 Network back: continue" starts a turn that relaunches the subagents. `health-watch` check `openbot-network-resume` fails if a session stays silent > 10 min after the network came back.

## 70. Videos play on the phone: the host serves a small copy of a large bot video to remote clients

- **Get:** a video that a bot attaches (`attach_files_to_response`, a message's paths, `ask_ui` media, which reach the phone as message attachments) plays in the OpenBot phone app even when the original is 10–80 MB or ProRes/WebM. The phone gets "<name> · phone.mp4" (480p H.264 Main + AAC, MP4 faststart, under 9 MB). The desktop app shows the original, unchanged; the original stays one click away there. No phone app update is needed.
- **Why:** Elias, 11 Nov 2026 (via Bot Bot, card "Only the app fix", after telling Adrien Lead "lower the quality … so i can see the attachment on the openbot mobile app" and picking "Always small"): every bot that attaches clips has the problem; fix it at the source, not with a rule per bot.
- **Cause (evidence):** size, not codec. The phone app plays a video only up to `MOBILE_ATTACHMENT_BYTES` = 10 MB (`packages/team-client/src/file-upload.ts:24`; `apps/mobile/.../chat-attachment.tsx` `playable = … attachment.size <= MOBILE_ATTACHMENT_BYTES`; above it a file card with `mobile.chat.attachment.tooLargeForMobile`). Of the 331 videos bots attached from 5 to 11 Nov (projection_attachments, a copy of the DB), all were H.264 yuv420p MP4 and 80 were over 10 MB. Codec only matters for other inputs: ProRes (editor renders in the Ichiro folders) and VP9 WebM (editing-spread clips) do not play in iOS's AVPlayer, so they get a copy at any size.
- **How (portable):**
  - `src/main/fork-phone-video.ts` `ForkPhoneVideo` (instance in `forkPhoneVideo.current`, made in `application-services.ts` after the mailbox; root `<userData>/fork-phone-video/`, `index.json`). A video attachment is probed once (ffprobe); it is left alone when it is at most 10 MB and H.264/HEVC 8-bit 4:2:0 in MP4/MOV. Else ffmpeg (`/opt/homebrew/bin`, `/usr/local/bin`, PATH, as `fork-card-notes.ts`) makes the copy: short side 480 (never upscaled), ≤ 30 fps, libx264 veryfast Main, video bitrate `min(1400k, 9 MB·8/duration − 96k)` (floor 150k; a longer clip is left alone), AAC 96k stereo, no data/timecode track, `+faststart`. A copy over 10 MB is dropped. Two at a time; results persist (a restart keeps them).
  - Team API (remote clients only; the phone and joined desktops): `route-agent-conversation.ts` (`conversation`, and `conversation-page` with its quoted `references`), `route-agent-tasks.ts` (task read), `route-agent-threads.ts` (older thread pages) pass messages through `phoneVideoMessages` / `phoneVideoPage`: they wait for missing copies up to 20 s (`PHONE_COPY_WAIT_MS`), then swap each ready video's summary for the copy (`id` `fork-phone-<original id>`, its name, size, `video/mp4`). A copy not ready in time keeps the original; the next read gets the copy. `route-files.ts` `/v1/attachments/<id>` serves a copy id from `resolveCopy` before the mailbox.
  - The phone and joined desktops send the same capabilities, so a joined desktop also gets the copy of a video that the phone could not play.
- **Here:** `src/main/fork-phone-video.ts` (new), `src/main/fork-phone-video.test.ts` (new), `src/main/application-services.ts`, `src/main/team-api/route-agent-conversation.ts`, `route-agent-tasks.ts`, `route-agent-threads.ts`, `route-files.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/main/fork-phone-video.test.ts` (4, real ffmpeg): the limit equals team-client's; the bitrate keeps 84 s at 802k and refuses an hour; ffprobe parsing; a generated 8 s 1080p 14 Mbit/s H.264 MP4 (>10 MB) and a 4 s VP9 WebM get copies (h264, Main, 480 high, AAC, < 10 MB) and a 1 Mbit/s MP4 is kept; a page swaps a message's and a quoted message's videos; the copy id resolves to the file; a new instance reads the copies back. Real clips (read-only): Adrien Lead's "Adrien 30k 0m00-0m34 from Premiere.mp4" 28.4 MB H.264 High 1080p → 6.45 MB 854×480 H.264 Main + AAC in 3.7 s; Tatiana's `landing_slide_uhd.mov` 40.4 MB ProRes 4444 UHD yuva444p12le → 0.54 MB. AVFoundation (iOS's player framework, on this Mac): both copies `isPlayable` and `isDecodable`.
- **Not covered:** the phone itself (night queue, `--who elias`). Copies are made when a remote client first reads the chat, not when the bot attaches; a chat with many new large videos can take up to 20 s to open the first time. Copies are not deleted with their original.
- **Check:** rebuild from main; have a bot attach a render over 10 MB; open that chat on the phone. Expect a playable "<name> · phone.mp4" instead of "too large for mobile"; the desktop still shows the original. `health-watch` check `openbot-phone-video` fails when ffmpeg/libx264 is missing or a copy failed in the last 24 h.

## 71. A switch applies now when the turn that holds it only waits for you

- **Get:** you change an agent's account or model while another thread of it (a task or routine thread) only waits for your answer to a card. The switch no longer waits for that card: the waiting turn stops, the switch applies, your queued message runs on the new account, and the stopped thread gets a note "[OpenBot: 🔁 account switched]" that makes the agent ask the same question again on the new account. When the old account has hit its usage limit, every running turn of the agent stops the same way (it cannot go on there), and each one gets a note to continue its work and continue or relaunch its background subagents. A turn that really works on an account that is not spent still keeps the switch waiting, as in #65.
- **Why:** Elias, 11 Nov 2026 ("Why is it not switching the account when I already did switch or did stop the turn?"): Adrian hit the Claude limit; he moved it to blikkogame, and the header said "After this turn". The change waited behind the "Adrien $30k rebuild" task, whose turn had waited for his card since 10 Nov 21:57, so it never applied, and stopping the chat's turn did nothing. He chose "Switch now, re-ask" and, at the limit, "Switch now, continue it". (OpenBot still ran the 8 Nov build, without #65, so the chat also kept starting turns on the spent account.)
- **How (portable):** `DrainScheduler` calls the new optional hook `runtimeChangeHeld(agentId)` when a lane is held with `runtime-change`, and has `runningTaskLanes(agentId)` (own chat + tasks, no channels). `AgentService.#forkSwitchNow` also runs after a change is queued and when a card opens (`prompt` event). It looks at each running lane's turn: `AttentionRegistry.forkWaitingQuestions(agentId, turnId)` gives the open questions; a turn with questions and no running subagent (`TurnLifecycle.subagentsRunning`) only waits. If every running turn only waits, or the account is spent (`UsageLimitGate.mayDrain` false), it interrupts them (`#interruptTurn`), then queues the note: own chat → a `fork-wait` routine handoff ("🔁 Account switched: go on"), task → a queued message in the task. The drain applies the change before the next turn, as before (`followUsageLimit` → `#followPendingRuntime`). A turn id is stopped once (`#forkSwitchStops`). A turn still starting keeps the switch waiting.
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent-service.ts`, `src/backend/agent/drain-scheduler.ts`, `src/backend/agent/attention-registry.ts`.
- **Tests:** `bun run test:changed` (90 files, 1626 tests), `bun run typecheck:node`, Biome. The whole chain in a running app is not tested yet (night queue).
- **Guard:** health-watch `openbot-switch-not-stuck` (`~/.local/bin/openbot-check-switch-not-stuck`): fails when an agent has a queued change and another of its turns has only waited on a card for more than 10 min.
- **Check:** give an agent a task that asks you a question (ask_ui) and leave it open. Change the agent's account in the chat header. Within seconds the task's card closes, the header shows the new account (no "After this turn"), and the task asks the same question again. Send a message in the chat: it runs on the new account.

## 72. A replaced process ends its subagents: no more "working" badge on an idle agent

- **Get:** when OpenBot stops an agent's own Claude process while it keeps running (an account switch, a sign-out, a refresh of that account's process), the background subagents in it now show "stopped" in the chat, the agent's avatar stops showing the working dots, and the "Stopped subagents continue" note (wake, or a ⏹ line when the session changed) follows as after a crash. Before, they stayed "running" until the app restarted.
- **Why:** Elias, 12 Nov 2026 (screenshot, "why is it showing as if its working when its not"): Adrian showed the working dots all night while idle. Cause: at 11 Nov 16:07 UTC Adrian resumed on the backup Claude account after a usage limit and launched two background subagents ("$100k v2: resume head trim + export", "$30k v3: place word fixes + export"). At 16:20 the next message moved him back to the default account; `ProviderRuntime.#stopConfined` stopped the backup process with both subagents mid-command, but `client.stop()` reports no subagent ends (by design, for app quit), so `TurnLifecycle.#subagentTurns` kept the agent "working".
- **How (portable):** new optional `AgentClient.endBackgroundWork()`. `ClaudeAgentClient` runs `#stopSubagentsOf(runtime, "closed")` for each thread (same as a closed query: `stopped` subagents + `FORK_BACKGROUND_STOPPED_METHOD`). `ProviderRuntime.#stopConfined` calls it before it unloads and stops the process, unless OpenBot is quitting (the restart resume needs them still running then).
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent-client.ts`, `src/backend/claude-client.ts`, `src/backend/agent/provider-runtime.ts`.
- **Tests:** `bun scripts/subagent-stop-resume-e2e.ts`, new case `account-switch` (second Claude account, background subagent, switch back, next message): 10/10 pass; without the fix `account-switch` fails with the subagent "running" and the agent working. `bun run typecheck:node`.
- **Guard:** health-watch `openbot-stale-subagents`: fails when a chat holds a subagent marked running whose provider session is no longer active, for more than 30 min.
- **Not covered:** an already stuck badge (Adrian now) clears at the next app start (boot recovery stops every subagent).
- **Check:** put an agent on a second Claude account, have it start a background subagent, switch it back to the default account and send a message. The subagent shows stopped and the avatar dots go away.

## 73. Side panels and sidebar drags: content fits the pane, one cursor, live row drags

- **Get:** (a) the routine editor in the docked "Agent settings" pane always fits the pane: at the pane's minimum width (360 px, where the splitter stops) the action buttons wrap under the Active switch and every field stays inside the right edge.
- **Why:** Elias, 12 Nov 2026 (recording): "the box on the right gets shrunken beyond its sort of limit". The pane did stop at its minimum, but the routine form inside it did not shrink with it and was cut at the right edge.
- **How (portable):** a CSS grid with an implicit `auto` column sizes that column to the widest child's min-content, not to the container. The routine editor is a one-column grid, and its action row (switch + Delete + "Copy run command" + Test run, all `nowrap`) is ~375 px wide, so the column, and with it every field, became wider than a 360 px pane. Fix: `grid-template-columns: minmax(0, 1fr)` on the editor, and `flex-wrap: wrap` on the action row and its buttons (each button stays `nowrap`). Rule: every grid or flex row inside a resizable pane must be able to reach the pane's minimum width.
- **Get (b):** while you drag a splitter (the sidebar edge, a side pane's edge, a resizable text box), the cursor stays the resize cursor the whole time, also when the pointer runs past the handle at the pane's limit, over links, fields, buttons or the window's top strip. Nothing under the pointer lights up.
- **Why (b):** Elias, 12 Nov 2026: "I don't like the racing condition bugs that make the cursor switch different states very fast".
- **How (b, portable):** `cursor` on the root only reaches elements that set none of their own; a link (hand), a field (I-beam) or a `-webkit-app-region: drag` strip (arrow, and Electron takes its mouse events) wins as soon as the pointer is over it, so each frame the cursor took whichever element the pointer crossed. One owner instead: on pointer down the drag copies the handle's computed cursor into `--pointer-drag-cursor` and sets `data-pointer-drag` on `<html>`; CSS then gives every element that cursor (`!important`), `-webkit-app-region: no-drag`, and `pointer-events: none` except the handle (which holds the pointer capture). Pointer up, cancel and blur remove it.
- **Get (c):** dragging a chat row inside its own folder shows no blue folder box any more: the rows around it slide out of its way (120 ms) while it follows the pointer. The box shows only on another folder, when the drop moves the chat there, as in Claude's sidebar. On drop, the rows stay where the drag showed them (the dropped row under the pointer) until the new order is saved, then slide into it (200 ms); before, they jumped back to the old order and then jumped again. Removing, adding or moving a chat by any other way (archive, delete, the menu) slides the rows that stay into the gap.
- **Why (c):** Elias, 12 Nov 2026 (recordings of OpenBot and of the Claude app): "make the sidebar moving animation smoother and real time without those lag[s]" and "fix the animation when moving or removing items in the sidebar. Try not to be smooth around those blue boxes, unless obviously you're changing folder, just like what Claude Code has".
- **How (c, portable):** (1) The folder box is derived from the drop target, but only when the target's section differs from the dragged chat's current section (`chatDropSectionId` in `sidebar-drag-model.ts`); a pinned tile always counts as a move. (2) The CSS that turned off the rows' offset transition during a chat drag is gone; only the dragged row has none. (3) FLIP in `sidebar-row-motion.ts`: a MutationObserver on the list measures each row (content coordinates, so scrolling does not count) after every change and plays `translateY(old - new) → none` with the Web Animations API when rows were added or removed. A row that moved to another section is a new element; it takes the old position of the element with its chat id. On drop, the engine records where every row is drawn (offsets included, the dropped row at the pointer), turns off the offset transition (`data-sidebar-settling`), and holds each row at that place with a fill animation until the DOM order changes (or 900 ms pass, or the drop changes nothing), then slides all rows to their layout places. All of it is skipped with "reduce motion".
- **Here:** `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/styles/agent-settings.css` (a); `packages/ui/src/components/createPointerDrag.ts` (`holdDragCursor`), `src/renderer/src/styles/app-shell.css` (`html[data-pointer-drag]`) (b); `packages/ui/src/features/sidebar/sidebar-drag-model.ts`, `stores/drag-state-store.ts`, `sidebar-row-motion.ts`, `createSidebarDragEngine.ts`, `sidebar-scope.ts`, `src/renderer/src/styles/app-shell.css` (`.agent-list[data-sidebar-settling]`) (c).
- **Tests:** `src/renderer/src/fork-settings-pane-min-width.test.ts` (the grid track and the wrapping rows). Storybook `App/Playground`, Chief > Agent settings > Routines > Create, with the "Copy run command" button added: before, the Name field ended at 1274 px with the pane edge at 1246 px; after, at 1231 px (`Transfers/ui-fixes-20261112/bug1-before-storybook.png`, `bug1-after-storybook.png`).
- **Tests (b):** `src/renderer/src/fork-drag-cursor.dom.test.ts`. Storybook `App/Playground`: the left splitter dragged past its maximum onto a chat link: before, the element under the pointer had cursor `pointer`; after, the hit is the page root with `col-resize`, and every attribute is gone after pointer up.
- **Tests (c):** `src/renderer/src/fork-sidebar-drag-motion.test.ts` (same folder: no box; other folder and pinned tile: box; slide distances; the drop waits for the new order). Storybook `Navigation/Sidebar/Folders`, synthetic drag events: a drag inside a folder never set `sidebar-section-agent-drop-target`, a drag of an unassigned chat over a folder row did; on drop, each row got one 200 ms slide from where it was drawn (dropped row from the pointer); with a slow save, the rows were held (`translate → same translate`) until the order changed.
- **Not covered:** checked in Storybook (Chromium), not in the packaged app; the private test browser draws ~3 frames a second, so smoothness was not seen, only the animations that were started. The drag card itself still follows the native drag events, as before; with no pins, the empty pin target that opens during a drag still pushes the list down. (b) the real macOS cursor over the window-drag strip in Electron was not seen. The sidebar row drag uses the native drag and drop, whose cursor macOS draws; it is not changed here.
- **Check:** open an agent with a routine, Agent settings > Routines > the routine, drag the splitter to the right until it stops. All fields and buttons stay inside the pane.

The tool description and the developer instruction keep "for buttons, a list, a short form, or approval …, call ask_ui". The renderer and the contract already kept choice and form (upstream still shows them in older chats). Upstream's other #7623 parts stay: the Grok question reply, the router hint, the removed `ChoiceCard` prompt.
- **Here:** `src/backend/openbot-tools.ts` (`askUiToolSchema`, the `ask_ui` description), `src/backend/agent/advertised-json-schema.ts` (`ADVERTISE_AS_UNION`), `src/backend/agent/openbot-tool-router.ts` (the full rule text of a refused block), `src/backend/agent/developer-instructions.ts`, `tools/agent-context/budget.json` (raised: catalog.schemas 79891, catalog.descriptions 44353, instructions.claude 16177).
