  - **A numbered list in a `.docx` goes on counting.** The Word preview started a new `<ol>` at 1 each time a list was cut by bullet sub-items or by a paragraph, so "1. Open, 2. Fill, • Version, • Date, 3. Save" showed "1. Save". Now `parseWordDocument` (`word-document.ts`) counts the items of each list (`numId`) per level, starts a deeper level at 1 again after a shallower item, and sets `start` on the next `<ol>` (`WordFilePreview.tsx`); items of another level start a new block. Test: `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/WordListNumbering.test.tsx` (3 tests: 3 failed before, 3 pass after). Shots: `docx-numbering-before.png`, `docx-numbering-after.png`. Still flat: sub-levels are not indented, and `w:start`, `w:startOverride` and letter or roman formats are not read.
  - **The Markdown source view says when it is cut.** The panel shows the first 1 000 000 characters of a file. The rendered Markdown view and a text file said "Preview truncated after 1,000,000 characters.", but the Markdown source view (`<>`) cut the file with no note, because the note was shown only for `previewKind === "text"`. Now any source view shows it (`FilePreviewPanel.tsx`, one condition). Test: `FilePreviewText.test.tsx` › "a Markdown file longer than the preview limit" (failed before, passes after). Shots: `md-raw-truncated-before.png`, `md-raw-truncated-after.png`.
  - **A file with no extension gets the FILE badge.** `fileBadge` took the text after the last `.`, and for a name with no `.` that is the whole name, so `LICENSE` showed the badge "LICE" and `README` "READ" (message box chips; also the queue, the Library and the file list, which use the same function). Now it uses `attachmentFileExtension` and falls back to "FILE" (`AttachmentCards.tsx`). Test: `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/FileBadge.test.tsx` (2 tests: 1 failed before, 2 pass after). Shots: `file-badge-before.png`, `file-badge-after.png`.
- **Not covered:** a native `<select>` option (confirm select field, form select field) cannot hold typeset math, so it shows the TeX. A file chip that a Markdown reply makes from a code span (`MarkdownMessageText`, the Markdown core) and the image viewer title still show a name as written. The phone app (`apps/mobile`) was not changed. Not run in the packaged app.
- **Check:** ask an agent for an `ask_ui` choice with `meta: "$x^2$"` and a confirm card with a field value `$\frac{1}{2}$`: both show typeset math.

## 102. Chat Markdown and math: formulas, fences and copies that came out wrong

- **Get:** a reply's Markdown and math show and copy as written: formulas inside bold, italic and strikethrough, math and tilde fences, code in list items, and the text that Copy as CSV, a selection copy and the selection actions take.
- **Why:** bug hunt of the chat's Markdown and math renderer, 24 Nov 2026 (Elias asked for every bug in the renderer core, each with a failing test, a fix and proof). Shots: `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/chat-bughunt/core/`. The AGENTS.md rule "no unit tests" is set aside on request, as in #98.
- **Fixes:**
  - **Bold, italic or strikethrough closed inside a formula.** `*a $x*y$ b*`, `**The set $\{x \mid x^* > 0\}$ is open**`, `**uses $A^{**}$ twice**`, `*Ratio \(x*y\) per unit*` and `~~old $a~~b$ removed~~` showed broken emphasis and the formula as raw text with its `\{`, `*` and `~` eaten. marked's `emStrong` and `del` tokenizers look for the closing `*`, `_` or `~` in the rest of the line before the math extension has read the formula (`MarkdownMessageText.tsx`), so a `*` in `x^*` or `a*b` closed the emphasis. Now both tokenizers get a masked copy of the line in which the `*`, `_` and `~` inside each formula (found with the chat's own inline math rule, code spans skipped) are letters, as remark-math and GitHub read math before emphasis. Prices such as `*from $5 to $10*` stay text. `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/inline-math-mask.ts` keeps the mask of the last few lines, so a long paragraph with many `*` is masked once. Test: `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/MarkdownMathEmphasis.test.tsx` (24 tests on a reply, a Markdown text and a reply's table cell; 18 fail on the old tokenizers). Story `Conversation/MessageBody` › `MathInEmphasis`. Shots: `math-in-emphasis-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **A math fence in a reply showed as code.** A reply with a ```` ```math ```` fence showed a "MATH" code block with the TeX source. A quote, a user message and a Markdown preview typeset it (`MarkdownMessageText.tsx`), and GitHub does too. A reply's top-level fences are taken out of the text by `messageContentBlocks` (`DataTable.tsx`) and drawn as a `CodeBlock`, which does not know math. A math fence now stays in the text block, so marked's code token draws it with `MathFormula`. While the fence is the last block of a streaming reply it shows as code, so a half-written formula does not jump between the KaTeX error fallback and typeset math; it is typeset when the reply goes on or ends. Test: `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/ReplyFencedBlocks.test.tsx` › "a math fence in a reply" (2 tests, both fail on the old code). Story `Conversation/MessageBody` › `MathFence`. Shots: `math-fence-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **A tilde fence in a reply lost its content.** `messageContentBlocks` (`parseCodeAt` in `DataTable.tsx`) knew only backtick fences. So in a reply with `~~~markdown` and a table in it, the table was taken out as a data table between two empty code blocks; a backtick fence inside a tilde fence also split it. A fence of 3 or more `~` now opens a code block too, and only a `~` fence at least as long closes it, as in CommonMark and GitHub. Test: `ReplyFencedBlocks.test.tsx` › "a tilde fence in a reply" (2 tests, both fail on the old code; they copy the code block and compare). Story `Conversation/MessageBody` › `TildeFence`. Shots: `tilde-fence-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **Code in a list item kept the item's indent.** In a reply, a fence under a list item (`1. Install:` then `   ```bash`) is taken out of the list by `parseCodeAt` (`DataTable.tsx`), which kept each code line with the item's 2 or 3 spaces. The code block showed every line pushed right, and Copy gave `   def f():`, which Python refuses (unexpected indent). Now up to as many spaces as the opening fence has are taken off each code line, as CommonMark says. Test: `ReplyFencedBlocks.test.tsx` › "a fence inside a list item in a reply" (2 tests copy the code of an ordered and a bullet item; both fail on the old code). Story `Conversation/MessageBody` › `FenceInListItem`. Shots: `fence-in-list-item-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **Copied and quoted formulas came out as KaTeX glyphs.** Three paths took the text of typeset math from the page, which holds each formula twice (MathML for screen readers and the drawn glyphs): Copy as CSV of a reply's table (`DataTable.tsx`, `innerText`) gave `"𝑒\n𝑖\n𝜋\n+\n1…e\niπ\n+1=0"` for `$e^{i\pi} + 1 = 0$`; the selection actions (Quote, Ask, the queued note, Copy from the note field; `SelectionActions.tsx`, `selection.toString()` and `Range.toString()`) quoted `Cost is \n𝑥\n2\nx \n2…`; and a ⌘C of chat text with a formula (KaTeX's `copy-tex`, which `selection-copy.ts` let through) kept the TeX but lost every line break, so two paragraphs and a list came out as one line. A new `math-plain-text.ts` writes the text of a range from the page as the reader sees it, with a line break after each block (two after a paragraph, as `innerText` does), a tab between cells, and each formula as `$…$` or `$$…$$` from its TeX annotation. The three paths use it when the selection or cell holds a formula; text without math is copied as before. A CSV cell stays on one line. Test: `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/MathPlainText.test.tsx` (5 tests: CSV, the selection actions' text of prose, of one formula and of table cells, and a ⌘C with `copy-tex` loaded; all 5 fail on the old code). Chromium check on a probe story (results in the shots folder): `csv-copy-before.json` / `-after.json`, `selection-actions-before.json` / `-after.json`, `selection-copy-before.json` / `-after.json`.
  - **A table with a short delimiter row lost its actions.** A reply's top-level table becomes a data table with "Copy as Markdown" and "Copy as CSV" only when `separatorAlignment` (`DataTable.tsx`) accepts its delimiter row, and it asked for 3 or more `-` in each cell. So `|--|--|`, `|-|-|` or `| :-- | --: |`, which GFM, GitHub and marked accept, fell back to marked's table: other frame, no actions. Now 1 or more `-` is enough, as in GFM. Test: `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/ReplyTableSeparator.test.tsx` (4 delimiter rows; all fail on the old code). Shots (probe story): `short-delimiter-table-before.png`, `-after.png`.
- **Not covered:** the packaged app was not rebuilt or run; the fixes were checked in jsdom tests and Storybook (headless Chromium). The mobile app (`apps/mobile`) has its own Markdown parser and was not changed.
- **Found, not fixed (judgement calls):** an inline formula wider than the bubble with no break point (one long `\left(…\right)` group, many fractions in a row) is cut at the bubble edge with no scroll (`.ui-bubble-content` clips; display math scrolls). A fix changes how long inline formulas wrap or needs a width check per formula. Shot: `wide-inline-math-clipped.png`. A table or a fence under a list item is taken out of the list and drawn after it (the list goes on with the right number). `\[1\]` is display math, not escaped brackets (upstream choice, as ChatGPT). GFM footnotes (`[^1]`) and raw HTML (`<details>`) show as text.
- **Check:** ask an agent for `**the adjoint $A^*$ of $A$**`: the line is bold and shows A*.

## 103. Rich blocks in replies: bug hunt fixes (code, previews, images, links)

- **Get:** code blocks, HTML and Mermaid previews, images and links in a reply act as written. Each fix below has its own commit.
- **Why:** Elias, 24 Nov 2026: a bug hunt over the non-prose blocks of a chat message (code, previews, visuals, media, links).
- **Fixes:**
  - **A link with code as its text opens only the link.** `[`run.ts`](https://…)` showed a local file chip inside the link, so one click opened the workspace file `run.ts` and the web page. Root cause: the link's text was drawn with the file openers, so `MarkdownInline` made its code span a `MentionedFileChip` (a button in the `<a>`). Now the link's text gets no file openers and shows as code. `MarkdownMessageText.tsx` (`case "link"`). Test `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/MarkdownCodeLink.test.tsx` (FAIL before: "TSrun.ts", a button in the link). Story `Conversation/MessageBody` › `LinkWithCodeText`. Shots: `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/chat-bughunt/blocks/link-code-span-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **A Markdown image of a local file shows the file and its picture.** `![Logo](/Users/…/OpenBot/Shared/logo.png)` showed only the word "Logo": no chip to open the file and no picture, while a link or a code span to the same file showed both. Root cause: the image case kept only `http(s)` sources and `messageImageFiles` collected only links and code spans. Now a local image source shows as a file chip (shared or workspace, as for a link) and joins the reply's named image files, so the picture loads under the text. `MarkdownMessageText.tsx` (`case "image"`, `messageImageFiles`). Test `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/MarkdownLocalImage.test.tsx` (FAIL before: no chip, no load). Story `Conversation/MessageBody` › `MarkdownImageOfFile`. Shots: `blocks/image-local-path-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **A code block names its file also for fences in a quote, a deep list or a user's message.** ```` ```ts src/run.ts ```` at the top of a reply showed "src/run.ts" in the block's header, but the same fence in a `>` quote (or `~~~python app.py` there, before #102 also at the top of a reply) showed only the language. Root cause: those fences are drawn by marked's `code` token, and `MarkdownBlock` kept only the first word of the info string. Now it uses `parseCodeInfo` (exported from `DataTable.tsx`), as the reply's top-level fences do. `MarkdownMessageText.tsx` (`case "code"`). Test `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/MarkdownCodeFilename.test.tsx` (FAIL before). Story `Conversation/MessageBody` › `CodeBlockFileNames`. Shots: `blocks/code-fence-filename-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **An HTML preview that sizes itself to the window stays small.** A page with `body{min-height:100vh}` (a card centred in the window, as agents often write) or `height:100vh` grew to the 420 px limit, mostly empty. Root cause: `HtmlPreviewFrame` set the frame to the page's height, the page's height followed the frame's height (`vh`, `100%`), and the frame's `ResizeObserver` measured again: 160 → 184 → … → 444 px. Now `measure` also reads the page at a 0 px tall frame (its holder keeps its height, so the chat does not move): when the page is taller at the shown height, it depends on the frame, and the frame takes its content height or 160 px, whichever is larger. Pages that do not use the window's height size as before. `CodePreview.tsx` (`HtmlPreviewFrame`). Test `bun scripts/chat-html-preview-e2e.ts --storybook=<url>` (story `Conversation/MessageBody` › `HtmlPreviewSizes`; FAIL before: "The centred card got a 420px frame"). Shots: `blocks/html-preview-viewport-height-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **A link to a part of an HTML preview scrolls only the preview.** A table-of-contents link (`<a href="#late">`) in a fenced html page also scrolled the chat, to put the heading at the top of the chat. Root cause: `openLink` called `scrollIntoView()`, which scrolls every scrolling box around the heading, also the chat around the (same-origin) frame. Now it scrolls only the frame's page (`scrollBy` to the heading). A bad `%` escape in the fragment no longer throws. `CodePreview.tsx` (`HtmlPreviewFrame` `openLink`, `fragmentId`). Test `bun scripts/chat-html-preview-e2e.ts --storybook=<url>` (FAIL before: "The link to #late moved the chat: the preview went from 280px to 259px"). Shots after the click: `blocks/html-preview-anchor-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **A visual reply follows light mode.** In light mode a visual (an agent's HTML page in the chat) drew its plain form fields, buttons, lists and system colours dark: dark grey fields on the light chat. Root cause: the visual page's base style set `color-scheme: dark` always, and `ChatVisual` sent the app's colours but not its scheme (the host message also said `theme: "dark"`). Now the base style reads `color-scheme` from `--openbot-color-scheme` (default dark, so the side panel's HTML file previews do not change), `ChatVisual` sends it with the theme (in the frame URL, in `srcdoc` and in the message when the app's scheme changes, so an open page switches at once), the message says the real scheme, and the frame element takes the app's scheme in light mode so the frame stays clear (a frame whose scheme differs from its page gets an opaque white canvas). Pages that set their own colours do not change. Desktop and web client. `packages/contracts/src/chat-visual.ts` (`CHAT_VISUAL_COLOR_SCHEME`, `BASE_STYLE`, `chatVisualThemeMessage`), `ChatVisual.tsx` (`visualTheme`), `styles/chat-visual.css`. Test `bun scripts/chat-visual-color-scheme-e2e.ts --storybook=<url>` (story `Conversation/ChatVisual` › `PlainForm`: light, then dark, then light again; FAIL before: "the app is light, the page is dark", "rgb(59, 59, 59) field"). Shots in light mode: `blocks/visual-light-mode-before.png`, `-after.png`.
  - **A link to a local file whose path has spaces opens that file.** `[weekly report](/Users/…/OpenBot/Shared/Weekly report.md)` (no `<…>` around the path, as agents write it) showed `[weekly report](` and a chip "Weekly" that opened `/Users/…/OpenBot/Shared/Weekly`, then `report.md)`; a path outside the shared folder (`…/Application Support/…`) showed the raw Markdown. Root cause: Markdown needs `<…>` around a link target with spaces, so marked kept it as text, and the shared-path finder cut the path at the first space. Now the local file link repair (`repairEscapedLocalFileLinkTokens`, which already mended `[x]\(<path>)`) also puts `<…>` around a target that starts with `/`, `~/` or a drive letter, has a space and no `(`, `)`, `<`, `>` or `"`; images too, and `messageImageFiles` collects such images, so their picture loads. Code spans stay as written. `MarkdownMessageText.tsx` (`normalizeEscapedLocalFileLinkCandidate`, `SPACED_LOCAL_FILE_LINK_START`, `messageImageFiles`). Test `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/MarkdownSpacedFileLink.test.tsx` (FAIL before: no "Open shared file my report.md"), and a spaced image in `MarkdownLocalImage.test.tsx`. Story `Conversation/MessageBody` › `FileLinksWithSpaces`. Shots: `blocks/file-link-spaces-before.png`, `-after.png`.
- **Check:** see each fix's line.

## 104. Dark paper for PDFs, and a brightness slider for PDFs, images and web pages in the file panel

- **Get:** for a PDF, the file panel's header has two buttons. The moon opens "Page colors": Off, Dark paper, Sepia. The sun opens "Brightness": a slider from 40 to 100 % with Reset. An image or a rendered web page shows only the sun. Dark paper shows a white page as dark gray (`#262626`) with light gray text, and Sepia as warm paper with brown text. Photos stay natural, and colored text and figures keep their hue. The brightness applies on top of the page color, so Dark paper at 70 % is darker again. The page dims at once while you drag. The two settings are kept independently for the next file and after a restart. Off is Chromium's PDF viewer as before, and the slider dims it too. While a setting is on, its button shows in a brighter color (and the sun changes to a dimmed sun). Only one of the two pop-ups can be open. Escape closes only the open pop-up, not the panel.
- **Why:** Elias, 24 Nov 2026: a PDF in the side panel is a white page next to the dark app, too bright at the monitor brightness that the dark app needs. He compared three drafts (A: brightness slider, B: dark paper, C: automatic tone) and chose A and B together: dark paper for PDFs, the slider for images and pages. C was not merged; its branch `draft/auto-tone` is kept.
- **How (portable):**
  - Page colors: with a mode on, the panel shows the PDF with the pdf.js `PDFViewer` (text layer for selection and copy, link layer, page tracking, zoom) in place of the browser's viewer. After each page draws (`pagerendered`), the page canvas is changed pixel by pixel in OKLab: the lightness maps from ink (L = 0) to paper (L = 1), and the hue and chroma are added back at 90 % (Sepia 80 %). A vivid color (OKLCH chroma above 0.07) keeps most of its own lightness, so an orange bar stays orange. The image areas that pdf.js records (`recordImages`) are sampled first: a photo is only dimmed, and a scan or line art changes with the page. A page that is already dark (slides) stays as it is. A canvas is hidden until it is changed, so no white page flashes. Colors come from the brand tokens `--openbot-reading-{dark,sepia}-{paper,ink}`.
  - Brightness: CSS `filter: brightness(x)` on the element that shows the file. Page CSS cannot reach into Chromium's PDF viewer, but a filter on its frame element works. The panel writes the value to `--file-preview-brightness` on the content box, and a `[data-dimmed]` rule applies the filter to the PDF frame, the image, the page frame and any element with the class `file-preview-dimmable`, only below 100 %. The pdf.js reader puts that class on its page container. So the filter applies after the recolor (the recolor reads the canvas pixels, which a CSS filter does not change), and the reader's toolbar and the gaps between the pages keep the app's colors. One value for all file kinds: the glare is the same problem for each light file. The slider applies each step at once and saves on release, key release or Reset.
  - One pop-up state in the panel: opening one pop-up closes the other. A capture-phase Escape handler closes only the open pop-up and marks the key as handled, because the conversation's Escape handler closes the side panel when focus is outside a dialog or menu. If the slider closes before a release (Escape, the moon, a click outside), the panel saves the value that shows, so the shown value and the stored value stay the same. The slider pop-up moves focus to the slider when it opens, and Reset does too. After Escape, focus goes back to the button of the pop-up. A choice in the Page colors menu closes the menu (Kobalte radio items stay open by default).
  - Storage: `localStorage` `openbot:file-preview-reading-mode` and `openbot:file-preview-brightness`, as the panel's wrap-lines and width values. These are display choices of this computer; a joined server does not need them.
- **Here:** `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/FilePreviewPanel.tsx` (`PageColorsControl`, `BrightnessControl`, pop-up state, the reader slot), `file-preview-brightness.ts` (clamp, parse, style, which kinds dim), `pdf-reading-mode.ts` (modes, labels, reader slot type), rules in `styles/file-preview.css`; `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/FilePreviewPanel.tsx` (storage, gives `PdfPaperReader`), `PdfPaperReader.tsx`, `pdf-paper.ts` (recolor), `pdf-paper.css` (pdf.js layer styles), `pdf-js.ts` (shared pdf.js loader, also used by `message-pdf-pages.ts`); icons `Sun`, `SunDim` in `packages/ui/src/icons.ts`; tokens in `packages/brand/src/tokens.css`. i18n: `preview.panel.brightness*` in all 15 languages; `preview.reading.*` and `preview.pdf.*` in English only (listed in `untranslated.json`). Stories: `Conversation/FilePreviewDimmer` (the real app with a chat that links the worksheet PDF, an image and a web page; and the panel alone), `Conversation/FilePreviewChat` › `Chat (PDF worksheet, …)`, `Conversation/FilePreviewPanel` › `PDF worksheet (…)`. Test PDF: `src/renderer/stories/assets/algebra-worksheet.pdf` (LaTeX text and formulas, a photo, a TikZ chart, blue headings, links). Draft A's own test PDF had a macOS wallpaper photo and is not kept.
- **Tests:** `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/FilePreviewPanel.test.tsx` (8): draft A's 5 (stored value parse and clamp, which kinds dim, a saved 60 % on a PDF, Reset saves 100 % and focuses the slider, Escape closes only the slider, no button for Markdown) and 3 for the combination (a saved Dark paper and 70 % show the pdf.js reader dimmed, and a slider change that closes before a release is saved; an image shows the sun and no moon; the two pop-ups open one at a time and Escape closes only the open one). `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/pdf-paper.test.ts` (10, draft B's recolor tests). `typecheck:renderer`, `typecheck:storybook`, `i18n:check`, Biome on the changed files: PASS. A Biome rule forbids style assertions in tests, so the filter itself is checked in the browser only. Storybook in headless Chromium (Playwright, the real app story at a 3440×1440 window and at a 960 px window), 26 checks, all PASS: the moon and the sun on a PDF, only the sun on an image and a rendered web page; the computed filter is `brightness(0.7)` on the pdf.js page container and `none` on its toolbar and scroll area; Escape closes only the open pop-up and gives focus back to its button; Escape with no pop-up still closes the panel; 48 fast alternating clicks on the two buttons in 4 rounds and double-clicks: never two pop-ups, and Escape always recovers (no stuck `pointer-events` or `aria-hidden`); a mode change during a slider drag and six fast mode and slider changes: the stored value always equals the value that shows, and the filter is on the new reader; a reload keeps Dark paper and 70 %; at 360 px the header buttons and the reader toolbar fit without overlap. Shots and the script with its results: `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/pdf-dim/AB-final/` (sheet `AB-final-sheet.png`, self-test in `selftest/`).
- **Lost with a page color on:** the Chromium viewer's find (⌘F), print, thumbnails, outline, form filling and rotate. A change between Off and a page color starts again at page 1.
- **Not covered:** not run in the Electron dev app or a packaged build (the machine was too loaded; Storybook uses the same Chromium PDF viewer and pdf.js). Drag smoothness of the filter on a long PDF was not measured. The Library viewer and the side panel read the two values when they open and do not update each other live. A dark web page or a dark image dims too below 100 %. Video and audio do not dim. The source of the photo in the worksheet PDF (draft B) was not checked.
- **Check:** open a PDF in the side panel, pick Dark paper with the moon, then drag the sun's slider to about 70 %: the dark page goes darker while you drag. Open an image: only the sun shows, still at 70 %. Quit and reopen OpenBot, open the PDF: Dark paper and 70 % are still on.
- **Changed by #124:** Dark paper and Sepia failed in the built app (the pdf.js worker was served with a wrong type), and a PDF opened from a path showed blank; both fixed. The panel now keeps each PDF tab's page, place and zoom (also "Off"), and a page color change no longer starts at page 1.

## 105. Channel feed: threads with the open thread beside it

- **Get:** a channel shows a feed of threads by default (header switch **Feed / Chat**, kept per channel on this computer). One row per root message, oldest at the top and newest at the foot; a new reply never moves a row. A row shows the author's face, name and time, the text cut at about 5 lines (a long text fades out), and, when the thread has replies, "N replies · Last reply 5 min ago" with up to 3 replier faces. A click on a row, or its hover icon "Reply in thread", opens the thread in a panel beside the feed: "Thread · <channel>", a ⋯ menu (Open in Chat view, Copy first message) and a close button; the root and every reply with the normal message rendering (Markdown, code, attachments, handoff and routing lines); its own composer, "Reply…", with `@` mentions of the channel's members. Every send from the panel is a reply to the root. A half-written reply stays with its thread when the panel closes or another thread opens (until OpenBot quits). The feed composer says "Start a thread in <channel>": a send is a new root, the panel opens on it and the focus stays in the feed composer; a reply quoted in Chat (Reply on a message) and sent from the feed goes into the quoted message's thread and opens that thread. The panel is 440 px by default, at least 360 px, leaves the feed at least 360 px, and keeps its width (drag or keyboard on its edge, double-click resets). Esc in the panel or on the feed closes it and gives the focus back to its row; Esc in the feed composer or another text field outside the panel leaves it open. Scrolling up the feed loads older threads by itself and keeps the rows in view. It updates live: new replies, counts and bot answers. **Chat** is today's flat view, unchanged.
- **Why:** Elias, 24 Nov 2026: MausBot's room feed (`~/Developer/lab/RE-mausbot/notes-ui.md`), ported as "look only": bots keep one shared context per channel; no new tables, sessions or queues.
- **How (portable):**
  - A thread is a root plus every message whose parent chain leads to it. The parent is `replyToMessageId`. An agent's answer has no `replyToMessageId` in a channel: it carries only its `taskId`. Its parent is the newest member message among the request messages of its task and of the task's root task (`requestMessageId` and `sourceMessageIds`) that is older than the answer. So answers, subtask answers, results, routing lines and handoffs of a request land in the thread of the message that asked; after a later message continues the task (a thread reply, or a "yes" in the feed), the next answers follow that message. A system line of an agent (handoff, routing) prefers this task link to its own `replyToMessageId`, because a handoff quotes a source that the agent chose and that can sit in another thread. A missing parent makes the message a root; a reply cycle ends at its oldest message. A deleted request is skipped when a live one is older than the answer, so the answer to a deleted thread reply stays in the thread; the replies of a deleted root (its tombstone has no `replyToMessageId`) stay together under the oldest of them. No backend change was needed: verified with a backend test (root → answer, panel reply with `@Ben` → Ben's answer, Ben's `channel_assign` handoff → Ana's subtask answer and `channel_result`, then a new root → its answer: all in the right thread).
  - Counts come from the renderer's page cache, with no main-process query: the loaded messages are always one unbroken run up to the newest message (`mergeChannelPage` drops older pages that no longer join), a reply is always newer than its root, and every link of a chain lies between the two, so every root that the feed shows has all of its replies loaded. Older roots show after "Load older threads". Counted: person messages and agent answers that have content (`channelConversationLine`); not counted: thinking, plan, subagent, memory and channel-change rows, routing/handoff lines and deleted rows. **Superseded answers are counted** (a decision against the brief, which asked to leave them out): the backend marks every earlier answer of a request as superseded when a later message continues it, and a reply in a thread always continues the root's request, so in the smoke test a thread showed 5 replies while the brief's rule counted 3. Superseded answers stay visible in the panel and in Chat. To leave them out, add `entry.superseded ||` to the counting loop in `channelThreads`.
  - Feed rows: roots that are conversation lines (a person's message, a routine request, an agent's own post). Routing markers, channel-change notes, context resets and thinking are never rows: they show inside the thread they belong to (through their task), or only in Chat when they belong to no request.
  - Feed mode replaces the timeline in the conversation view (one `Show` in `ConversationView`); the panel is an absolute `aside` beside it, and the conversation panel gets `padding-inline-end` of the panel width (`data-channel-thread="beside"`). When the conversation is narrower than 720 px (feed 360 + panel 360) the panel covers the feed (`"over"`). Opening the thread closes the side panel (settings, browser, files) and opening one of those closes the thread. Question prompts, approvals, browser takeovers and "… is working" show at the foot of the feed, as in Chat.
  - Feed composer: a reply target set in Chat is sent as a reply (`feedThreadToOpen` opens the root of the sent message). The feed column must not shrink (`flex: none`), or rows above the window cannot be scrolled to. Agent-authored rows show `markdownPreviewText` (no raw `**`, `#`, fences). "Open in Chat view" uses the timeline's own message focus (`messageFocusRequest` with the channel id; upstream `conversation-scope.ts` matches `props.agent?.id ?? channel?.id`), because a plain `scrollIntoView` lost against the timeline's glide to the latest message.
- **Here:** `packages/contracts/src/fork-channel-threads.ts` (parent and root resolver, rows, summaries; export `@openbot/contracts/fork-channel-threads`); `src/renderer/src/features/channels/ChannelFeedView.tsx` (feed, row, thread panel, panel composer, Feed/Chat switch), `channel-feed.ts` (send command, relative time, per-channel view and panel width in `localStorage` `openbot:channel-view:<id>` and `openbot:channel-thread-panel-width`, the 768 px gate), feed styles at the end of `channels.css`, wiring in `channel-conversation.tsx` and `WorkspaceChannelConversation.tsx`. Upstream files: `conversation-types.ts` (optional `feed` slot on the channel source), `ConversationView.tsx` (feed in place of the timeline), `ConversationComposer.tsx` (the feed placeholder), `conversation-scope.ts` (a message focus request may name a channel). i18n: `channel.feed.*`, `channel.thread.*`, English only (listed in `untranslated.json`).
- **Tests:** `src/renderer/src/features/channels/channel-feed.test.ts` (21, jsdom: chains, missing and deleted parent, answer of a deleted reply, replies of a deleted root, cycle, answers by task, continued task, subtask, handoff citing another thread, order, rows without system lines, an agent's own post, summaries with ≤3 repliers newest first, what counts, panel and feed send targets, the thread a feed send opens, thread drafts, which Esc closes the panel, panel max width, relative time) and `src/backend/fork-channel-threads.test.ts` (1, the real `ChannelService` with SQLite: answers, handoff and result of a panel reply land in its thread). `typecheck:renderer`, `typecheck:node`, `check:ui`, `i18n:check`, Biome on the changed files: PASS. Smoke test on an isolated dev instance (own `HOME` and `CFFIXED_USER_HOME`, seeded profile, agents on the bench's fake Codex), driven with cua-driver: 3 roots with `@` mentions (each opened the panel, answers streamed into it), a reply and an `@Builder` question from the panel (Builder's answer in the thread, count 5), 12 fast clicks across rows (one panel, one open row, no error), drag to 541 px (feed held at 360) and to 360 px (minimum), Chat and back, Esc, a 960 px window. Shots: `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/openbot-105/`.
- **Not covered:** the web client and a separate narrow chat window (< 768 px) keep the flat view (the feed is passed only by the desktop workspace; the web client uses the same components but was not checked). No per-row unread dot: the channel's unread count works as before (the controller marks the channel read when it loads the page). The panel composer takes text and mentions only, no attachments, voice or quote-reply; panel messages have no hover actions (copy, reply, delete: use Chat). Copy link is missing: OpenBot has no link to a channel message. No "jump to latest" button in the feed. Find (⌘F) works in Chat only. A reply whose root is older than the loaded pages shows as its own row until the root is loaded. A send from the feed shows its row after the host answers (no pending row). Esc's focus return was checked with a key event in the window; a cua-driver keypress that first activates the window lets the composer take the focus back (upstream's focus restore on window focus). Not run in a packaged build.
- **Check:** open a channel: rows with "N replies". Click a row: the thread opens beside the feed with the focus in "Reply…". Write "@" and pick a member, send: the answer appears in the panel and the row's count goes up. Esc: the panel closes, the row has the focus. Switch to Chat: the flat timeline as before.

## 106. A message whose provider was briefly down waits and runs, instead of failing for good

- **Get:** a message (a WhatsApp Watch line, an app message, a teammate's result, a routine run) whose start failed only because its provider was not there for a moment (the CLI being replaced or updated, the SDK client stopped, the CLI not ready yet, OpenBot quitting) goes back to the queue ("Queued" in the chat) and starts once the provider is back: when the provider resumes, a new message arrives, or after a backoff of 15 s → 2 min. A message caught by OpenBot's quit runs after the next start. It gives up after 12 failed starts or 3 hours (from the first failure in this run of OpenBot) and then fails as before. A messaging message (a WhatsApp line) that fails now shows in the orchestrator's chat with the **Failed** state, as an app message does, and the owner's "Message yourself" chat gets the failure note as before; nothing goes to a watched chat (the #68 guard is unchanged). Real errors fail at once as before: a missing sign-in, an auth error (401), a usage limit, a bad request, a provider crash ("… exited with code 1").
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026: Ichiro's watched line ("👁️ Ichiro Akiyama: Waiting on tapa …", 09:45 UTC) never reached the WhatsApp Orchestrator. The logs and database (read only) showed: at 09:45:57 the Claude CLI update waited for Claude to be idle, so every new Claude message was held (`A queued message waits … provider-cli-replaced`); the hold lasted until the quit at 12:07:03, when stopping the provider runtimes ended the update wait (`#endUpdateWhenIdle` → `onProviderResumed` → `scheduleDrains`) while the agent service was not stopping yet. The held messages started, the Claude client stopped under them, and `startDelivery`'s catch marked them failed for good ("Claude Agent SDK is not running." ×5, "Claude CLI is not ready or signed in." ×1 in that second, plus two routine runs). Same pattern after the quits of 12, 14, 17 and 20 Nov. A failed WhatsApp line was also invisible: `MailboxStore.conversationMessages` skips messaging messages, and the start had failed before it added the line to the chat.
- **How (portable):**
  - Root cause: `DrainScheduler.startDelivery`'s catch (`drain-scheduler.ts`, upstream line ~1280) has a "wait and try again" branch only for `ProviderStoppedError` from a resume (3 tries), a turn that is still active and a missing sign-in. A plain `Error("Claude Agent SDK is not running.")` (`claude-client.ts` 937/1482), the CLI "not ready" of `ProviderRuntime.ensureProvider` or Codex's "Codex App Server stopped." fell through to `markTerminal(…, "failed")`.
  - Fix: `#forkWaitForProvider`, called after the routine start retry and before the failure: `forkProviderDownAtStart` (`fork-handoff-retry.ts`) matches `ProviderStoppedError`, the clients' own "… is not running." / "Codex App Server stopped." texts and the `error.provider.cliNotReady` / `error.provider.closing` source texts, never when the sign-in is required, a `ProviderSignInError` or an auth error. The batch goes back with `restoreQueued`. While OpenBot stops (`isStopping`) that is all: the queued state is stored and boot drains it. Otherwise the lane gets the existing `provider-turn` hold (only when no drain was asked for during the start) and `#retryBusySession`'s backoff; `onProviderResumed` → `scheduleDrains` lifts the hold. `ForkHandoffRetry` counts failed starts per delivery (in memory, reset by a successful start); after `FORK_HANDOFF_RETRY` (12 starts or 3 h) the old failure path runs.
  - Visibility: `forkSyncMessagingLine` puts a messaging message's state into its thread snapshot (`MessagingThreads.threadForDelivery`, `registerExecutionThread`, `MailboxSync.syncDeliveryMessage`): "queued" when it waits, and on failure it adds the line when missing, with `status: "failed"` (the renderer shows "Failed", `app-message-projection.ts`). Saved through `emitConversation`.
  - Not changed: the upstream hold itself. A CLI update or restart "when idle" holds every new message of that provider until no turn of it runs; on 25 Nov that took 2 h 20 min. The message now survives it, but it still waits that long.
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent/fork-handoff-retry.ts` (new); `src/backend/agent/drain-scheduler.ts` (the `#forkWaitForProvider` call and method, `forkSyncMessagingLine` in the failure path, the retry count's `forget`/`clear`).
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-retry-handoff.test.ts` (10: which errors wait and which fail; cap and give-up time; then the real `DrainScheduler`, `MailboxStore`, `MessagingThreads`, `ConversationRuntime` and `MailboxSync` on SQLite with a stub provider: a watched WhatsApp line held while the CLI is replaced runs once when it is back; a start refused with "Claude Agent SDK is not running." is queued again, holds without looping and runs once; "CLI not ready" waits; the give-up marks the line failed in the orchestrator's chat (saved) and sends one failure to the self chat, never the watched contact; a line that failed after it reached the chat shows failed, not "starting"; auth, bad request and a missing sign-in fail at once; a start interrupted by the quit is queued and runs on a new scheduler and mailbox on the same database). Without the fix 5 of them fail. Related suites: `fork-whatsapp-watch*`, `fork-channel-*`, `fork-network-resume`, `fork-restart-resume`, `fork-backup-only-at-limit`, and all of `src/backend` + `src/main`. `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, Biome: PASS. Smoke test on an isolated dev instance (own `HOME` and `CFFIXED_USER_HOME`, `dev:seed`, agents on the bench's fake Codex with a "provider down" and a "start pending" switch, driven with `dev:automation`): (A) the provider refused two starts ("Queued" in the chat, attempts at +0 s and +15 s), came back, and the message ran once at +45 s; (B) OpenBot quit while a start was pending: the delivery stayed queued ("Codex App Server stopped.", `stopping: true`), and after the restart it waited for the providers and ran. Shots and logs: `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/openbot-106/`.
- **Not covered:** the real Claude CLI update was not reproduced (the sandbox has no Claude sign-in); the fake Codex refuses with the same texts. The give-up path was checked in the tests, not in the app (it needs 12 starts or 3 h). The retry count is per run of OpenBot: after a restart a message gets 12 new tries. "Claude ended the turn with no answer" (a turn that started) and API connection errors during a turn are other paths and are unchanged. A message that already ran when OpenBot quit is the restart resume's case (#42).
- **Check:** in a dev instance, make the provider unavailable (stop its process or hold its update) and send a message: it shows "Queued" and `main.log` says "A message waits for its provider to come back."; when the provider is back the message runs once.

## 110. A `/compact` turn that compacted ends as done, not "Claude ended the turn with no answer"

- **Get:** the "🗜 Compact before the cache goes cold" (and "Compact a big chat") routine run, and a `/compact` you type in a Claude chat, end as completed when Claude compacted: no failed delivery, no failed routine row, no red ✕ in the sidebar, no error text. A `/compact` whose compaction failed still fails with the no-answer error, and every other turn with no answer fails as before (also a normal turn with an automatic compaction).
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026: Apoo's routine run at 15:04 UTC compacted ("Context compacted"), then showed **failed** with "Claude ended the turn with no answer. Send the message again, or start a new chat." 19 of the 20 such failures since 23 Nov were these runs. The upstream merge of v0.76.1 (23 Nov) brought #13168, which runs the no-answer check (`TurnLifecycle.#holdNoAnswer`) for Claude too; the runs before it ended as completed.
- **How (portable):** root cause: `#holdNoAnswer` skips a turn whose `running.compacted` is set, but only Codex's `contextCompaction` item sets it. Claude reports a compaction with `openbot/context-compaction` (`claude-client.ts`: `status` compacting / `compact_result`, `compact_boundary`), which only fed `ContextCompaction`. A `/compact` turn ends with an empty answer by design, so it failed. Fix: on `openbot/context-compaction` `completed` / `failed`, `#forkCompactCommandEnded` sets / clears `compacted` on the running turns of that thread and client whose deliveries are all a `/compact` command (`isCompactCommand`: the text starts with `/compact`). Nothing else changes, so a turn with an automatic compaction keeps the check. The empty `…:assistant` row Claude's client writes at a turn end is not shown (`AgentThreadView` `shownInThread` hides an empty, settled answer). Codex, Gemini and the ACP providers: the routine runs only for Claude (`contextCompaction === "native"`); a Codex compaction already sets `compacted`, and the ACP providers have their own check (`noAnswerUnlessPlanOrCompaction`).
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent/turn-lifecycle.ts` (`#forkCompactCommandEnded`, its call in the `openbot/context-compaction` case); `isCompactCommand` in `src/backend/fork-cache-compact.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-compact-ok.test.ts` (5; the real `AgentService` and `ClaudeAgentClient` against a stand-in `claude` CLI, after `scripts/claude-empty-turn-e2e.ts`): the routine run (`enqueueRoutineHandoff`, `fork-compact`, the real prompt) and a typed `/compact` that compacted end completed with no error and no answer; a `/compact` whose compaction failed fails with the no-answer error; a plain answer completes; an empty turn and an empty turn after an automatic compaction still fail. Without the fix the two compaction cases fail.
- **Not done:** the real Claude CLI's `/compact` frames were not replayed (the stand-in sends `status` compacting, `compact_boundary`, `compact_result: success`, then an empty `result`, as the live log and database of 25 Nov show). A `/compact` that Claude refuses without a compaction ("not enough messages") still shows the no-answer error. Past failed runs stay failed in the history.
- **Check:** in a Claude chat type `/compact`: "Context compacted", the message stays done (no Failed, no ✕ in the sidebar, no error). Test switch for the routine: `OPENBOT_FORK_COMPACT_QUIET_MINUTES=2` (entry 15).

## 108. Section inheritance: shared instructions, skills and MCP servers per sidebar folder

- **Design note:** a sidebar folder (section) keeps optional shared defaults: instructions (Markdown), local-library skills and MCP servers. Nothing is copied into agents. At each turn start OpenBot resolves the agent's folder chain from the live sidebar layout (root folder → subfolder, up to the 3 folder levels) and adds the result to the turn, so an agent that is in the folder now, also one created or moved there later, gets it from its next message, and an agent moved out loses it. Rule: **parent folder text first, then the subfolder, then the agent's own Instructions; the agent's own Instructions and instruction files win on a conflict, and a deeper folder wins over its parent.** Skills and MCP servers are added (union, no duplicates); an agent can turn off a folder skill or MCP server for itself, and its own skill copy with the same name wins over the folder's. Defaults are stored apart from the sidebar layout (no change to the released layout format or the Team API), one record per folder.
- **Get:** right-click a custom folder in the sidebar → **Shared instructions and skills…**: a dialog with the folder's agents, what parent folders already share (badge "From Editing"), a Markdown text box, a filterable checklist of the local skills, and a checklist of the MCP servers. Esc or a click outside with unsaved changes asks "Discard changes?" first. In an agent's Settings, **Knows → From folder · Editing / Clients** (shown when its folder chain shares something) opens a read-only page: one card per folder with a badge, the shared text rendered as Markdown, the skills and MCP servers each with an on/off switch for this agent, and **Edit in Clients** (opens the folder dialog; the page reloads after a save there or a sidebar change). A skill or MCP server deleted after it was shared is left out of the page, the dialog and `read_section`, and the next save of the folder drops it; it restarts no agent. Agents get the tools `openbot.read_section` and `openbot.update_section_defaults`; `openbot.list_sections` shows each folder's shared defaults (`sectionDefaults`) and `openbot.read_agent` has a `section` field (folder, inherited layers, opt-outs) and marks MCP servers that came from the folder (`inheritedFrom`).
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026 (via Vegapunk): agents in one folder (`Editing ▸ Clients`: Tapa, Tatiana, Tyson, Adrian) need the same rules and skills; copying them into each agent's AGENTS.md drifted, and retros fixed one agent at a time.
- **How (portable):**
  - Store: `fork-section-defaults-v1.json` in userData, `{ version: 1, sections: { <sectionId>: { instructions, skillIds, mcpServerIds, updatedAt } }, optOuts: { <agentId>: { skillIds, mcpServerIds } } }`. The Markdown is kept as typed. An emptied folder is removed; folders deleted from the sidebar are pruned on the next layout change.
  - Resolve (`resolveSectionInheritance`): `agentAssignments[agentId]` → `sidebarFolderParents` + `sidebarFolderAncestors` → layers root first. Effective skills/MCP servers = union in chain order minus the agent's opt-outs. Results are cached per agent and dropped on every layout or store change.
  - Prompt: the same mechanism as the section teammates list. The developer instructions get one constant line (`SECTION_DEFAULTS_POINTER`: what the block is, the precedence rule, and "update the folder once with update_section_defaults, not each agent"), placed before the agent's own `<agent_instructions>`; it never changes, so it does not restart a Claude query. The content goes as a turn-context block `<openbot_section_defaults>` (`TurnContext`, sent once, again only when it changes, after compaction or a revert, and a "no longer apply" block when the agent leaves). It is placed before the agent's own `<openbot_agent_instructions>` block. The thread lifecycle's turn-context key includes the folder defaults, so a change resumes the session with the new params on the next message. This is one path for Claude (Agent SDK), Codex (app-server; the param is removed from `thread/start`/`thread/resume` like the other turn-context params) and every ACP agent (Gemini/Antigravity, OpenCode, custom ACP, Pi, Muse).
  - Skills: folder skills are local-library skills (`local-skill-…`). The newest revision is unpacked once to `userData/fork-section-skills/<id>/<revision>/` and listed in the block with name, description and the `SKILL.md` path ("read its SKILL.md first and follow it"), as OpenBot already does for home skills on Gemini. Nothing is written into agent workspaces.
  - MCP servers: the per-agent MCP filter (`mcpAgentAccess`) allows a server when its own access list allows the agent **or** the agent's folder chain grants it (minus opt-outs). An opt-out removes only the folder grant, not a server that is open to everyone. When an agent's folder MCP set changes (edit, move in/out, opt-out), only that agent's runtime is refreshed.
  - Tools and permissions: `read_section {sectionId?}` (omit for your own folder) returns path, text, skills, MCP servers, parent layers, agents and the rule. `update_section_defaults {sectionId, instructions?, skillIds?, mcpServerIds?}`: each field replaces the old value; skills by id, name or slug (the name the turn block shows) from the local library, MCP servers by id or name. Refused when any person behind the turn (direct or relayed through a teammate) is a server member who is not an owner or admin (`personCanAdminister`, as `set_browser_profile`). Adding an MCP server shows the owner-only approval card; text and skills need no card, as `update_profile` (agents edit their own and others' profiles without a card). Tool guidance for the section and profile tools says to keep shared rules in the folder.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-section-inheritance.ts` (store, resolver, prompt block, `ForkSectionInheritance`), `src/backend/agent/fork-section-tools.ts` (tools, `list_sections` summary, `read_agent` view, guidance), `src/main/fork-section-defaults.ts` (wiring, prune and MCP refresh on layout change), `src/main/fork-section-skills.ts` (unpack library skills), `packages/contracts/src/fork-section-defaults.ts` (types, parsers, decoders); small hooks marked `Fork (docs/fork/FEATURES.md #108)` in `fork-hub.ts`, `agent/turn-context.ts`, `agent/developer-instructions.ts`, `agent/thread-lifecycle.ts`, `app-server-client.ts`, `agent/openbot-tool-router.ts`, `agent/sidebar-tools.ts`, `agent/tool-guidance.ts`, `openbot-tools.ts`, `main/application-services.ts`, `main/index.ts`, `main/ipc/agent-admin-handlers.ts` (4 `agentAdmin` endpoints, this computer only), `preload/index.ts`; UI `packages/ui/src/features/sidebar/SectionDefaultsDialog.tsx` + `section-defaults.css`, the menu item in `SidebarSectionHeader.tsx` (`onOpenSectionSettings`), `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/AgentSectionDefaultsView.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/features/sidebar/fork-section-defaults.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/fork-agent-section.ts`, the row in `AgentSettingsPanel.tsx`; i18n keys `sidebar.section.sharedDefaults`, `sidebar.sectionDefaults.*`, `agentSettings.section.*`, `error.backend.sectionDefaultsThisComputerOnly` (English only, in `untranslated.json`). `scripts/dev-automation/bench-fake-codex.ts` got an opt-in `prompts` switch that logs each turn's input; `scripts/provider-fakes/agents/codex-turn-input.ts` and `acp-turn-input.ts` are test fakes.
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-section-inheritance.test.ts` (9; vitest by request, an exception to the repo's E2E-only default): store round trip and pruning; resolution for folder only, agent outside folders, nested parent → child, agents moved in/out and a new agent assigned later; opt-outs and the agent's own skill copy; MCP refresh only for agents whose folder set changed; turn block before the agent's own instructions block, sent once, again on change, "no longer apply" on leave; pointer vs inline placement before `<agent_instructions>`; a real Codex app-server client and a real ACP client against fake CLIs (the folder text reaches `turn/start` input / `session/prompt`, and is not sent to Codex in `thread/start`); deleted skills and MCP servers left out and dropped on the next save without an agent restart, two concurrent partial updates both kept; tools: member refusal, update, MCP approval declined then accepted, unknown skill, a skill by slug, `read_section` of your own folder, `list_sections` summary, `read_agent` view.
- **Smoke test:** isolated dev instance (`HOME`/`CFFIXED_USER_HOME=/tmp/openbot108/home`, `dev:seed`, the seeded agents switched to the bench's fake Codex with `prompts: true`, a local skill `client-voice` in the sandbox library, folders `Editing ▸ Clients` with Chief and Research), driven over CDP like `dev:automation`. Chief's turn before: no block. Set the Clients rule and the skill in the dialog (and a rule on the parent Editing): Chief's next turn starts with `<openbot_section_defaults>` holding the Editing rule, then the Clients rule, then `client-voice` with its unpacked `SKILL.md` path. A new agent Tyson created and put in Clients got the same block on its next turn. Chief's Settings showed Knows → From folder · Editing / Clients and the page with both folder badges and Edit in Editing / Edit in Clients (opens the dialog). Chief moved out of the folder: its next turn said the earlier section defaults no longer apply, and the row was gone. Proof in `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/openbot-108/` (screenshots 00–08, `turn-proof.txt`, `codex-turns-all.jsonl`). The sandbox was stopped with `dev:stop`.
- **Not done:** joined servers and the web client: the folder dialog and the agent page show only for this computer's own server (`kind === "local"`); there is no Team API route yet (the agents on a server still inherit, and its admins can change folders through an agent). The existing `create/rename/delete_section` and `assign_agent_section` tools and the sidebar Team API routes still have no role check (upstream behaviour; see #107 for roles). Marketplace and home skills cannot be folder skills (home skills already reach every agent). Claude does not list a folder skill in its own Skill tool; the agent reads the `SKILL.md` by path. An agent that was moved out while OpenBot was closed gets no "no longer apply" block in a resumed provider session. A local skill revision reaches agents at their next session resume, not mid-session.
- **Check:** right-click a folder → Shared instructions and skills…, type a rule, tick a local skill, Save. Send a message to an agent in that folder (or in a subfolder, or one created there afterwards): its next turn starts with an `<openbot_section_defaults>` block that has the rule and the skill's `SKILL.md` path. Open the agent's Settings → Knows → From folder: the rule shows with the folder badge and Edit in <folder>. Move the agent out of the folder and send again: the turn says the earlier section defaults no longer apply.

## 111. A Gemini (Antigravity) reply shows once it is written, also while the agent's subagents still run

- **Get:** a reply of a Gemini agent shows in the chat about 10 seconds after its last word, also when the agent keeps working (subagents in the background) and also when you steered the message into its running turn. Before, such a reply stayed "being written" until the agent's next step, often minutes later, and the chat (default "natural" reply style) holds a reply back until it is written: the agent seemed to stop answering. Whatever the turn ends with is still its answer.
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026: "Vegapunk stops answering my messages". Measured on a read-only copy of his database and Antigravity's own conversation file: one turn (`9a6e959a…`) ran from 13:36 to past 16:20 UTC because Antigravity keeps a prompt open while the agent's background subagents run, and each of his 25 steers joined it. Every steer reached the agent (`ACP Request received: session/prompt` in `acp-server-…-57713.log`) and got a reply within 0.1–3.5 min, but e.g. the reply to his 15:56 message was written 15:58:55–15:59:26 (model step 608), the model then waited for its subagents until 16:03:09, and the row stayed `streaming` until the next step at 16:04:41; the 13:49:55 reply stayed `streaming` until his next message at 14:00:27. Since 18 Nov, up to 137 replies of 6 Gemini agents had their next step more than a minute later (Vegapunk 92, 22 over 5 min). A side effect seen at 16:04: the stored text of that streaming row was only the part written after the pause.
- **How (portable):** root cause: ACP marks no end of a reply; `AcpAgentClient` completes a reply only at the next tool call or thought, or when the prompt ends (`#completeMessage`), and `ANTIGRAVITY_ACP_FLAVOR.promptOpenForBackgroundTasks` keeps the prompt open. Fix in `acp-client.ts`: for a flavor with `promptOpenForBackgroundTasks`, each reply chunk sets `AcpTurn.textAt` and starts one watch (`#watchQuietAnswer`); when the reply got no chunk for `ACP_ANSWER_QUIET_MS` (10 s) and the turn still runs, `#completeQuietAnswer` completes it as `commentary`, exactly what the next step would have done (a narration row in the "natural" style, a step in the classic one). It skips a Gemini failure answer, a held text tool call, a paused answer, retry notices and a provider error, which wait for the prompt's end as before. The quiet reply is kept in `AcpTurn.quietAnswer` until a tool call or another reply follows; if the turn ends with nothing after it, `#completeTurn` sends it again as `final_answer` (same item id), so it stays the turn's answer, also in channels. Other providers are unchanged: their prompts end when the reply is written.
- **Here:** `src/backend/acp-client.ts` (`ACP_ANSWER_QUIET_MS`, `answerQuietMs` option, `AcpTurn.textAt` / `quietWatch` / `quietAnswer`, `#watchQuietAnswer`, `#completeQuietAnswer`, the promotion in `#completeTurn`).
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-quiet-answer.test.ts` (4; the real `AcpAgentClient` with Antigravity's flavor over the `steer-read` fake `agy acp`, whose prompt stays open): a reply after a subagent call completes while the turn runs, a steer's reply gets its own item, the last answer is `final_answer`, each text completed once; a quiet reply that is the turn's last output becomes its answer (same id); chunks closer than the quiet time stay one reply; a Gemini failure answer stays open and the turn still fails. Without the fix the first three FAIL ("Timed out waiting for the quiet reply"). Unchanged: `fork-steer-read.test.ts` 4/4, `test:antigravity-steer`, `test:antigravity-ask-question`, `test:antigravity-subagent-permissions`, `test:antigravity-restart-keeps-subagents` PASS. `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, Biome clean.
- **Not done:** the turn itself still stays open while Antigravity keeps the prompt open (by design of #92 and the steer-by-prompt support), so a routine run or another queued message for that agent waits until it ends (Vegapunk's health-watch run queued at 15:14 UTC still waited at 16:22). Not tried against the real `agy` in a dev instance (it needs a signed-in Google account); the fake sends the same updates his log shows. A reply that pauses more than 10 s mid-text shows as two rows.
- **Check:** in a Gemini chat, ask for two background subagents, then send a question while they run: the answer shows about 10 s after its last word, not when the subagents finish.

## 109. The chat notes why it left the latest message (diagnosis of the "disappearing messages" jump)

- **Get:** when the desktop chat stops following the latest message or moves up by more than a screen without the reader's wheel, drag, key or touch, it writes one line to the main log (`logs/main/main.log`, tag `[chat-scroll]`): the reason (`reader-up`, `focus-in`, `message-focus`, `quote`, `unread-opening`, `set-leave-latest` with the caller, `unprompted-move`), the agent, the shown row count, the first and last row ids, whether the list is virtualized, and the scroll offsets. Ids, counts and offsets only, no message text; at most 60 lines a minute per window. Nothing changes on screen.
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026, "disappearing messages": two screenshots one second apart (16:37:50 → 16:37:51 local) of Vegapunk's chat. The first shows his newest messages at the bottom, but none of Vegapunk's replies between them (15:29–15:35 UTC) and no rows after his 15:35:06 message; the second shows a reply from 24 Nov 19:48 UTC (ordinal 2529, ~300 rows back; not 2686 as first thought) with its video player and the jump-to-latest button, i.e. the chat stopped following the latest message. The database holds every row; the cause could not be reproduced, so this adds the evidence the next occurrence needs instead of a guessed fix.
- **How (portable):** renderer `chat-scroll-log.ts` (`noteChatScroll`) prints a tagged `console.info` line; `conversation-scope.ts` calls it (`noteScroll`) at each place that sets `stickToLatest` to false and in `followReaderScroll` for an upward move larger than the viewport with no reader input. Main `fork-chat-scroll-log.ts` (`forwardChatScrollLog`) listens to `console-message` of the main window and of each chat window, keeps only tagged lines, caps them and writes them with the `chat-scroll` logger.
- **Diagnosis (25 Nov, read-only copies of his database and logs):**
  - Thread `openbot-thread-agent-c3ec…`: 3,971 rows, intact. Turn `9a6e959a…` open since 13:36 UTC (177 rows at 15:38: 127 commentary, 12 attachment rows, 26 steers, exchanges, 3 question prompts). A health-watch routine request queued at 15:14 sorts after the whole turn, so the latest page (`wholeGroups`) holds only that one row and the read cursor sits on it.
  - The trace log shows rapid chat switches at 15:37:37/39/42/46 (each `browser:set-visible` + `read-conversation-page`), a `preview-shared-file` at 15:37:46.8 (the video link of row 2529 rendered at the open), the attachment row 2835 at 15:37:49, and the next switch at 15:37:52.9. The `final_answer` that started at 15:38:03 came after the jump, so it is not the trigger.
  - Replayed his real rows through the real merge and projection code (`pageWindow`, `snapshotRows`, `narrationRows`, `placeUpdatesAfterSteers`) and through the whole app in Storybook on the mock host with a host that cuts pages and snapshots as the backend does (headless Chromium, 1600×1000): chat open, stay, leave and come back while rows arrive (inactive trim to 50 rows), open from an empty store (page = routine only, then a capped snapshot, then automatic older pages), scrolled back over older pages, window blur/focus, every row from 2828 to 2856 one by one (the list crossing 100 rows → virtualized). In every run all replies stayed and the view stayed at the bottom; no run showed the 15:37:50 state or the jump.
  - Ruled out: data loss; the out-of-order `created_at` of Antigravity replies (rows place by time: e.g. reply 2853 at 15:56:40 shows after his 15:56:05 steer); steers inside the old turn; the routine row (no `turnId`, not folded as a routine run); the unread opening (the backend read state is 0 because the cursor sits on the last row in shown order); a stale message focus request or search; the #95 page merge (a latest page that holds only the routine row joins, no tail is dropped while the routine is the last stored row).
  - Found on the way, now fixed elsewhere: Vegapunk's newest reply (row 2856, 15:59:25) stayed `streaming` until 16:04:41, and the "natural" style holds a streaming reply, so the latest answer was missing from the chat for minutes (fix #111, on `main`; this branch is rebased on it). Confirmed in the Storybook replay: before #111 the row never showed while streaming.
- **Here:** `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/chat-scroll-log.ts`, `conversation-scope.ts` (`noteScroll` and its calls), `src/main/fork-chat-scroll-log.ts`, wired in `src/main/main-window.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/main/fork-chat-scroll-log.test.ts` (2: only tagged lines pass; 60 a minute). Checked in Storybook that a wheel-up writes `reader-up` with offsets and row ids. `typecheck:renderer`, `typecheck:node`, Biome, `check:ui`.
- **Not done:** no fix for the jump itself (not reproduced). Open leads for the next log line: the screenshot shows the composer focused one second after the jump (a focus or window refocus right then); the first screenshot already lacked the turn's replies, as #95 did, though no replay reaches that state. Not addressed: at 16:04 the stored text of row 2856 briefly held only the text written after the pause (`turn-lifecycle.ts` assigns a message's text only from item events, line `if (isString(item.text)) message.text = item.text`, and the row kept its 15:59:25 `created_at`, so the short text came with an ACP item event, not from `handleStreamingDelta`'s append); the replay harness (Storybook story and node replay) stayed local and is not committed.
- **Check:** after the next jump, open `~/Library/Application Support/OpenBot/logs/main/main.log` and look for `[chat-scroll]` lines at that minute: the reason and offsets say which path moved the chat.
## 107. Channel-only partner access, default-closed roles and a per-channel outbound webhook

- **Get:** Server settings → Roles has a **Channels** list under **Agents** on the Everyone page and on each role page: per channel **No access**, **Can view** or **Can chat** (owners and admins always have full access; a member gets the highest of Everyone and each role that names them). A member with Agents = No access everywhere and Channels = Can chat on one channel sees that channel and nothing else: the channel is in the sidebar with every message of its agents, they send and `@`-mention (`@Traf`, `@everyone`), upload and open the channel's files, steer and cancel its tasks and answer the question cards and approvals of its agents' channel work; every 1:1 chat, agent route and agent event stays hidden (404). **Can view** reads the same and gets 403 on every write. **No access** hides the channel (404, not in the list or the events). New and unlisted agents and channels are closed to members (**default-closed**). Channel settings (name, title, instructions, picture, lead, members, memories, routines, archive, restore, delete, delete history) are owner/admin only: the Team API answers 403 and the desktop, web and phone hide or lock the controls. A member with channels but no agents opens straight in the first channel (desktop and web; the phone lists the channel with no "Add agent" scene). Channel settings of an owner or admin have an **Outbound webhook**: address, optional signing secret, on/off; when an agent finishes its answer in that channel, the host POSTs it as JSON.
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026 (spec `~/OpenBot/Shared/agency/ops/PARTNER_OPENBOT_SPEC.md`): invite his agency partner to `🎥 Agency` only, keep the 20 bots' 1:1 chats hidden, keep future bots and channels closed by default, keep channel settings his, and let a partner's bot listen to the channel's answers later.
- **How (portable):**
  - Levels: `ChannelAccessLevel` (`none | view | chat`, `packages/contracts/src/ipc-agent-access.ts`) lives in the same roles file as the agent levels: `AgentAccessState.everyoneChannels` and `AgentAccessRole.channels` (both optional, so a file without them reads as before and an older build ignores them). `channelAccessLevel()` (`src/main/agent-access-store.ts`) is "chat" for owners/admins, else the highest of Everyone and each role naming the member, default `none`. `agentAccessLevel()`'s unlisted default changed from `manage` to `none` (UI `everyoneLevel`, the phone's `use-server-roles.ts` and the audit diff's unlisted level too). The wire (`agent-access-v1`) gains optional `mineChannels`, `everyoneChannels` and `role.channels`; a save without them keeps the stored channel levels (an older client cannot wipe them) and drops levels of deleted channels. No new capability string: the Channels list shows when the admin snapshot has `everyoneChannels`.
  - Host gate: `TeamApiRequestContext.channelLevel(channelId)` and `threadChannelLevel(threadId)`. `requireVisibleChannel(context, channels, id, required)` answers 404 for `none` and 403 `error.team.channelViewOnly` below the required level; `requireChannelAdmin` answers 403 `error.team.channelSettingsAdminOnly`. Channel list, read and mark-read need `view`; send, request, stop, resume, reassign, cancel, clear/compact context and deleting one's own message need `chat`; save, archive, restore, delete, delete history, picture PUT/DELETE, memory and routine mutations (incl. `once` routines) need owner/admin; memory/routine/run lists and the picture GET need `view`. The old rule "a member sees a channel when one of its agents is visible" is gone; channel messages are no longer filtered by `hiddenAuthor` for the member's agent levels (only for agents the client's protocol cannot show). Every JSON reply of a channel route is filtered with the member's hidden agents minus the members of the channels the member sees, so channel member ids, authors and the lead stay in channel payloads.
  - Events: `channels-changed`, `channel-memories-changed` and `channel-routines-changed` skip a member whose level for that channel is `none`. `hiddenAgentView()` takes an `exposedThread` test: a hidden agent's `prompt` event, `approval` and `pendingPrompts`/`pendingApprovals`/`activeTurns` entries pass when their `threadId` is a thread of a channel the member sees; nothing else of that agent passes (its conversation snapshot, latest messages, queue, direct prompts). `/v1/pending-prompts`, `/v1/pending-approval` and `/v1/prompts|approvals/respond` accept a request whose thread is such a channel's thread (`chat` to answer, 403 for `view`). A question or approval of a channel thread follows the channel level alone: a member who can chat with the agent but has `none` for its channel neither gets it (events: `hiddenAgentView`'s `closedThread` test; the lists) nor answers it (404), and `view` answers 403 (`threadChannelLevel` is `null` for a thread that no channel owns). Phone notices (`PushNotificationService` `channelClosed`) and Live Activity questions/approvals (`LiveActivityPushViewer.closedThread`) of a channel thread skip such a member too. Approvals are not split by kind (spec Q9-C).
  - Attachments: `GET /v1/attachments/:id` and `/preview` answer 404 to a member unless the attachment is a draft (any member who has its random id, not only the uploader: drafts record no uploader), belongs to a message delivered to or sent by an agent the member can see, to a message or generated file of a channel thread the member sees, or appears in a message of a channel the member sees (`MailboxStore.forkAttachmentScope`, `forkAttachmentAllowed`). Phone video copies resolve to their source id first. Replies and events to a member who cannot administer carry no provider sign-in identity: the `email` of `auth`, of each provider row and of each provider account, and the account `label`, become `null` (`forkMemberProviderView`, `team-api/fork-member-redaction.ts`; team member emails stay). `GET /v1/analytics` for such a member counts only the agents they see (`readHost(input, onlyAgents)`): totals, days, models, provider days and rows agree; an `agentId` they cannot see is 404. `GET /v1/agents/conversation-reads` leaves out the agents a member cannot see (the reply is keyed by agent id, which the reply filter does not reach). `GET /v1/shared-files` keeps the upstream rule (any file of the shared folder) for owners, admins and members who see at least one agent; a member who sees no agent gets a shared file only when a message of a channel they see names it as `Shared/<path>` (`~/OpenBot/Shared/<path>` and absolute paths count), else 404 (`forkSharedFileMention`).
  - Agents in a member's channel turn: `#memberRequesters` now adds the remote people whose messages started or continued the channel task (`ChannelService.forkTaskPeople`, through the assignment of the delivery). `list_agents`, `read_agent` (now gated for every member-started turn) and `send_message` reach the sender, the channel's member agents and the agents where every requester has `chat`; others read as unknown ids. `list_channels` lists only the channels every requester sees. `send_message` and `send_to_channel` were already refused in channel turns. Channel management tools (`channel_add_member`, `channel_remove_member`, `channel_set_instructions`, routine create/update/delete) and `channel_forget_memory` are refused when the requester is a remote non-admin member (`personCanAdminister` hook); `channel_list_routines` and `channel_remember` stay. In every member-started turn, `interrupt_agent` keeps to the same reach (others read as unknown). A turn that a non-admin member started or joined (a 1:1 message or a channel task) cannot use the tools that set up the server or its agents: `create_agent`, `update_profile`, `update_agents`, `delete_agent`, `set_browser_profile`, `set_commit_co_author`, `create_channel`, `update_channel`, the section tools, `save_mcp_server`, `set_mcp_server_enabled`, `remove_mcp_server`, `save_api_key`, `remove_api_key`, the skill install/create/enable tools and agent routine create/update/delete; they answer "Only an owner or admin of this server can …" (`FORK_MEMBER_ADMIN_TOOLS` in `openbot-tool-router.ts`). Owner and admin turns are unchanged. A channel task that a remote member started or continued counts as a member's message for the workflow and CRM tools too (`#fromRemoteMember`), so `list_workflows`/`read_workflow` are refused there.
  - Outbound webhook: preference file `openbot-channel-webhooks-v1.json` (channel id → url, enabled, secret encrypted with the app's secret storage cipher, base64). Routes `POST /v1/channels/webhook` and `/v1/channels/webhook/set` (owner/admin, 403 for members, 404 when the channel is hidden), IPC `hostAdmin.getChannelWebhook/setChannelWebhook` with local and remote branches. Replies say `hasSecret`, never the secret; logs name only the address's origin. Address rules (`channelWebhookUrlProblem`): https, or http only to loopback, Tailscale `100.64.0.0/10` or `*.ts.net`; no user/password; metadata hosts, link-local (`169.254/16`, `fe80::/10`), `100.100.100.100`, multicast and unspecified are refused, also written as IPv4 inside IPv6 (`[::ffff:169.254.169.254]`, which the URL parser turns into `[::ffff:a9fe:a9fe]`, `::a.b.c.d`, `::ffff:0:a.b.c.d`, NAT64 `64:ff9b::/96`), and the delivery refuses an address whose DNS answer is in those networks. Trigger: `ChannelService.complete()` of a completed assignment picks the newest non-commentary, non-system agent answer of that turn (or its `channel_result`) and calls `forkHub.channelWebhooks.turnAnswered`. Delivery is fire-and-forget: POST JSON `{event: "channel.message", channelId, channelName, messageId, sequence, author, text, timestamp, taskId, origin}` with `X-OpenBot-Timestamp`, `X-OpenBot-Delivery` and, with a secret, `X-OpenBot-Signature-256: sha256=<hex HMAC-SHA256 of "<timestamp>.<delivery>.<body>">` (the inbound `webhook-security.ts` scheme), `redirect: "manual"`, 10 s timeout per attempt, 2 retries (2 s, 8 s) on a network error, timeout, 429 or 5xx, then one warning. Echo guard: only agent answers go out, never a person's or the inbound webhook's own post; `origin` says whether a member, a routine (an inbound webhook) or something else started the task; at most 30 deliveries per channel per minute.
  - Clients: `ServerRoles.tsx` (`ChannelAccessRow`, Channels section), `ServerRolesSection.tsx` loads the channel list. Channel header title is plain text for a non-admin (no settings pane), sidebar Edit/New channel hidden for non-admins (desktop, web). Web `/app` and desktop open the first channel for a member with no agents and do not open the first-agent setup or show its sidebar button; the phone's channel form is read-only for a member.
- **Migration:** none in the database. `openbot-agent-access-v1.json` keeps its shape (version 1); its new fields are optional. A server whose file has no Everyone level for an agent closes that agent to members on this build (was: open). Elias's Mac had no roles file on 25 Nov 2026, so nothing changes for him until he saves roles; an existing member elsewhere would lose agents until an admin opens them in Everyone.
- **Here:** contracts `ipc-agent-access.ts`, `team-protocol/agent-access-v1.ts`, `ipc-server-audit.ts`, `fork-channel-webhooks.ts` (new, subpath `@openbot/contracts/fork-channel-webhooks`), `team-protocol/optional-routes.ts`, `ipc-endpoints.ts`; host `src/main/agent-access-store.ts`, `team-api-server.ts`, `team-api/request-context.ts`, `team-api/fork-channel-access.ts` (new), `team-api/fork-route-channel-webhook.ts` (new), `team-api/route-channels.ts`, `route-storage.ts`, `route-chat-history.ts`, `route-routines-once.ts`, `route-message-delete.ts`, `route-files.ts`, `route-pending-prompts.ts`, `route-pending-approval.ts`, `route-agents.ts`, `route-agent-access.ts`, `provider-visibility.ts`, `dependencies.ts`, `route-live-activity-push.ts`, `fork-channel-webhooks.ts` (new), `push-notifications.ts`, `live-activity-push.ts`, `application-services.ts`, `host-service.ts`, `index.ts`, `ipc/host-admin-handlers.ts`; backend `channel-service.ts` (`forkTaskPeople`, admin-only management tools, `#forkAnnounceAnswer`), `agent/fork-channel-reach.ts` (new), `agent/openbot-tool-router.ts`, `agent-service.ts`, `mailbox-store.ts` (`forkAttachmentScope`), `fork-hub.ts`; preload `index.ts`; renderer `ServerRolesSection.tsx`, `WorkspaceOverlays.tsx`, `app-port.ts`, `WorkspaceShell.tsx`, `agents-context.tsx`, `WorkspaceSidebar.tsx`, channels `ChannelConversationParts.tsx`, `channel-conversation.tsx`, `WorkspaceChannelConversation.tsx`, `ChannelEditor.tsx`, `ChannelWebhookSettings.tsx` (new), `channels-port.ts`, `channels-context.tsx`, `channels.css`, web client `WebWorkspace.tsx`, `web-channels-runtime.ts`, preview `mock-openbot.ts`; UI `ServerRoles.tsx`, `SidebarChannelContextMenu.tsx`; mobile `use-server-roles.ts`, `channel-form-screen.tsx`; e2e `agent-access-e2e.ts`, `team-api-routes-e2e.ts` (open-by-default shim for checks that predate #107), `queue-window-e2e.ts`, `team-session-end-e2e.ts`. i18n: `error.team.channelViewOnly`, `error.team.channelSettingsAdminOnly`, `error.team.channelWebhookInvalid`, `server.roles.channels`, `server.roles.noChannels`, `server.roles.channelLevel`, `channel.webhook.*`, English only (listed in `untranslated.json`).
