- **Tests:** `src/main/fork-channel-access.test.ts` (9: the real `TeamApiServer` over HTTP with an owner, a chat member, a view member and a no-access member: default-closed agents and channels, channel levels saved through `agent-access-v1` and kept when an older client saves agents only, list/read 200/200/200/404 with the agents' messages, mark-read and cancel by level, settings saves, archive, memories, routines, picture and delete 403 for members, channel files 200/200/200/404, direct agent routes 404, attachment downloads scoped (channel file 200/200/200/404, direct files 200/404/404/404), webhook settings 200/403/403/404 with no secret in the reply and a metadata address refused), `src/main/fork-channel-access-store.test.ts` (4: unlisted levels, highest level wins, roles file keeps channel levels and drops deleted channels, channel-thread prompts pass the hidden-agent filter and nothing else of that agent), `src/main/fork-channel-webhooks.test.ts` (8: one signed POST with the payload, `redirect: "manual"` and a timeout signal, no secret in the file or the logs; no POST for a person's or the coordinator's message; 30 per minute per channel; 2 bounded retries on timeout/5xx with the path left out of the log; no retry on 4xx; a DNS answer in a blocked network refused; address rules; secret kept or cleared), `src/backend/fork-channel-partner.test.ts` (5: the remote member of a channel task from its delivery, local person not remote, `list_agents` reach in a channel turn, `channel_set_instructions` refused for a non-admin member, the turn's final answer reaches the webhook port). Tester additions (25 Nov 2026): `fork-channel-partner.test.ts` +2 (a member's `channel_forget_memory` refused, an admin's allowed), `fork-channel-access.test.ts` +2 (read states keep hidden agents out; shared files of a member without agents only when a channel message names them) and the IPv4-in-IPv6 webhook address, member analytics counting only visible agents and provider identities hidden from members (+2), `fork-channel-access-store.test.ts` +2 (closed channel's questions/approvals dropped also for a visible agent; an older roles file without channel levels), `fork-channel-webhooks.test.ts` (IPv4-in-IPv6 and NAT64 cases), `src/main/fork-channel-push.test.ts` (new, 2: no phone notice about a closed channel); the e2e adds `update_agents`/`interrupt_agent` of a hidden agent, a partner's `channel_forget_memory`, `create_agent`, `update_profile` and `save_mcp_server` (refused), and the owner's `update_agents` and `update_profile`. The webhook form names the reason of a refused address (`channel.webhook.blocked`, `channel.webhook.credentials`). Run: `bunx vitest run src/backend/fork-channel-partner.test.ts src/main/fork-channel-access.test.ts src/main/fork-channel-access-store.test.ts src/main/fork-channel-webhooks.test.ts src/main/fork-channel-push.test.ts` (36 PASS; the builder's run had 26); with the related suites (`fork-channel-*`, `src/main/ipc`, `ipc-channel-coverage`, `trusted-ipc`, `src/preload`, `channel-feed`, `fork-subagent-push`, `fork-notification-preview`, `fork-phone-video`) 37 files, 572 PASS; `fork-network-resume`, `fork-whatsapp-watch-placement`, `fork-retry-handoff` 23 PASS. E2E: `bun scripts/channel-partner-access-e2e.ts` (new: real Team API over HTTP, `AgentService` with a fake Codex that calls OpenBot tools, a local webhook listener; 44 checks PASS: the 200/403/404 matrix, a signed webhook POST of Traf's answer, `list_agents`/`read_agent`/`list_channels`/`channel_set_instructions` in the partner's channel turn), `bun scripts/agent-access-e2e.ts` PASS (updated for default-closed), `bun run test:team-api-routes` PASS (678 checks; a shim keeps the old open default for checks that predate #107, and the member picture and `once` routine checks now expect 403), `bun run test:agent-message-access` PASS. `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, `check:ui`, `i18n:check`, Biome on the changed files: PASS (remaining warnings: two upstream `noConfusingVoidType` in `channel-service.ts`, and the test's `vi.mock` of `DEFAULT_TEAM_MEMBER_LIMIT`, which has no seam). Proof: `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/openbot-107/`.
- **Not done:** channel push notifications and Live Activities for a member who cannot see the channel's agents (push stays agent-based, so such a member gets none). The phone has no Channels list in its Roles editor and no webhook editor (desktop and web do), and it lands on the home list rather than inside the channel. The fork's web client is not what `openbot.run/app` serves: a partner on the public web client or the store phone app gets the host's rules (404/403) but not the fork's landing or locked controls. The channel picker for `@` lists only agents the client knows, so a channel-only member types `@Traf` (the host resolves typed names) and sees authors by name without avatars. A member who creates an agent cannot see it until an admin opens it (default-closed). The roles audit log records agent levels only, not channel levels. The DNS check of the webhook runs before the POST, not inside the socket (a rebinding host can still race it). `agent-input-resolved` of a hidden agent does not reach a channel member; the next runtime snapshot clears an answered card. A wait that wakes a turn keeps the old reach rules. Host analytics count usage per agent, so a channel turn of an agent the member sees also counts when the channel is closed to them. A channel-only member gets 404 for a workspace file link of a channel agent (`/v1/workspace-files` checks the agent level) and for a shared file named without `Shared/`. A channel's webhook entry stays in the preference file after the channel is deleted. An inbound-webhook post that an agent answers goes out on the outbound webhook (`origin: "routine"`), so two bots that answer each other loop until the 30-per-minute cap. The member's view of the fork web client (landing in the channel, sending, attachments) was not checked in a GUI: the dev Auth API refuses an email invitation for a local dev host, so only the owner's GUI and the member's API were checked against the running dev app.
- **Check:** in Server settings → Roles make role "Agency": all agents No access, Channels → `agency` Can chat; invite a member, give them Agency. Sign in as the member: only `agency` shows, it opens at once, agents' messages are there, a send with `@Traf` works, `GET /v1/agents` is `[]`, an agent route is 404, the channel's settings are not clickable. Set the channel to Can view: a send answers 403. Owner: channel settings → Outbound webhook → `https://…` + secret → Save; an agent answer in the channel POSTs a signed JSON.


## 112. A quote or selection action in the channel thread panel replies in that thread

- **Get:** in a channel's Feed view (#105), select text in a message inside the open thread panel. The selection bar (Describe edits · Quote · Explain · Improve) now belongs to the panel. **Quote** (also ⌘⇧. and Enter in the empty "Describe edits" box) puts the quote chip in the panel's **Reply…** box, which takes the focus; the send is a reply to the thread's root, so it and the bots' answers land in that thread (its count goes up, no new feed row). **Explain**, **Improve**, the other presets and a typed edit (Enter or the arrow) send at once as a reply to the root. A quote waits in the thread's own draft like typed text (kept per thread until OpenBot quits). Text outside the panel keeps the conversation's bar as before.
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026 (screenshot of 🎥 Agency): he quoted a passage of Traf's answer in the thread panel and wrote "why not 2 ? …"; it became a new root post in the feed with the quote chip, not a reply in the thread.
- **How (portable):** root cause: the conversation mounts one selection bar for the whole view (`ConversationView.tsx`, `MessageSelectionActions` with `onQuote={quoteSelection}`). Its quote goes to the main composer through the scope's `quoteRequest` (`conversation-scope.ts` `quoteSelection`), and in Feed view that composer is "Start a thread in …": a send with no reply target is a new root. The panel's composer had no quote input. Fix, in the side thread's pattern (`AgentThreadView`): the panel's scroll area is its own selection scope (`data-selection-scope`), so the conversation's bar ignores a selection there, and the panel mounts its own `MessageSelectionActions` with that scope: `onQuote` feeds a `quoteRequest` to the panel's `ComposerEditor`, `onSend` is `sendReply(rootId, body)`. No `onQueueNote`, so a typed edit sends at once (the panel box has no note cards). No upstream file changed.
- **Feed rows:** a row's text cannot be selected (#105 makes it `inert` under the row's button, and `RichMessageText` has no `data-selection-message-id`), so the bar never opens on a row: click the row and select in the panel. That keeps a row a single click target, which avoids a second route for the same action.
- **Here:** `src/renderer/src/features/channels/ChannelFeedView.tsx` (`ChannelThreadPanel`, now exported: the selection scope, its bar and `quoteRequest`; `ThreadComposer` takes `quoteRequest`).
- **Tests:** `src/renderer/src/features/channels/ChannelThreadQuote.test.tsx` (4, jsdom: the real `ChannelThreadPanel` with the conversation's own bar beside it; Quote from a panel answer fills the panel box and its thread draft and is sent with the root as reply target, the feed's quote and send untouched; Explain is sent into the thread; Enter in the empty describe field quotes into the panel box; a selection outside the panel still goes to the conversation's bar). Without the fix the first three fail. Channel and conversation renderer suites with `fork-channel-threads`: 26 files, 154 tests PASS. `typecheck:renderer`, `typecheck:node`, `check:ui`, `i18n:check`, Biome on the changed files: PASS.
- **Smoke test:** isolated dev instance (own `HOME`/`CFFIXED_USER_HOME`, seeded profile, agents on the bench's fake Codex), Launch room in Feed with the "Draft the release note" thread open. Before (driven with cua-driver): select "The retention claim" in Research's answer in the panel, Quote: the chip went to "Start a thread in Launch room"; sent, it became a new feed row (5:47 PM) and the panel jumped to it. After (same steps over the dev instance's CDP input, because the desktop was in use and a foreground cua drag took its keyboard): the chip shows in the panel's Reply… box, the feed composer stays empty; sent, the thread went from 2 to 4 replies (the reply and Launch's answer in the panel), still 4 feed rows. Shots: `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/openbot-112/`.
- **Not done:** the panel still has no note cards ("Add to message" in Chat): a typed edit in the panel's bar sends at once. A quote in Chat view sent from the feed composer after switching to Feed stays a new root (its reply target is not set by a quote). Not run in a packaged build; the web client was not checked.
- **Check:** open a channel in Feed, open a thread, select text in an answer in the panel, press Quote, type, Enter: the message shows in the panel and the row's count goes up, with no new row in the feed.


## 114. A reply with text is as wide as any other reply, also when it shows an image it names

- **Get:** an agent reply that names an image file in its text (for example `[shot.png](file:///…/shot.png)`), and so shows that image under the text, is as wide as a normal reply (up to the wide reply width, 720 px in a 840 px column) instead of 420 px. The image keeps its own size (420 px; 480 px for a video generation; 520 px for a gallery of several images). Replies that are only an image, video or gallery keep their current size.
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026, screenshot of Vegapunk's chat (Gemini/Antigravity, row 2836 `…:assistant:332`, `commentary`, 15:39 UTC): the long reply under the image of 3 stacked frames was about as narrow as the image and left the right half of the column empty. He also read it as "the first letter of each line is cut off". On his screenshot, that is the left edge of his own red box drawn over the text: zoomed in, "Attached", "the tapered" and "spark/flicker" are whole. In the replay of his text no paragraph, list item, inline code or bold run started left of the bubble edge, as a commentary row or as a final answer.
- **How (portable):** root cause: `messages.css` sized the whole bubble by its media: `.message-entry .ui-bubble:has(.image-generation) { width: min(420px, 100%); max-width: min(420px, 100%) }`, with the same rule for `.media-generation` (480 px) and `.image-gallery` (520 px). A reply whose text names an image file gets that image in the same bubble (`MessageRendering.tsx`, `namedImageFiles` → `fileImages` → `.message-image-attachments` > `ImageGeneration`), so `:has()` also matched long text replies. Commentary (narration) rows and final answers render alike, so both were narrow. Fix: the three rules apply only to a bubble without text (`:where(:not(:has(.message-markdown)))`, so their specificity is unchanged and the video grid of #82 still wins for ghost bubbles). In a bubble with text, the image block takes the media width instead (`> .ui-bubble-content > .message-image-attachments` 420 px, `.media-generation` 480 px, `.image-gallery` 520 px).
- **Here:** `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/styles/messages.css` (two blocks marked `Fork (docs/fork/FEATURES.md entry 114)`; "entry" because `check:ui` counts `#114` as a hex colour literal).
- **Tests:** CSS only, no unit test. Replayed on an isolated dev instance (own `HOME`/`CFFIXED_USER_HOME`, `dev:seed`, plus his rows 2834–2837 with a placeholder 1952×1262 image and an mp4, once as `commentary` and once as a final answer). Measured over CDP: before, both text bubbles were 420 px in an 839 px column; after, 720 px, the image 420 px; at a 960 px window 599 px (the full column). An image-only bubble stayed at 420 px and the image + video attachment bubble stayed at the full column. Dark and light. Before/after screenshots: `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/openbot-114/`. `typecheck:renderer`, `typecheck:node`, `check:ui` PASS; Biome adds no warning (7 before and after).
- **Not done:** the phone app (React Native) has its own message layout and is not changed. The channel feed and thread panel (#105) and the web client use the same `ChatMessageRow` and CSS, but were not opened with such a reply. Nothing changed for the left edge, because nothing was cut there.
- **Check:** ask an agent to reply with a long bulleted text that links a PNG from the shared folder: the reply fills the column like other replies and the image under it stays at 420 px.


## 113. Agents on a second provider account continue after a restart, and a message written first says what the restart stopped

- **Get:** after an OpenBot restart, an agent on a second provider account (Gotham, Owl, Adrian, Tapa… 14 of the 21 agents on 25 Nov) continues by itself like the others: the "🔄 Resumes after the OpenBot restart in a few seconds" wait closes about 10 s after the start and "🔄 Resumed after restart" runs, with the stopped background subagents by agent ID and "continue each with SendMessage". When the user writes before that (or a message waited in the queue), the message runs instead, as before, but the agent now gets a note before it: "[OpenBot: 🔄 restart note] At 17:40 on 25 Nov an OpenBot restart stopped your work: your background subagents were stopped while you waited for them … These subagents were stopped mid-task: a8d31d88f5a7d0d48 — Fork #112 quote in thread pane; … Answer the user's message first. Then, unless that message replaces or cancels the stopped work, continue it … SendMessage to its agent ID … The request that was stopped: …". The wait closes with it. Cancel keeps working: a cancelled wait gives no wake and no note.
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026, after rebuilding and restarting OpenBot at 17:41 local: "why is stuff not auto resuming after i restarted openbot?". Gotham's chat showed "1 open wait — 🔄 Resumes after the OpenBot restart in a few seconds" still "Waiting" 37 s after the start; its two background Claude Code subagents (#112, #107) stayed stopped, and Gotham's turn only ran when Elias wrote. Evidence (read only: `fork-restart-resume.json`, `logs/main/main.log`, `logs/providers/providers.log`, a copy of the database):
  - The note was captured at 16:42:01Z (`subagents:51b2f029…`, both agent IDs known), the providers were up at 16:42:02–07, no MCP server or CLI update was waited for (the "MCP server not started: windows_pc" toast at 16:42:13 is the Gemini agent Vegapunk's own process, a separate failure). Gotham's own Claude process started only at 16:42:35.3, for Elias's message (delivery `2d703b6d…` created 16:42:35.07); the note ended `skipped: another turn started since the restart`.
  - Cause: `ForkRestartResume.#fire` waits for `hooks.providerReady`, which required `ProviderRuntime.clientForAgent(agent)` (`agent-service.ts` `#restartResumeHooks`). For an agent with `providerAccountId` (or a confined workspace) `clientForAgent` returns null until `ensureAgentClient` starts the agent's own process (`provider-runtime.ts` `clientForAgent`: `#ownProcessFor(agent) ? null : …`), and only a turn calls that. So the wake retried every 3 s for `PROVIDER_WAIT_MS` (10 min) and then wrote "🔄 Not resumed after the restart: claude was not ready." The same day's 13:05 restart shows exactly that in Gotham's chat at 13:15:41Z (= start + 10 s grace + 10 min); Owl got the same line on 6 Nov. The e2e reproduces it: before the fix the account agent's note ends `skipped: provider not ready` with "codex was not ready".
  - Second gap: a note taken over by a message of the user (`another turn started since the restart`, `a queued message runs instead`, `another turn runs`) was dropped without a word (entry 42 "No line for the quiet skips"), so the user's turn did not know about the stopped turn or subagents.
  - How often: every restart that stopped work of an agent on a second account. 22 app starts since 6 Nov; 4 wakes ran (Adrian 12 Nov, Tapa 20 and 22 Nov, Gotham 22 Nov, each on the shared process at that time: no own-process start in the provider log for them before the wake), 2 "was not ready" lines (Owl 6 Nov, Gotham 25 Nov 13:15), and 25 Nov 16:42 Gotham waited until the user wrote. Not a timer or MCP problem: the grace (10 s after the boot step "arm routines") and the provider wait were as designed.
- **How (portable):** (1) `providerReady` is true when the provider is ready and the agent's account is signed in and there is either the agent's client or the provider's shared client (`clientForAgent(agent) ?? currentClient(provider)`); the wake's own turn starts the agent's process through the drain (`ensureAgentClient`), as any message does. Still bounded by `PROVIDER_WAIT_MS`. (2) Reading a Claude session's subagent IDs and outcomes needs the agent's own config folder, so those hooks start the agent's client (`ensureAgentClient`) when it has none; a failed start reads nothing, as before. (3) `ForkRestartResume.handOverNote({agentId, deliveryId, typed})`, called from `deliveryPromptInput` for every user message (after the fork's own prompt changes); it acts only when the delivery is one of the agent's own-chat deliveries (`unresolvedOwnDeliveries`: not a task, channel or messaging one). It takes the restart notes of the own chat (not process stops, entry 47 has its own line) that are pending (also from an earlier start that stopped before its wake) or that a message took over in this run (kept in memory), closes their wait, marks them skipped ("another turn started since the restart", so no wake follows) and handed to that delivery (`handedTo`; the same delivery's prompt built again gets the same note, another message none), and puts `restartHandOverText` before the user's text with the current-message separator. Not for a slash command (the provider runs it only at the start of the prompt), a task or channel thread, a note skipped in an earlier run, a routine run, a question, a cancel, or `/autoresume off`. The note's header is "[OpenBot: 🔄 restart note]", not the wake's. Claude: subagents by agent ID with `SendMessage`; Antigravity: start a new subagent for the work left; other providers: no subagent part.
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent-service.ts` (`#restartResumeHooks`: `providerReady`, `restartResumeClient`); `src/backend/fork-restart-resume.ts` (`handOverNote`, `restartHandOverText`, `#handOver`, `handedTo` on the note, `QUEUED_MESSAGE_RUNS`); `src/backend/agent/delivery-content.ts` (`deliveryPromptInput`).
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-restart-resume.test.ts` (18, 7 new: the note's text for Claude, Codex and Antigravity; given once per delivery and the wait closed; none for a slash command, a task-thread message or when off; a queued message gets it; a note still pending from a start that stopped before its wake gets handed over; a routine run's note is not). With `fork-restart-resume-projection.test.ts` and `fork-retry-handoff.test.ts`: 29/29. `bun scripts/restart-resume-e2e.ts` (20 cases, 1 new): new case T, an agent on a second Codex account whose first turn ran on its own process is resumed in the same provider thread on its own process (before the fix: `skipped: provider not ready` and "codex was not ready"); case K now also checks that the user's message that came first carries the note before its text and that the wait closed (before the fix: no note). I2 holds another marker, because the note repeats the stopped request. Before the fix 18/20 (T, K fail), after 20/20. `bun scripts/subagent-stop-resume-e2e.ts` (entry 47): 11/11. `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, Biome: PASS. Smoke test on an isolated dev instance (own `HOME` and `CFFIXED_USER_HOME`, own ports, `dev:seed`, the bench fake Codex, a seeded agent "Chief" on a second Codex account; driven with `dev:automation`): Chief mid-turn on its own process, OpenBot quit ("Quitting: quit."), started again: the note was captured at boot recovery (17:11:13.25Z), Codex was up at 17:11:13.47Z, and "🔄 Resumed after restart" started Chief's own process and its turn at 17:11:27.15Z, in the same provider thread, with the wake text and the request. Shots and logs: `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/openbot-113/`.
- **Limits:** a Claude Code background subagent dies with its `claude` process when OpenBot quits (the CLI is OpenBot's child; Claude Code has no detached background tasks), so nothing keeps it running across a restart; only its transcript stays, and the parent's `SendMessage` to its agent ID continues it, in the same session only. Codex and other ACP subagents cannot be continued at all. Task threads are still not resumed (entry 42). The note is given once: when that user turn fails before it runs, the note is gone. A user who writes only a slash command first gets no note. The 10-minute provider wait stays for a provider that really is not up.
- **Check:** in an agent on a second account: "start a background subagent that writes /tmp/rr-1..5.txt, one per 20 s, then wait for it". Quit OpenBot and open it again: within ~10 s the wait closes and "🔄 Resumed after restart" runs; the agent calls `SendMessage` to the subagent's agent ID. Again, but write "hi" right after the start: the agent answers and mentions/continues the stopped subagent; the wait is gone.

## 115. A scroll up while an agent works stays up: the chat no longer pulls the reader back to the bottom

- **Get:** in a chat whose agent is working (a reply streaming, steps or a timer updating), a wheel, trackpad, key or touch scroll up leaves the latest message and stays where the reader put it, also when the renderer is busy or its frames come late; the jump-to-latest button shows. Scrolling back down to the bottom follows the latest message again.
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026: "when I scroll up to see the older messages and scroll down to the bottom, that's a bug that I really don't like", seen in Vegapunk's chat (Antigravity, a turn open for hours, long rows, many videos and images).
- **Repro (isolated dev instance, `HOME`/`CFFIXED_USER_HOME=/tmp/openbot115/home`, `dev:seed` plus a 664-row chat built from the shape of Vegapunk's last 773 rows (authors, item types, turns, steers, text lengths, image and video counts, no text copied), the agent on the bench's fake Codex with a 6-minute stream, real wheel input with cua-driver):**
  - Three round trips up past several older pages (h 7.5k → 114k px) and back down, one of them while a reply streamed, were clean: no row moved on screen without input, the bottom was reached and kept (a row-anchored trace of every frame).
  - While a reply streamed, each scroll up of 1 or 3 wheel notches from the bottom was undone 200-400 ms later: scrollTop went back to the end, no jump button, and the new log line said `unprompted-move`, `stickToLatest: true`, `inputAge` 850-1090 ms. The same happened to a 32,000 px scroll up made of 8 bursts (`moved: -32000`). With 300 ms of renderer work after each wheel event it happened for every notch.
- **Root cause:** `followReaderScroll` (`conversation-scope.ts`, old line 2349) counted a move as the reader's only when the scroll event came within 250 ms (`READER_INPUT_WINDOW_MS`) of the wheel, key or touch. The scroll that the input causes reaches the page in one of the next frames; when those frames come late (a busy renderer, frames held back), it came after 250 ms, so a move up was not the reader's: `stickToLatest` stayed true and the next change of the working turn (`glideConversationBottom` from the effect on the last message, or the resize observer) took the view back to the end. The other way, a late scroll down to the bottom did not clear `readerLeftLatest`, so the chat did not follow the latest again. An idle chat has no such change, which is why it showed in the chat of an agent that is always working.
- **How (portable):** an input stays the reader's for 250 ms and also until 3 animation frames have run after it, whatever their time (`createReaderScrollIntent`: `note(up)` on wheel/key/touch, `current()` in the scroll handler; a frame counter that runs only after an input, so an idle chat schedules nothing). A scroll that an input caused lands in the first frame or two after it, so it counts however late that frame is; a move the chat makes itself later still does not. The scroll log's `unprompted-move` now also fires for a move up of more than 80 px while the chat follows the latest (it was: more than a screen) and carries `inputAge` (ms since the last input) and `maxShrunk` (how far the end moved up, i.e. content that shrank), so a pull-back that still happens says which case it is.
- **Here:** `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/reader-scroll-intent.ts` (new), `conversation-scope.ts` (`readerIntent`, `followReaderScroll`, `LATEST_MARGIN_PX`), `chat-scroll-log.ts` (`inputAge`, `maxShrunk`).
- **Tests:** `src/renderer/src/fork-reader-scroll-intent.test.ts` (6): a scroll 400 ms after the wheel with no frame between counts; a scroll in the frame after the input counts 2 s later; after 3 frames and 250 ms it does not; the 250 ms window stays after the frames; a new input restarts the count and sets the direction. With the old rule (0 frames) 3 of them FAIL. Unchanged: `fork-chat-scroll-log.test.ts`, `fork-long-turn-replies.test.ts`, `ChannelThreadQuote.test.tsx` (28 PASS). `typecheck:renderer`, `typecheck:node`, `check:ui`, Biome on the changed files: PASS.
- **Proof:** `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/openbot-115/`: `before-*` (main), `oldrule-*` (this branch with 0 frames, i.e. the old rule, same page state as `after-*`) and `after-*` (fix), each a screenshot just after the third scroll and at the end, plus a frame trace of scrollTop, scrollHeight, distance from the bottom and the jump button. Before and old rule: distance from the bottom 240 → 0 after every scroll, jump button never shown. After: 240, 480, … 1200, button shown, `reader-up` logged.
- **Not done:** not reproduced in Elias's own app (the live app predates #109's log and was not touched); in the sandbox the late frames came from the dev window being behind other windows and from a simulated 300 ms of renderer work, so it is not proven that Vegapunk's chat in his app stalls that long. If it still happens, `[chat-scroll] unprompted-move` lines in `logs/main/main.log` at that minute say how late the input was (`inputAge`) or that there was none (a scroll that came without a wheel on the chat, e.g. from an embedded page). Scrolling up in a virtualized chat can still shift rows a little when a row above grows (TanStack does not correct a re-measure while scrolling backward); scrolling down was clean in every run.
- **Check:** open an agent while it works, scroll up a few notches from the bottom: the view stays and the jump-to-latest button shows; scroll back down to the end: new output follows again.

## 116. Windows partner install: a taskbar OpenBot that updates from the fork's main on every click

- **What:** a partner's Windows PC runs Elias's fork, not the official release, and gets each push to `main` on the next click of its taskbar OpenBot, the Windows twin of the Mac Dock starter (`scripts/fork/app.sh`). No build tools on that PC: GitHub builds it.
- **How (portable):** on each push to `main` that changes the app, a Windows runner runs upstream's `package:win` (unsigned `dist/win-unpacked`), zips it, installs it through the real launcher from the staged files (shortcuts, a second run that must not reinstall), runs upstream's `verify:package:win` (file checks and a launch) on that installed copy, then uploads the zip, `launcher.ps1` and last `commit.txt` to the rolling prerelease `fork-windows`. The launcher (Windows PowerShell 5.1) compares `commit.txt` with `installed-commit`; when newer it shows a progress window (with "Open without updating"), downloads, extracts to `app-new`, swaps folders and refreshes itself, then opens `OpenBot.exe`. A running OpenBot is never replaced (the click brings it forward); no network or a failed download opens the installed copy. The first install copies an existing `%APPDATA%\OpenBot\openbot.db*` to a backup folder. The Start menu and desktop shortcuts carry the app id `app.openbot.desktop` (the one `src/main/index.ts` sets), so a pinned shortcut and the running window share one taskbar button. Upstream's updater stays off (#1), so the official release never replaces it.
- **Here:** `.github/workflows/fork-windows.yml`, `scripts/fork/windows/launcher.ps1`, `scripts/fork/windows/install.ps1`. Files in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\OpenBotFork` (`app`, `installed-commit`, `launcher.log`, `backup-*`).
- **Install (partner, PowerShell, no admin):** `irm https://raw.githubusercontent.com/eliasx45/openbot/main/scripts/fork/windows/install.ps1 | iex`, then right-click OpenBot in the Start menu > Pin to taskbar (Windows has no supported way for a script to pin).
- **Check:** Actions > Fork Windows build is green for the pushed commit; `https://github.com/eliasx45/openbot/releases/download/fork-windows/commit.txt` shows that commit; on the PC, `launcher.log` says `installed <commit>` after a click.

## 117. A member sees only the sidebar folders of chats they can open, and cannot change the folders

- **What:** a member who cannot administer the server (a partner invited with channel-only access, #107) no longer sees the host's folder names. Their sidebar has only the folders that hold an agent or a channel they can open, with those folders' parents. Empty folders and folders of hidden chats are left out. Folder changes by such a member (create, rename, delete, move, nest, assign) answer 403. Owners and admins see and change everything as before.
- **Why:** Elias, 25 Nov 2026. Hamza joined with no agents and saw all of Elias's folders (Editing, Clients, Specialists, Lab & Forge, Agency, Engineering, School), empty: "should not see them even if bots hidden". The layout is the server's, so a member's folder change also changed Elias's sidebar.
- **How (portable):** the host sent the whole `SidebarLayoutSnapshot` to every member: the `/v1/sidebar-folders` reply, the `sidebar-layout-changed` / `sidebar-folders-changed` events, and every reply that carries a layout (e.g. agent duplication). `hiddenAgentView` drops hidden agent ids but not sections. `forkMemberSidebarView(value, visibleChat)` (`src/main/team-api/fork-member-sidebar.ts`) finds each layout in a reply or event (`isSidebarLayoutSnapshot`) and keeps the assignments and order of visible chats, the sections they are in and those sections' ancestors, and the fixed `people` / `unassigned` entries, so the order stays complete. `visibleChat` (`#memberSidebarChat` in `team-api-server.ts`) is a channel the member sees (`#visibleChannelIds`) or a known agent not in the member's hidden set. It runs after `forkMemberProviderView` in `#json` (non-admin replies: `route.sidebarChat`) and in `#peerView` (events to a non-admin connection). `routeSidebarFolders` calls `requireAdmin` before any action; the read stays open.
- **Here:** `src/main/team-api/fork-member-sidebar.ts` (new), `src/main/team-api-server.ts` (`sidebarChat` on the response route, `#memberSidebarChat`, `#json`, `#peerView`), `src/main/team-api/route-sidebar-folders.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/main/fork-channel-access.test.ts` +3 (real `TeamApiServer` over HTTP: the owner makes Agency (the agency channel), Clients (a hidden agent), Private room (a closed channel), School (empty) and Specialists (empty, inside Agency); the owner reads all five, the chat and view members read only Agency with only the channel's assignment and none of the other names in the reply, the no-access member reads no folder; create, rename and delete by each member answer 403 and the owner's folders stay; the helper keeps a visible chat's folder with its parent inside an event). Without the fix the two server tests fail. With it: that file and `fork-channel-access-store`, `fork-channel-partner` 29 PASS; `bun run test:team-api-routes`, `bun scripts/channel-partner-access-e2e.ts`, `bun scripts/agent-access-e2e.ts` PASS; `typecheck:node` and Biome on the changed files PASS.
- **Not done:** not checked on a member's screen (Hamza's PC) yet; a client that cached the old layout shows the new one after its next read or folder event. A member's "Create your first agent" button still creates an agent on the host (not changed here).
- **Check:** as a member with only 🎥 Agency, the sidebar shows only the folder that holds 🎥 Agency (or none if it is outside folders); dragging a folder answers an error and the owner's sidebar is unchanged.

## 118. A member cannot add agents on the host and sees none of the host's AI account limits

- **What:** a member who cannot administer the server gets 403 when creating an agent (`POST /v1/agents`, also the "Create your first agent" flow), duplicating one, or importing one (agent-import v1 and v2). The member also reads no AI account usage: `GET /v1/agents/usage` answers `{ limits: [] }`, usage credits `{ accounts: [] }`, the usage-limit list empty lists, `usage-changed` events are not sent, and runtime snapshots leave out `usageLimits`, `usageLimitedModels` and `failedModels`. Owners and admins are unchanged.
- **Why:** Elias, 26 Nov 2026. Hamza (a member with only the 🎥 Agency channel) clicked "Create your first agent" while his app showed Elias's server and made "Bruce wayne" on Elias's Mac, on Elias's Claude account with full access and computer use. Elias: block it, and "no need to show the ai accounts limits for them.. keep it super lean".
- **How (portable):** `requireAdmin(member)` in `route-agents.ts` (create, duplicate) and `route-agent-import.ts` (every import route); `canAdministerServer` early answers in `route-agents.ts` (usage), `route-usage-credits.ts`, `route-agent-usage-limit.ts` (list); `#eventFor` empties a `usage-changed` event for a non-admin connection (the fan-out already skips an emptied usage event) and `#memberRuntimeSnapshot` strips the limit fields from both runtime-snapshot paths in `team-api-server.ts`.
- **Here:** `src/main/team-api/route-agents.ts`, `route-agent-import.ts`, `route-usage-credits.ts`, `route-agent-usage-limit.ts`, `src/main/team-api-server.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/main/fork-channel-access.test.ts` +2 (real `TeamApiServer` over HTTP: chat, view and no-access members get 403 on create with the exact Bruce wayne body and are refused a duplicate, the agent count stays; each member reads `{ limits: [] }`). Without the fix the create test fails. `scripts/team-api-routes-e2e.ts`: the usage, credits and usage-limit reads now run as the admin with a new member check each, and the import tests run as the admin with a member's part answering 403 adminRequired; 680 PASS (main: all PASS before, 3 usage/credits + 7 import checks failed with the fix until updated). `fork-*` tests 48 PASS, `channel-partner-access-e2e` and `agent-access-e2e` PASS, `typecheck:node` and Biome PASS.
- **Not done:** the member's app still shows the "Create your first agent" button; pressing it now shows the host's 403 error. Hiding the button is a client change that Hamza would get with the next Windows build.
- **Check:** as a member, "Create your first agent" fails and no agent appears in the owner's list; the member's model menu and usage view show no limits.

## 119. Your own messages stay in a long turn: the newest page holds the end of a turn that is too big for one page

- **What:** when an agent's turn has more than 100 rows (a Gemini agent you steer for hours) and a message sits after it, the newest page now holds that message plus the newest rows of the turn, with the messages you sent into it (steers). Before, it held only the message after the turn, so the chat showed the agent's replies (kept from live state by #95) but none of your messages in that turn.
- **Why:** Elias, 26 Nov 2026 (4 screenshots of Vegapunk: only Vegapunk's replies, "Now my messages are the ones disappearing"). Nothing was lost: turn `02a38ea2…` ran from 09:48 with 106 rows, 15 of them his; two health notes at 11:43 and 11:45 came after it, so the newest page was those 2 notes.
- **Root cause:** `wholeGroups` (`conversation-queries.ts`) keeps each turn whole and cuts a turn of more than 100 rows only when it is the first group of a page. When a smaller group came first, it stopped there. #95 kept the replies in the chat (steps row from live state) but your messages come only from pages.
- **How (portable):** `wholeGroups` takes an `oversized(groupId)` check (the group's row count, with the same marker filter, is over `PAGE_MESSAGE_LIMIT`); an oversized group is cut to fill the page also after other groups. A turn that fits one page still moves whole to the next page. Older pages continue from the cut, with no row twice. #121 also cuts the open turn when it fits one page alone but not after the rows after it, counts a turn on the turn index, and fixes the chat merge that this page shape broke (see ## 121).
- **Here:** `src/backend/database/conversation-queries.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-long-turn-page.test.ts`: the #95 case now expects 100 rows (99 of the turn, the steers 60–150, then the message after it); paging back reads every row once, with the first message and steer 10; a 3-turn thread of 40-row turns keeps whole turns (80 rows). Fails without the fix (`expected [ 'teammate-reply' ] to have a length of 100 but got 1`).
- **Check:** in a chat whose agent runs a turn of more than 100 rows with a message after it, your messages in that turn show; scrolling up shows the earlier ones.

## 120. A video you start in the chat holds the view: new output no longer scrolls it away or stops it

- **What:** pressing play on a video in a chat stops the chat from following the latest message. A working agent's new replies, steps and attachments then add below without moving the view, and the jump-to-latest button shows. Scrolling back to the bottom (or the button) follows the latest again.
- **Why:** Elias, 26 Nov 2026: "when im viewing a video and it ouputs again it stops me/scrolls me back down.. i hate it its super annoying" (Vegapunk's chat, a turn that posts videos and replies for hours).
- **Cause:** at the bottom the chat follows the latest message (`stickToLatest`); each new row glides the view down (`glideConversationBottom` from the resize observer), so the playing video moved up and out of view, and the virtual list then dropped its row, which ended the playback. Playing a video was not reader input (only wheel, key, touch, drag and focus moving up were).
- **How (portable):** `watchReaderVideoPlay` (`reader-video-play.ts`, a capture-phase `play` listener on the chat's scroll element, videos only) calls `readerVideoPlay` in `conversation-scope.ts`: `leaveLatest()` (as a scroll up does), a `[chat-scroll] video-play` log line, and the scroll fade/jump button update.
- **Here:** `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/reader-video-play.ts` (new), `conversation-scope.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/renderer/src/fork-reader-video-play.test.ts`: a play of a nested video calls back once with that video; audio, a video outside the chat and a play after cleanup do not.
- **Changed by #121:** audio files hold the view too, and a muted clip that plays with no click or key (a looping, autoplaying GIF-like video, or a script's `play()`) does not; `watchReaderMediaPlay`, `readerMediaPlay` (see ## 121).
- **Not done:** not tried in Elias's running app (it needs the rebuild); a video you scroll away from yourself still unloads with its row, as before.
- **Check:** in a chat whose agent is working, at the bottom, play the newest video: new output arrives below, the view and the video stay, the jump button shows; scroll to the bottom: the chat follows new output again.

## 122. A flowchart with an email or other special text in a label draws instead of failing

- **What:** when a Mermaid flowchart in a chat does not parse, the chat retries it once with every unquoted node label (`A[…]`, `A(…)`, `A{…}`, `A((…))`, `A([…])`, `A[[…]]`, `A[(…)]`, `A{{…}}`) and arrow label (`-->|…|`) wrapped in quotes. If that copy draws, it is shown; if not, the error of the diagram as written shows, as before.
- **Why:** Elias, 26 Nov 2026 (screenshot, Agency chat): Franky's DNS diagram showed "Cannot draw the diagram. Parse error on line 5" because of `MAIL[hello@immunity-media.com → your Gmail]`.
- **Cause:** Mermaid 12 reads `@` (and other characters) in an unquoted label as syntax; agents seldom quote labels.
- **How (portable):** `quoteFlowchartLabels` (`mermaid-label-repair.ts`) only for `flowchart`/`graph` sources (after front matter and `%%` comments); quoted strings are matched first and kept, so text inside quotes is never rewritten. `drawDiagram` in `mermaid-diagram.ts` calls it only after the first render fails, so diagrams that already draw are unchanged.
- **Here:** `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/mermaid-label-repair.ts` (new), `mermaid-diagram.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/renderer/src/fork-mermaid-label-repair.dom.test.ts` (real Mermaid parser): Franky's diagram and an email arrow label fail as written and parse repaired; all 8 shapes; subgraph/class/style lines; quoted labels and plain arrows untouched; sequence and pie diagrams untouched; front matter.
- **Not done:** other diagram types (sequence, class, …) are not repaired; `-- text -->` arrow labels and `>` / `[/…/]` shapes are not quoted.
- **Check:** after the rebuild, open the Agency chat: Franky's 10:27 PM plan shows a drawn diagram.

## 121. Review of #119 and #120: the open turn on the newest page, early steers kept by a page read, and only a play you start holds the chat

- **What:** (1) your messages in an agent's open turn also show when the turn has 100 rows or a few less and messages came after it; (2) a page read on an agent's state change no longer takes the early steers of a long turn out of the chat; (3) an audio file you play in the chat holds the view like a video; a muted clip that plays by itself does not.
- **Why:** review of #119 and #120 (Elias, 26 Nov 2026), with the page cutter of the #95 test harness switched to the cuts the backend now makes.
- **Root causes and fixes:**
  1. **#119 covered only a turn of more than 100 rows** (`wholeGroups`, `conversation-queries.ts`). An open turn of 99 or 100 rows with 2 or 1 messages after it (or 91–100 rows with 10 after) still crossed the 100-row cap after those messages, was not "oversized", and the newest page held only the later messages: the same page as before #119, and the chat (harness) showed none of the turn's 18 steers. Now `#cutGroup` also lets the **open turn** (`active_turn_id`) be cut to fill the page. An ended turn that fits one page keeps the whole-turn rule.
  2. **The #119 page shape broke the chat's latest-page join** (`mergeConversationPage`, `conversation-merge.ts`, desktop only). A latest page that holds only the end of a turn shares the turn's steps row (`thinking:<turn>`), which stands at the turn's first step; the join cut the chat at that row, so every loaded row between it and where the page really starts (the user's early steers, up to 16 in the harness) left the chat at each agent state change, and a chat opened again after scrolling back lost 15 rows. The join now starts at the first shared row that is not a steps row, keeps the loaded rows before it, and `placeTurnStepRows` places the steps row. The web client and the phone merge raw messages (no steps rows) and were not affected.
  3. **Oversized count cost:** the COUNT used the order index by `thread_id` only, so it scanned the whole thread (5 ms on a 7,279-row thread of the live database copy, up to 3 times per page). It now seeks the turn index (`thread_id, turn_id`), stops at 101 rows, never counts a one-message group, and uses `cachedStatement`.
  4. **#120 held the view for any `play` of a video, and none of audio.** A muted, looping or autoplaying clip, or a script's `play()`, would stop the chat from following; an audio attachment (`MediaFilePreview`'s `<audio>`) stopped when its row left the virtual list, like a video did. `readerStartedMedia` (`reader-video-play.ts`) counts a play when the page has user activation (a click or key just before it) or the media has sound; video and audio. Today's chat videos all start from a click (`VideoPlayer`, native controls, `autoplay` after a click loads the file), so this changes nothing you can see for them.
- **Checked, no bug:** paging back and around-anchor reads with the anchor or cursor inside an oversized turn, turns of exactly 100 and 101 rows, several oversized turns in a row: every row once, in the shown order, from the newest page and from any anchor (test below). The COUNT uses the page's marker filter. #120: a remounted row does not fire `play` again (`playRequested` clears on play; `playOnLoad` is per component); a play while already scrolled up only keeps the view; a pause or end keeps the view until you scroll to the bottom (unchanged); the jump button shows once output arrives (the resize observer calls `updateScrollFade`); the full-screen player is a popover inside the row, so it plays in the same chat. The virtual list does not follow appends once the chat stops following (`followOnAppend` is off with `followLatest`, the end threshold is `-1`), and keeps rows above the fold in place.
- **Here:** `src/backend/database/conversation-queries.ts`, `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/conversation-merge.ts`, `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/reader-video-play.ts`, `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/conversation-scope.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-long-turn-page.test.ts`: "hold the open turn of 100 rows with 1 / 99 rows with 2 / 100 rows with 5 later message(s) on the newest page" (FAIL before: `expected [ 'note-0' ] to have a length of 100 but got 1`); "read every row once, in order, back from the newest page and from any anchor, across oversized turns"; "count a turn's rows with the same marker filter as the page". `src/renderer/src/fork-long-turn-replies.test.ts`: the host cuts pages as upstream or as the fork does now; the 12 provider cases run with both; "shows the user's steers of an open turn of 100 rows when messages come after it"; "joins a latest page that holds the end of a turn after the steers before it". With the old merge, 10 FAIL (`latest page while the reply streams: 16 earlier rows left the chat: expected [ 'steer-1', 'steer-3', …(14) ]`). `src/renderer/src/fork-reader-video-play.test.ts`: audio holds; a muted looping autoplaying clip and a silent video without user activation do not; the same clip with user activation and a video with sound that autoplays after loading do (2 FAIL before).
- **Not covered:** not tried in a running app. A row that spans the top of the view and grows (an open steps row above the video) can still push the video down, because the chat sets `overflow-anchor: none` and the virtual list keeps only rows wholly above the fold in place; not seen, not changed. A muted video that you start more than about 5 seconds after your click (a slow file load) does not hold the chat.
- **Check:** a chat whose agent runs a turn of about 100 rows with a message after it shows your steers in it; switch chats and back, they stay. Play an audio file at the bottom while an agent works: the view stays and the jump button shows.

## 123. Saved apps: keep an agent's chat page as an app with its own data, install it, give it live data

- **What:** a chat page an agent made (`html_render`) gets "Save as app" in its toolbar (spinner, then "Saved · Open"; a failure shows a short red line). Saved apps show on a new **Apps** rail page as cards with a live, inert thumbnail (the page at 880×550 scaled, loaded near the view), inline rename (More → Rename; Enter or leaving the field keeps, Esc cancels), Go to chat, Own window and Remove with a 6 second Undo (removed at once when you leave the page), or an empty state. An app opens full-pane (name, "By agent", a version menu with Newest or a kept version, Go to chat, Own window) or in a window of its own (1100×760, min 420×360, one per app, its size kept). An app follows the agent's newer pages of the same agent, thread and title; the newest 20 versions and a pinned one are kept. Pages keep data with `openbotPage.state.get(key)`, `.set(key, value)` (null deletes) and `.keys()`: the chat page and its app share it, across versions and reloads. `openbot://app/<id>` opens the app on the Apps page, `openbot://app/<id>?window=1` in its own window. **Phase B:** a saved app installs on this computer: More → "Install on this computer" (card menu and the app view's More menu) writes `~/Applications/OpenBot Apps/<Name>.app`, a small bundle whose launcher opens `openbot://app/<id>?window=1` (the app in its own window) and whose icon is the app's letter on a rounded plate; "Add to Desktop" puts a copy of it on the Desktop (installing first when needed); "Uninstall" and "Remove from Desktop" take them away; an installed app shows a quiet "Installed" pill; a failure shows one red line. Renaming an installed app renames its launcher; removing the app uninstalls it. On Windows a Start menu shortcut in "OpenBot Apps" (and a Desktop copy). Linux shows "Installing apps is not available on Linux." **Phase C:** a page an agent made here that declared `uses: ["data"]` in its `html_render` call reads OpenBot's shared tables live with `await openbotPage.data.query(sql, params)` → `{ columns, rows (objects by column name), truncated }` and `openbotPage.data.tables()`, in the chat and in its saved app; any other page hears "This page did not ask for data." (or "Only pages made on this computer can read data.").
- **Why:** Elias, 27 Nov 2026: port OpenMausBot's "Local apps" (Apache-2.0, `src/components/published-apps/Local*`, `server/local-apps/*`, `electron/local-app-windows.mjs`, `electron/app-link.mjs`; see `lab/RE-openmausbot-apps/REPORT.md` and `DESIGN-123.md`).
- **How (portable):** main owns everything. `SavedAppsStore` (`userData/saved-apps/apps.json`, 0600 atomic writes, invalid rows dropped on read, ≤200 apps, id `app_` + 16 base64url) keeps a copy of each version's page as `pages/<sha256>.html` (copied from the mailbox attachment, or downloaded from a joined server) and sweeps copies no app keeps. Saving the same page (server + agent + thread + title) returns the existing app and adds the version when new. `PageStateStore` keeps `state/<sha256(server|agent|thread|title)>.json` with limits: key 1–200 characters, value ≤64 KB, page ≤256 KB, ≤200 keys. `forkHub.savedApps.pageAdded` is called by `#renderVisual` (html_render) and adds the new page as a version (follow newest). The `openbot-visual` protocol serves `openbot-visual://app/<id>?rev=N`. The page bootstrap (`chat-visual.ts`) adds `openbotPage.state` as JSON-RPC `openbot/page-state`; `ChatVisual` answers it through `onState`, or with "Saved data is not available here."; the IPC `app:fork-page-state` reaches main for a chat page (server, agent, attachment, title; main finds the thread) or an app id. IPC in the `app` group: `fork-list-apps`, `fork-get-app`, `fork-save-page`, `fork-rename-app`, `fork-pin-app`, `fork-remove-app`, `fork-page-state`, `fork-open-app-window`, `fork-show-app-chat`, `fork-take-pending-app`, events `fork-apps-changed`, `fork-open-app`, `fork-go-to-chat`. The own window (`openSavedAppWindow`, modeled on `openChatWindow`) loads the renderer with `?window=app&app=<id>`, which renders only the app view. `parseSavedAppLink` (contracts) is the one strict link parser; `deep-link-router.ts` kind `app`, handled in `index.ts` (open-url, second-instance, argv at start). The html_render tool text tells agents about `openbotPage.state`. **Phase B:** `src/main/fork-app-install.ts` (no Electron; adapted from MausBot `electron/app-install/{installer,launchers,icons,names}.mjs`): Info.plist (`CFBundleExecutable` launcher, `CFBundleIconFile` AppIcon, `CFBundleIdentifier` `<host bundle id>.savedapp.<sha256("openbot-saved-app\n"+id)[0:16]>`, APPL, `LSUIElement`, `NSHighResolutionCapable`, id marker `OpenBotSavedApp`), PkgInfo, `MacOS/launcher` (0755: `/usr/bin/open -b '<host>' 'openbot://app/<id>?window=1' 2>/dev/null || exec /usr/bin/open '…'`), `Resources/AppIcon.icns` from the renderer's 256 px PNG via sips + iconutil (16–256 px), built in a `.openbot-<key>.staging` folder and renamed into place. The host bundle id comes from the running app's Info.plist (fallback `app.openbot.desktop`). Windows: `shell.writeShortcutLink` (target `process.execPath`, args the link, `.ico` wrapping the PNG, `appUserModelId`). Tracking list `userData/saved-apps/installed.json` (0600); the PNG is kept in `saved-apps/icons/` for reinstalls. Security: ids pass `SAVED_APP_ID` before any file or shell text; shell text single-quoted, plist XML-escaped; a file is replaced/removed only when it carries this app's id marker and is no symbolic link; a launcher folder that is a link is refused; writes stay in the launcher folder, the Desktop folder and the state folder; the Desktop folder is never created and is read only once a Desktop item exists; names cleaned as MausBot `names.mjs` (64 graphemes/180 bytes, reserved Windows names, `Name 2` on a clash, case- and form-insensitive); rename = write the new launcher, then remove the old; removed bundles go to the Trash; all operations run one at a time. `src/main/fork-app-install-host.ts` wires the real folders: packaged builds only, or any build with `OPENBOT_SAVED_APPS_INSTALL_DIR=<absolute dir>` (then `<dir>/Applications/OpenBot Apps` and `<dir>/Desktop`, removed launchers deleted instead of trashed). `SavedAppsStore.readable()` is false when `apps.json` is there but unreadable; the cleanup after each list load (and at start) uninstalls launchers of apps no longer saved, and does nothing after a failed read. IPC (`app` group): `fork-app-installs`, `fork-install-app`, `fork-uninstall-app`, `fork-add-app-to-desktop`, `fork-remove-app-from-desktop`, event `fork-app-installs-changed`; the name comes from main's store, never the renderer. **Phase C:** `htmlRenderToolSchema` gains `uses: ["data" | "state"]`; the tool router passes it to `forkHub.savedApps.pageAdded`, which records it at once in `PageUsesStore` (`userData/saved-apps/page-uses.json`, keyed by the new attachment id with its agent, newest 5,000) — written by main in the html_render call, so a page cannot forge it. A saved app's versions carry the declaration (`uses`) of their page; pages of a joined server never carry one. Bootstrap `openbotPage.data.query/tables` → JSON-RPC `openbot/page-data`; `ChatVisual` answers through `onData` with a per-frame budget (10 in 10 s, 120 in 10 min) or "Live data is not available here."; the host (chat reply: server, agent, attachment, title; app view: app id) — not the page — names the page in IPC `app:fork-page-data`. `src/main/fork-page-data.ts` checks on every call: chat page → local server, the attachment is in the chat with that agent, and its record declares data; app → local app and the shown version declares data; then a per-window budget; then `AgentTables.query` (its read-only SQL checks, read-only connection, 20,000 SQL characters, 100 parameters, 500 rows, 256 KB of cells, 5 s) and rows reshaped to objects (repeated names `_2`), cut to 1 MB of JSON. Thumbnails answer data calls with "Live data is not available here."
- **Here:** `packages/contracts/src/fork-saved-apps.ts` (new), `chat-visual.ts`, `ipc-endpoints.ts`; `src/main/fork-saved-apps.ts` (new), `application-services.ts`, `session-configuration.ts`, `main-window.ts`, `index.ts`, `deep-link-router.ts`, `ipc/app-handlers.ts`; `src/preload/index.ts`; `src/backend/fork-hub.ts`, `agent/openbot-tool-router.ts`, `openbot-tools.ts`; `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/ChatVisual.tsx` (+ `styles/chat-visual.css`), `features/window/PageRail.tsx`; `src/renderer/src/features/saved-apps/` (new: store, page, view, window, css), `ChatVisualReply.tsx`, `app-providers.tsx`, `index.tsx`, `usage-context.tsx`, `window-page-context.tsx`, `WindowPageView.tsx`, `WorkspacePageRail.tsx`, `layout.tsx`, `lazy-views.ts`, `styles.css`, `preview/mock-openbot.ts`; i18n `en/app.ts`, `en/chat.ts`, `untranslated.json`. Phase B/C: `src/main/fork-app-install.ts`, `fork-app-install-host.ts`, `fork-page-data.ts` (new, with tests); `src/renderer/src/features/saved-apps/fork-saved-app-install.tsx` (new); `src/renderer/src/fork-page-data.dom.test.ts` (new); and the files above.
- **Tests:** `src/main/fork-saved-apps.test.ts` (16): save (strict id, 0600, page copy), same-page dedupe, follow newest (skips a known message or an unchanged page), 20 versions + pinned and the sweep of page copies, pin/unpin, rename cleaning, remove (page data kept), invalid rows dropped, 200-app limit, window sizes, page data get/set/keys/delete and all four limits, the state host through an app and a chat page (wrong agent refused, follow-newest hook), link parser accept/reject cases, strict state request parser. `src/renderer/src/fork-saved-apps-state.dom.test.ts` (5): the real page bootstrap in a frame → `ChatVisual` host → state answers and a counter that survives a reload; an over-limit error and a bad key reach the page; no host → "Saved data is not available here."; Save as app saves once under fast clicks, then opens; a failure line; an already saved page starts as "Saved · Open". Live check on a throwaway DEV profile (`lab/UX-openbot-saved-apps-123/`: seeded chat with two "Counter" pages, local auth API): screenshots in `OpenBot/Shared/gotham/saved-apps-123/` (chat page count 3 kept over a reload, Save → Saved · Open in 15 ms, Apps page with live thumb showing the saved count, rename, menus, version pin to v1 and back, app view +4 → 7 over a reload, own window, Go to chat, both deep links through a second instance, window size 820×600 kept, remove/undo/empty state, re-save keeps the data). `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, Biome, UI foundation and i18n checks pass. Phase B: `src/main/fork-app-install.test.ts` (14, temp folders only): plist keys/escaping/marker and bundle id, the exact launcher text and a bad host id, name cleaning, `.ico`; a real bundle via sips+iconutil passing `plutil -lint` with a valid `.icns`, 0755 launcher, 0600 list; no icon when sips fails; a bundle without the marker is never touched (`Notes 2`); two apps of one name; a symbolic link in place of a launcher is neither removed nor written through; a launcher folder that is a link is refused; rename moves launcher and Desktop copy; Add to Desktop installs first, never creates the Desktop folder, removes only its copy; cleanup removes apps no longer saved and nothing after a failed list; unavailable (dev, Linux) and invalid ids; Windows shortcut options, `.ico`, Desktop copy, a person's own shortcut kept. Phase C: `src/main/fork-page-data.test.ts` (7): the permission matrix (declared local page; undeclared; in the chat but never declared here; another agent's declaration; a joined server's page; a missing page; a saved app following an undeclared version loses data, pinned back gets it; a remote app; a gone app), declarations kept on disk and capped, the per-window budget, the request parser, repeated column names, the 1 MB cut, and real `AgentTables` (spawned host): rows with a parameter, tables, a DELETE and two statements refused with the table unchanged, 600 rows cut to 500. `src/renderer/src/fork-page-data.dom.test.ts` (3): the real bootstrap → `ChatVisual` → host and back, main's refusal and no host, the per-frame budget (10 of 12 answered). All five #123 test files: 45 pass. Live check (throwaway profile, `OPENBOT_SAVED_APPS_INSTALL_DIR=/tmp/obmock-apps/install`): screenshots `OpenBot/Shared/gotham/saved-apps-123/B-*` and `C-*` — install from the card menu (both bundles pass `plutil -lint`, the launcher text is exact with the dev host id `com.github.Electron`, AppIcon.icns has 16–256 px images), the pill, Add to Desktop, rename to "Tally" renamed both bundles, the app view's menu, Remove from Desktop, the failure line with no Desktop folder (folder not created), Add to Desktop installing first, Uninstall, removing the app uninstalled it; a "Tasks live" page (declared) lists the seeded table in the chat and as a saved app (a row added with sqlite3 showed up), "Tasks plain" (undeclared) shows the refusal, the card thumbnail gets no data. The launcher was not run (macOS would hand the link to the real OpenBot).
- **Not done:** Pages of a joined server are copied once and do not follow newer versions; their thread is not known, so their data is per server + agent + title. A page's `sendMessage`/`setContext` in an app view or own window answers "Refused" (no route to the source chat). A change of saved data does not reach other open frames of the same page until they reload. A removed app's page data stays on disk (saving the page again finds it). Thumbnails only read saved data. The web client has no Apps page. Follow newest was tested with the store hook, not with a live agent's html_render call. In the dev check the sidebar's dev-worktree badge made the top-row title start overlap the Forward button, for the Apps page as for Usage (upstream layout, not changed). Install: the Windows path is unit-tested with a fake shell only, never on Windows; no Linux desktop entry; the launcher was never opened (live checks only verify its text and the bundle); MausBot's `refreshTargets` (rewrite Windows shortcuts after OpenBot moves) and taskbar window details are not ported; an app's icon letter changes only when it is installed again (rename does it). Live data: the live check seeded the declaration file instead of running a real agent's `html_render` call (the router hook is typed and covered by the store test only); data is read-only (no writes from pages), and the page reads every shared table, not only its agent's; no per-agent "data off" switch; the declaration file keeps the newest 5,000 pages, so a very old page loses data (its saved app keeps it). `bun scripts/agent-context-budget.ts` reports bytes over its baseline; #123 adds about 330 bytes of tool text per phase, the rest (instructions, guidance) is not from this branch.
- **Check:** in a chat with an agent page, hover the page → "Save as app" → "Saved · Open" opens it on the Apps page; a counter page using `openbotPage.state` keeps its count in the chat, the app and its own window; `openbot://app/<id>?window=1` opens the window. Packaged build: Apps → More → Install on this computer → `~/Applications/OpenBot Apps/<Name>.app` opens the app in its own window from Spotlight/Launchpad; Add to Desktop puts a copy there. A page made with `html_render(…, uses: ["data"])` that calls `openbotPage.data.query("SELECT …")` shows live rows in the chat and in its saved app; one without `uses` shows "This page did not ask for data."

## 124. PDF side panel: PDFs show in the built app, local file links show, the page stays, files reload by themselves

- **What:** (1) Dark paper and Sepia show the PDF in the installed app (they said "Could not read this PDF." with "1 / 1"). (2, 4) A PDF opened from a chat link to a path (plain, with spaces, `file://` with `%20`, non-ASCII) shows in the panel; before, the panel stayed blank white (grey when dimmed) with no toolbar, even with page colors Off. (3) Each side-panel PDF tab keeps its page, place in the page and zoom when you switch to another agent and back, switch tabs, change page colors, and (for Dark paper and Sepia) after a restart. Tab order was already kept. (5) An open file in the side panel reloads by itself when it changes on disk (LaTeX or an agent rewriting it, also replaced by rename), and a ↻ "Reload file" button in the side panel's tab bar, left of "+", reloads it by hand (it spins at least 0.45 s; repeat clicks while it spins do nothing). Both keep the PDF's page and zoom. All file kinds the panel shows reload (PDF Off and paper modes, images, text, Markdown, HTML).
- **Why (Elias 27 Nov):** 12:30: "moon → Dark paper shows Could not read this PDF", and his `~/Downloads/2 - CS/Recall-Sheet.pdf` from a chat link showed a blank panel. Later: "when i move to another bot and come back … i get sent back to the top"; 13:11: "The links you're giving me in the chat … I get empty files", while the same file from the Files search worked. He also asked for a live reload and marked the empty spot left of "+" for a reload button.
- **Cause:**
  - Bug 1, only in a built app (#104 was tested in Storybook only): the built renderer comes from the `openbot-app:` protocol, and `src/main/application-content-type.ts` typed only `.js` as JavaScript. The pdf.js worker is `assets/pdf.worker.min-CjEcRF4W.mjs` (same file in his `/Applications/OpenBot.app`), so it went out as `application/octet-stream` with `nosniff`. Console: "Warning: Setting up fake worker." then "Failed to load module script: Expected a JavaScript-or-Wasm module script but the server responded with a MIME type of "application/octet-stream"". Every pdf.js use failed, also the chat's PDF page peeks.
  - Bugs 2 and 4: a path link is read with `previewSharedFile` / `previewWorkspaceFile` (main decodes `%20`), and the panel shows the bytes from a `blob:` URL in the iframe. The CSP `frame-src` never allowed `blob:`: "Framing 'blob:openbot-app://app/…' violates the following Content Security Policy directive: "frame-src 'self' openbot-attachment: …". Grey was the blank page under his brightness setting. Files search opens the file as an attachment (`openbot-attachment:` URL), which the CSP allowed. Older than #104.
  - Bug 3: on an agent switch `resetPanelsForAgent` (conversation-scope.ts) drops the shown file; back again, `openSidePanelFile` reads the file again and the panel mounts a new iframe or pdf.js reader, at page 1.
- **How (portable):**
  - `.mjs` is served as `text/javascript`; `frame-src` adds `blob:` (the renderer makes these URLs itself, and a blob document keeps the app's CSP).
  - Paper modes: the pdf.js reader saves page, offset in the page (0–1) and zoom (`null` = fit width) per tab, 150 ms after a scroll or zoom, in `localStorage` `openbot:file-preview-pdf-positions` (newest 200), and puts them back on `pagesinit` and again on `pagesloaded` if nothing moved. The tab key is owner agent + file key.
  - Off: the browser viewer's frame is not dropped on unmount but parked with `Element.moveBefore` (a state-keeping move, Electron 45) in a hidden fixed box, at most 3, and reused for the same tab and the same file (source URL, or a sampled fingerprint of the bytes). A changed file needs a new frame, so the panel also reads the viewer's place every second through main (`WebFrameMain.executeJavaScript` in the `chrome-extension://mhjf…` frame whose parent is the iframe; `pdf-viewer.viewport_.position` and `getZoom()`), stores it in `openbot:file-preview-pdf-frame-positions`, and sets it in a new frame once the viewer has loaded (`loadProgress_ === 100`), again after 600 ms. This uses private fields of Chromium's viewer; if they change, Off just opens at page 1.
  - Live reload: the renderer asks main to watch the shown shared or workspace file (`agent:watch-preview-file`, one per window, stopped when the panel shows another file or none, or the window goes). Main watches the folder (so a rename into place counts), filters the file name, waits 300 ms after the last event and sends `agent:preview-file-changed`. The panel then reads the whole file again with the existing refresh, which replaces the preview only if the bytes changed. The button runs the same refresh. Attachments do not change and are not watched; a joined server's files are not watched (the button still works).
- **Here:** `src/main/application-content-type.ts`, `content-security-policy.ts`, `preview-file-watcher.ts` (new), `pdf-viewer-position.ts` (new), `ipc/attachment-handlers.ts`, `ipc/agent-inputs.ts`; `packages/contracts/src/ipc-attachments.ts`, `ipc-endpoints.ts`; `src/preload/index.ts`, `conversation-decoding.ts`; `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/FilePreviewPanel.tsx` (`PdfFrame`), `pdf-frame-keeper.ts` (new), `pdf-reading-mode.ts`, `SidePanel.tsx` (`ReloadFileButton`), styles `file-preview.css`, `side-panel.css`; `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/PdfPaperReader.tsx`, `pdf-positions.ts` (new), `FilePreviewPanel.tsx`, `ConversationPanels.tsx`, `conversation-scope.ts`, `conversation-runtime.ts`, `stores/panels-store.ts`, `preview/mock-openbot.ts`. i18n `conversation.sidePanel.reloadFile` (English only, in `untranslated.json`).
- **Tests:** `src/main/application-content-type.test.ts` (1), `content-security-policy.test.ts` (+1, 1 changed), `preview-file-watcher.test.ts` (3: one change after a burst, rename into place and other names ignored, stop and switch), `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/pdf-positions.test.ts` (4), `pdf-frame-keeper.dom.test.ts` (5), `FilePreviewPanel.test.tsx` (+4: the same frame comes back for the same tab, a new one for another tab, a changed file puts the viewer back at its place, a tab switch shows the new tab's URL). `typecheck:node`, `typecheck:renderer`, `i18n:check`, Biome: PASS. Electron, built renderer from `openbot-app:` (`electron-vite build`, same worker file name as the installed app) on a throwaway profile: before/after shots in `~/OpenBot/Shared/gotham/pdf-panel-124/` (`before-*`, `after-*`): attachment Off/Dark/Sepia, the spaced path in all three, the three link forms at 70 %, p.4 → other agent → back in Off and Dark (also 60 % zoom, tab switch, restart), six fast moon/Off/Dark rounds, the file replaced by rename (Off) and rewritten in place (Dark paper), the Reload button spinning.
- **Not done:** live reload was checked in Electron with the PDF only (images, text, Markdown and HTML use the same refresh, not clicked). The Files panel route of bug 4 was checked in code, not clicked. A joined server's files do not reload by themselves. Several windows each watch only their own shown file. Off keeps its place over a restart only if the viewer reported one before. Not run in a packaged, signed app (an `electron-vite build` served from `openbot-app:` was used).
- **Check:** in the installed app open a chat PDF attachment, pick Dark paper: pages show dark with light text. Click a chat link to a local PDF with spaces in its path: it shows with Off. Scroll to page 4, open another agent, come back: still page 4. Rewrite the PDF on disk: the panel updates at the same page; ↻ left of "+" reloads.

## 125. Page titles clear the forward arrow

- **Get:** on full pages (Apps, Usage, Schedule, Tasks…) with the sidebar's top row showing Search, Back and Forward, the page title starts after the Forward arrow instead of on top of it.
- **Why:** Elias's installed app, 27 Nov 2026: the "Apps" title (#123) overlapped the Forward arrow; Usage did too, so it was not #123.
- **Cause:** the fork puts its Search button in the sidebar's top row, before Back. The title offset `--top-row-tabs-start` (window.css) counts the controls start, Back/Forward, the toggle and the update button, but not that Search button, so titled pages started one button too early.
- **How (portable):** a `--top-row-search-width` term in `--top-row-tabs-start`, set to one small control plus its 3px gap when the sidebar has `.sidebar-topbar-search`.
- **Here:** `src/renderer/src/features/window/window.css`.
- **Tests:** CSS only; checked in the installed app after the restart (zoom on the top row of Apps and Usage).
- **Check:** open Apps, then Usage: the title starts right of the Forward arrow with a gap.

## 126. Gemini agents continue after a restart too

- **Get:** when OpenBot restarts while a Gemini (Antigravity) agent, or another ACP agent, works in its own chat, the agent gets its "🔄 restart" wake like a Claude agent and goes on, instead of staying silent until the next message.
- **Why:** Elias, 27 Nov 2026: "why is it not responding": three restarts that day (13:23, 13:52, 13:54) each stopped a Vegapunk turn, and none was resumed; his "progres?" got no answer.
- **Cause:** as OpenBot quits, `markTurnsStoppedForShutdown` aborts each ACP client's turn and the turn is settled as interrupted before the app exits. Boot recovery then finds no running turn, so `capture` ("Agents continue after a restart", #113) never notes it. Claude and Codex turns stay "running" until boot, so they were noted.
- **How (portable):** `AgentService.stop` first notes each own-chat turn whose client has `markTurnsStoppedForShutdown` (`capture` with `quitting`: interruptedAt is the quit time, the entry carries `quit`). Boot keeps that note (also for a wake, so the try count goes up once), and `start` clears `quit` and gives a note that a queued message took over back to the in-memory hand-over list.
- **Here:** `src/backend/agent-service.ts` (`#noteQuitInterrupted`), `src/backend/fork-restart-resume.ts`, fake `hold-turn` in `scripts/provider-fakes/agents/antigravity-subagent.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-restart-resume.test.ts` (quit notes: settled at boot, Claude noted once, a wake counts one try, a queued message gets the note); `scripts/antigravity-quit-resume-e2e.ts` (real service twice with the fake `agy`: the quit leaves the turn interrupted, the note is pending, the next start sends one wake; fails without the fix); `scripts/restart-resume-e2e.ts` and `scripts/antigravity-restart-keeps-subagents-e2e.ts` still pass.
- **Check:** while a Gemini agent works, quit and reopen OpenBot: its chat shows "🔄 Resumes after the restart", then the agent continues.
