## 130. Team follow-through

- **Get:** when a teammate reports back to a lead agent (a reply to the lead's `openbot.send_message` request) and the lead gives it no next step or decision within 3 minutes, the lead gets one "👥 Team follow-through" wake in its own chat that names each such teammate with the time and the start of its report. The lead sends the next step, or ends quietly with `routine_no_update` when the teammate's part is finished.
- **Why:** Vegapunk (Gemini, Antigravity), 27 Nov 2026: Distiller replied "Status: done …" at 14:27:45Z; the turn handling it began forwarding Distiller's toolkit, the user steered a new request into the same turn at 14:28:29Z, and Distiller never got its next step. Elias asked "did u forget to followup w Distiller" 8 minutes later. Many other teammate "done" replies of that day got no decision either.
- **Cause:** a teammate's reply is one delivery in the lead's turn; when that turn turns to other work (a steered message, a long reply, a restart), nothing in OpenBot remembers that the report still waits for a decision.
- **How (portable):**
  - No state file: everything is read from the mailbox, so it holds over a restart. A report is a message from another agent, delivered (completed) to the lead in its own chat (not a channel, messaging or task thread), whose `replyToMessageId` is a message the lead sent, at most 2 h old. It is pending when it is at least `OPENBOT_FORK_FOLLOW_THROUGH_DELAY_MS` (3 min) old, it is that teammate's newest report, and the lead sent that teammate no message since (any send counts as a decision).
  - A sweep every `OPENBOT_FORK_FOLLOW_THROUGH_SWEEP_MS` (60 s) queues, per lead with pending reports, one wake for all of them when the lead's own lane is idle (`laneBusy`/`laneQueued` of the restart wake), its provider is ready, and it had no follow-through wake in the last 10 min (`..._GAP_MS`, test switch). The wake is `enqueueHandoff` with a routine sender (`routineId` `fork-follow-through`, no real routine), idempotency key `fork-follow-through:<oldest report id>`. A report gets at most one wake: one whose key exists, or that a follow-through wake queued at least the delay after it covered, is done. `OPENBOT_FORK_FOLLOW_THROUGH=0` turns it off. Hidden, deleted and being-deleted agents are skipped, as leads and as teammates.
  - The wake is classified like the wait and restart wakes, not like a routine run: its text is the whole prompt (`delivery-content`), it runs in the lead's own session (`drain-scheduler` `#runsInOwnSession`), and a restart that interrupts it resumes it (`fork-restart-resume` `#captureSkip`: the report has only this one wake, so dropping it like OpenBot upkeep would lose the follow-up). Unlike the wait and restart wakes it may end quietly: the turn's `quietRoutineDelivery` hook lets `routine_no_update` hide it, like keep-warm.
- **Here:** `src/backend/fork-follow-through.ts` (new), `src/backend/agent-service.ts` (`#followThroughHooks`, start and stop, `#forkFollowThroughDelivery`), `src/backend/mailbox-store.ts` (`forkFollowThroughTraffic`), `src/backend/fork-hub.ts`, `src/backend/agent/delivery-content.ts`, `src/backend/agent/drain-scheduler.ts`, `src/backend/fork-restart-resume.ts` (`localTime` exported, the capture skip); fake `report-done` in `scripts/provider-fakes/agents/antigravity-subagent.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-follow-through.test.ts` (13: selection — a reply to the lead's request, a later message to the teammate resolves it, a newer report supersedes, too new and too old, not a reply, a reply to someone else, channel or task delivery, unfinished delivery, the user or the lead itself; one wake per report over sweeps and a new instance; grouping keyed by the oldest; idle and provider gating; 10-minute gap; off switch, hidden lead, gone teammate; the wake text). `scripts/fork-follow-through-e2e.ts` (real service four times with the fake `agy`: Distiller's "Status: done" reply reaches Vegapunk, one wake in Vegapunk's own session names Distiller, later sweeps and a restart add none, Shaka who messaged Lilith after her report gets none; fails with the sweep off). `scripts/restart-resume-e2e.ts`, `scripts/antigravity-quit-resume-e2e.ts`, `scripts/antigravity-restart-keeps-subagents-e2e.ts` still pass.
- **Check:** have a lead agent ask a teammate for work with `expectsReply`; when the teammate's report arrives, send the lead something else at once. About 3 minutes after the lead goes idle, its chat shows "👥 Team follow-through" and the lead gives the teammate its next step (or stays quiet when the teammate is done).

## 132. No interim states

- **Get:** a message shows where it belongs from the start and moves only when its state really changes. Sent while a routine run holds the turn (or between turns behind queued messages, or while the queue is held), it goes straight to the queue panel, labelled "Not steered", never first as a sent bubble. A queued send shows in the panel at once (its actions wait until the host lists it), never as a bubble after 400 ms. A message the host is starting or steering stays in the panel ("Starting" / "Steering", in its slot) or as your own bubble until the provider takes it, then moves into the chat once; a refused start or steer no longer pops into the chat and back. A teammate's "Messaged X" chip stays queued until its turn really starts. The red error banner ends when a later turn starts. A late end of an older turn no longer clears the running turn's stop button. A refused delete brings its row back; a fast double-click on Delete deletes one row, not also the row that slides under the pointer. A moved row (drag or Alt+↑/↓) stays where it was dropped while the host answers. In "waiting for replies", a teammate whose reply was read stays "Replied" while the others work, so the count does not shrink.
- **Why:** Elias, 27 Nov 2026: while the Health routine held Vegapunk's (Gemini) turn, his message showed as a sent bubble for about 3 s, then vanished and reappeared in the queue panel as "Not steered". "These sort of interim state bugs are annoying… fix all of them." Five investigators listed the cases in lab `UX-openbot-interim-states/SHEET.md` (A1–A5, B1–B7, C1–C4, D1–D2, E1–E2) with a combined list (F1–F9, S1–S5).
- **Cause:** the composer guessed "steer" from settings alone (no look at a routine run, a running turn or a hold) and kept its bubble on a fixed 3 s timer; the host published `starting` deliveries into the chat before the provider accepted the turn or steer, and put them back to `queued` on a refusal; several UI states (banner, pill, delete, reorder, "Replied") were set by one event and cleared only by an unrelated one.
- **How (portable):**
  - F1 (A1, A3, A5 partly): `runningTurnTakesMessages(queue, activeTurnId)` (a turn runs, not a routine run's, no hold) gates `steered` in `queueSend`; otherwise the existing queued path applies. A turn model the renderer cannot see (A5) is left to F3.
  - F2 (A2): a queued send is never drawn in the timeline (only a failed one, for retry). `queueSendingRows` gives the panel a `pending:<id>` row until the host lists the delivery (matched by id or, while sending, by text); `QueuePanel` `handoffs` swap it for the host row without exit/entry animation; `sendingIds` disable its actions.
  - F3 (A4, B1, B3, E1): one rule, `deliveryWaitsForProvider` (contracts) and `startingMessageWaits` (projection): the agent chat hides a `starting` user or teammate delivery (routine runs and teammate replies keep their rows); `presentQueueDeliveries` shows a drain start (`turnId` null) and a steer in the panel; `QueuePanel` keeps a row without a position at its last place and labels a drain start "Starting". The composer keeps its own bubble while its delivery is `starting`, and lets the panel take it at once when the host marks it queued with a `steerFallback`. The sender's chip reads `starting` as queued.
  - F4 (B2): `QueueControls.steer` marks a refused steer `steer-failed` before emitting; the renderer skips the extra error line for a labelled row.
  - F5 (C1): `turn-started` clears the agent's banner (`clearAtTurnStart`; a live retry notice stays). F6 (C3): `turn-completed` nulls the active turn and sets the Failed pill only when no later turn runs (`laterTurnRunning`).
  - F7 (B4): `cancelQueuedMessage` resolves false on failure and the panel brings the row back; the Delete button ignores the second click of a double-click. F8 (B7): `QueuePanel` keeps a local order until the host's order matches or the reorder fails (`reorderQueue` resolves false). F9 (B6): `agentAwaitingReplies` keeps a read reply as "Replied" while the request is open, and drops the request's rows together when all are done.
- **Here:** `packages/contracts/src/ipc-queue.ts`, `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/QueuePanel.tsx`, `packages/i18n/src/messages/en/queue.ts` (`queue.item.starting`), `src/backend/agent/queue-controls.ts`, `src/renderer/src/app-message-projection.ts`, `awaiting-replies.ts`, `queue-reconciliation.ts`, `turns.tsx`, `features/agents/agent-event-bridge.tsx`, `agent-error-text.ts`, `features/conversation/conversation-context.tsx`, `conversation-scope.ts`, `conversation-types.ts`, `ConversationComposer.tsx`, `stores/composer-actions.ts`, `pending-send-store.ts`, `queue-store.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/renderer/src/fork-interim-states.test.ts` (routine/hold/no-turn gate; starting rows in the panel, not in the chat; chip; sending rows and handoff; banner and late turn end; "Replied" kept), `src/renderer/src/fork-interim-queue-panel.test.tsx` (handoff without a second row; "Starting" in place; order shown before the host answers; refused delete comes back; double-click deletes one). Live in a separate dev mock with a fake Gemini CLI whose routine run holds the turn 25 s (lab `UX-openbot-interim-states/harness`, proof in `proof/`): on main the message was a bubble for 3.1 s, then "Not steered" in the panel (three fast sends the same); on this branch every send is in the panel from the first frame, and when the routine ends the rows go "Starting" → chat once; an idle send and a steer into a running turn never show a panel row; a real double-click on Delete deletes one row; Alt+↑ moves a row at once.
- **Not done:** B5 (a stored "routine-run" reason: needs a new queue contract value that older remote hosts reject; after F1 a send during a routine never tries to steer from this composer), C2 (#131), C4 (not observed), D1/D2 (the Antigravity answer → step → answer flip is the quiet-timer trade-off of #111; the visible flip was 6 of 397 items, and publishing the quiet reply as the answer would flip every reply followed by a slow tool call; D2 is classic style only), E2 (known gap of #105). Not checked live: the banner (F5), the late turn end (F6), a refused steer (F4), a refused delete/reorder, the "Replied" card.
- **Check:** in Vegapunk's chat, run a routine ("Run now") and send a message while it runs: it shows only in the queue panel, labelled "Not steered", and moves into the chat when the routine ends.

## 131. A slow Gemini agent is not ended; a hung one resumes by itself

- **Get:** a Gemini (Antigravity) turn held open for a background job (a training run, an ablation) is no longer ended because `agy` answered one liveness check late. OpenBot ends `agy` only when, for 10 minutes, it wrote nothing, answered no `session/list` probe and its whole process tree used no CPU; time while the Mac is overloaded does not count. If it must end it: a turn that already posted its answer stays completed (no red error); the chat resumes by itself in the same session with "🔄 Provider restarted: continue", which names the processes `agy` had started that still run ("do not start them again") and those that ended with it; a lead whose request that turn held gets one note "⏳ <Teammate>'s provider was restarted by OpenBot; it continues by itself — don't resend or restart it." and later the resumed answer as the reply, not "the request failed before the teammate answered".
- **Why:** Chopper (Gemini), 27 Nov 2026: a turn waiting for an ablation run since 14:42Z failed at 15:28:07Z with "Gemini did not answer in time, so OpenBot ended its process." The Mac ran at a load of 250–300 on 10 cores (antigravity trees at 17–19 GB, 300 %+ CPU); one `session/list` probe of 30 s timed out and OpenBot ended `agy` and the job. Four such kills that day (Chopper, Pacer, Shadow Tapa, Distiller, 15:28–15:35Z), and none resumed: the message classifies as `unknown`, so the network resume (#69) never opened. Their leads got failure notes and resent work, adding load.
- **Cause:** `AcpAgentClient.#endIfUnresponsive` (called by the stall watch of a prompt held open for background tasks, by a request that timed out and by a timed-out `session/resume`) sent one probe and ended the process when it timed out.
- **How (portable):**
  - Liveness by progress (`acp-client.ts` `#endIfUnresponsive`, new `fork-provider-hang.ts`): a probe every `ACP_HANG_PROBE_GAP_MS` (30 s) with the normal request timeout; after each timed-out probe the process tree of `agy` (by parent pid from `ps -A -o pid=,ppid=,time=,command=`; `agy` is not a group leader, so not by pgid) is compared with the sample before: a new process, or CPU growth of at least 1 % of one core over the round (at least 250 ms), is progress. Any probe answer, any transport output (`lastOutputAt`), turn activity or progress ends the watch (alive); the stall watch starts over. Time counts toward `ACP_HANG_MS` (10 min) only while the 1-minute load average is at most 4× the cores. The `#probing` guard is now per process (`WeakSet<AcpConnection>`), so one agent's 10-minute watch does not skip another's (Antigravity runs a process per session under one client). Test switches: options `hangMs`, `hangProbeGapMs`, `sampleProcessTree`, `systemOverloaded`; env `OPENBOT_FORK_ACP_HANG_MS`, `OPENBOT_FORK_ACP_HANG_PROBE_MS`, `OPENBOT_FORK_ACP_IGNORE_LOAD=1`, `OPENBOT_FORK_ACP_TURN_STALL_MS`.
  - An answered turn completes (`#endHungTurn`): when the turn has a quiet answer (#111) or text, it is abandoned as `completed` with no error; else it fails with the old message and cause `timeout`. The turn's task is interrupted first, then the session process ends, then the turn completes, so the job list says which descendants survived.
  - Jobs: `AcpConnection` ends only `agy`'s pid (SIGTERM, SIGKILL after 2 s), never its group, so descendants are not killed by OpenBot; whether they live on is `agy`'s doing. OpenBot lists the descendants of the last sample and, 1 s after the end, which still run (`processAlive`); at most 20, command lines redacted and cut to 300 characters. They go with `turn/completed` as `forkProviderRestart: {running, ended, silentMinutes}`.
  - Resume (`turn-lifecycle.ts`, `fork-hub.ts` `providerRestarted`, `agent-service.ts`, `fork-wait-conditions.ts` `openProviderRestartResume`): a turn of the agent's own chat with `forkProviderRestart` opens a fork wait whose condition is "the agent is idle" and whose wake ("🔄 Provider restarted: continue", `fork-wait` routine sender, same session) quotes the request, the jobs, and each teammate request it answers. It shares the network resume's limit (3 an hour per agent, in memory); a second hang before the wake joins the open wait. A completed turn does not close it (`closeNetworkResumes` skips it).
  - Leads (`mailbox-store.ts` `noteProviderRestart`, `turn-lifecycle.ts`): while the resume is open, a failed turn's requests from other agents get the ⏳ note (agent sender, `expectsReply: false`, no `replyToMessageId`, so it is no reply and no follow-through report; key `fork-provider-restart:<delivery>`) instead of the failure note. When the wake turn completes, its answer goes to each held request as the teammate's reply (`#relayLaterResult`), unless the teammate replied itself; when it fails and does not resume again, or the limit is spent, the requester gets the failure note as before. The held requests are in memory: after an app restart they get neither.
- **Here:** `src/backend/acp-client.ts`, `src/backend/acp-connection.ts` (`pid`), `src/backend/fork-provider-hang.ts` (new), `src/backend/agent/turn-lifecycle.ts`, `src/backend/fork-hub.ts`, `src/backend/agent-service.ts`, `src/backend/agent-waits/fork-wait-conditions.ts`, `src/backend/mailbox-store.ts`, fake `scripts/provider-fakes/agents/slow-probe.ts` (new).
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-provider-liveness.test.ts` (7, real client over the fake `agy`: `ps` parsing and tree, progress threshold; probes that time out while the tree uses CPU keep the agent; late probe answers keep it; overloaded time does not count; no progress for the window ends it with the old error and a restart record; an answered turn completes and lists the `sleep` job that still runs). `src/backend/fork-provider-restart-resume.test.ts` (3: the wake waits for idle, names jobs, request and teammate, survives a completed turn; answered-turn wording, one wait per agent, shared 3-an-hour limit; ⏳ note once, not a reply, the failure note still possible later). `scripts/antigravity-slow-probe-e2e.ts` (client with the real process table: late probes and a CPU-burning job keep the turn to its end; a hung agent is ended after the window, its turn carries the restart, the next turn works). `scripts/antigravity-hung-resume-e2e.ts` (whole agent service: lead Vegapunk's request to Chopper hangs → ⏳ note, wake in the same session, the wake's answer reaches Vegapunk as the reply, no failure note; an answered turn stays completed and is resumed; the 4th hang of the hour gives the failure note). All fail with the fix reverted. Live in a dev instance with the fake `agy` (lab `RE-gemini-liveness-131`, proof/README.txt): a slow agent (probes answered after 35 s) kept its 150 s job turn; a hung teammate was ended, its lead got the ⏳ note and then the resumed answer as the reply, no failure note; an answered turn stayed completed and resumed; a gentle quit mid-turn resumed the turn after relaunch (#126); two agents hung at once were both ended in the same window.
- **Not covered / limits:** a failed turn's red error banner stays in the chat under the resumed answer. the hang clock is paused while the load average is above 4× the cores; on 27 Nov 2026 the Mac stayed above that for hours, so then no Gemini agent is ended as hung at all (a busy-looping `agy` also counts as alive). Whether `agy` kills its own children on SIGTERM is untested with the real CLI. The resume limit and held requests do not survive an app restart.
- **Check:** with a Gemini agent waiting for a long command, nothing ends it while the machine is busy; `logs/providers/providers.log` shows "made no progress" only after 10 calm minutes, and then the chat shows "🔄 Provider restarted: continue".

## 133. CI on the fork is green again

- **Why:** every push to `main` sent Elias a "Run failed eliasx45/openbot CI - main" notice: CI failed on 11 of the last 12 runs (last success 5 Nov 2026, run 42064269347). Fork features changed behaviour that upstream's checks and tests still expected, and the fork lacks upstream's release tags.
- **What failed, why, what changed** (runs 46020436749, 46009719378, 46008694390, 45706385063):
  - **Check / Check unused files and dependencies (knip):** 53 exports and 10 exported types of fork code that nothing outside their file uses, a duplicate export (`parseAgentBrowserProfileChoices` = `decodeAgentBrowserProfileChoices`), `mermaid` imported by `src/renderer/src/fork-mermaid-label-repair.dom.test.ts` but not a root dependency, and `ffprobe`/`swiftc` not in the system binaries. Fix: the `export` keyword is gone from those symbols (the rule of #22), `forgetStoredPdfPositions` (used nowhere) is deleted, the IPC handler uses `decodeAgentBrowserProfileChoices`, the renderer stops re-exporting `isClaudeCodeMcpServerId`, `mermaid` is a root dev dependency (`catalog:`; `bun.lock` gains one line), `ffprobe` and `swiftc` join `ignoreBinaries` next to `ffmpeg` and `sips`. The later Check steps never ran on those runs; they failed too:
  - **Check image asset directories:** `perf/chart.png` (the load test report of #43) is outside the allowed folders; `perf/` is now allowed in `scripts/check-image-assets.ts`, as its message says.
  - **Check ratcheted TypeScript options (`exactOptionalPropertyTypes`):** 12 errors in fork code, fixed in the code, not the baseline: `| undefined` on optional fields that receive `undefined` (`db-load-test-report.ts`, `preview-file-watcher.ts`, `sidebar-drag-model.ts` `folderItems`, `subagent-updates.ts` `CodexSubagentModels.parent`, `agent-thread-attention.ts` `byAgent`), `NonNullable` for the cookie `sameSite` in `chrome-import.ts`, no `undefined` key (`fork-network-drop-e2e.ts` body, `db-load-test-report.ts` `executablePath`, `fork-app-install-host.ts` `trashItem` via `withoutTrash`, `fork-whatsapp-watch.test.ts`), `?? null` for the Voice notes select and the story's `sourceUrl`.
  - **Check agent context budget:** the fork's agent text grew since the budget was set at the 23 Nov upstream merge (`c99594789`). As #22 says, `tools/agent-context/budget.json` holds the fork's counts and is set from `bun run check:agent-context` after fork features add agent text: claude instructions 17,347 → 17,990 bytes, codex 17,410 → 18,053, visible tools 23,786 → 23,825, schemas 86,777 → 87,785, descriptions 47,218 → 48,040, guidance 26,259 → 27,297.
  - **Check released database schemas:** the fork's GitHub repository has the release tags only up to v0.59.0, so the generated `releases.json` would lack v0.60–v0.76. CI on the fork now fetches the tags from upstream and keeps those up to the merged version (`package.json`, now 0.76.1), so a newer upstream release (v0.77–v0.80 exist) does not fail it before the fork merges it.
  - **Tests (desktop 2/2):** all files passed but an unhandled `ReferenceError: matchMedia is not defined` failed the run: the image viewer (`ImageLightbox.tsx`) watches the screen resolution with `matchMedia`, which jsdom lacks. `src/renderer/src/fork-card-media.test.tsx` ("opens the image in the image viewer") now stubs it the way `ComposerAttachmentLightbox.test.tsx` does.
  - **`test:team-legacy-clients`** ("The created agent is not in GET /v1/agents"): intended behaviour, #107 (agents no level names are closed to members) and #118 (only owners and admins add or duplicate agents and read AI account usage). The test's owner opens the agents to Everyone; the duplicate routes and the usage reads (HTTP, WebSocket, and a second WebRTC peer) use an admin's session; the member's marked WebRTC duplicate now expects the route's 403 "Administrator access is required." after the protocol gate.
  - **`test:agent-create-retry`** ("403 !== 201"): intended, #118. The second person who creates with the same `operationId` is an admin, not a member.
  - **`test:workflow-folders`** (member saw no folders): intended, #107. The open agent is set to `manage` explicitly.
  - **`test:provider-exit-session`** ("Timed out waiting for the session to be deactivated"): a fork bug from #92. `ThreadLifecycle.#backgroundHold` held a refresh for good (no limit) when `holdsBackgroundWork` failed; a Codex app-server without `thread/backgroundTerminals/list` (the e2e's fake, older CLIs) fails it, so after the Computer Use driver stopped, the Codex agent kept its old session and tools forever. Before #92 only Claude waited on that check. Fix: a failed check holds only Claude (as before #92); a successful one still holds every provider (Antigravity subagents, #92).
  - **`test:antigravity-quota-failover`** ("rate limit before work": `failed` instead of `completed`): intended, #81 (only a usage limit moves an agent to a backup model). The rate limit (before and after work), dropped connection and model-gone cases (upstream #11320, #13019) now expect the message to fail on the agent's own model with the error shown and no prompt on the backup. The spent-quota cases, the spent AI credits balance cases and the channel case still move, as the code does today.
- **CI noise (`.github/workflows`):** `ci.yml` cancels the run of the previous push on the fork (`cancel-in-progress` also when `github.repository != 'nightly-labs/openbot'`; upstream keeps finishing runs on `main` for its production deploy, which the fork skips). `remote-desktop-runtime.yml` no longer runs on the fork on a push or by hand (48 minutes of builds, then HTTP 403 creating a release, as on 5 Nov); pull requests still build. Other workflows on the fork: `Fork Windows build` (push, passes), `Contribution policy` (schedule, passes); `Cloudflare preview`, `Announce mobile release on Discord`, `Status page`, `Release health`, `Provider CLI canary`, `Release regression report`, `Release upgrade history` and `Provider live E2E` skip on the fork already (#22 pattern); the release, mobile and Android workflows run only by hand or on tags or paths the fork does not push.
- **Here:** `.github/workflows/ci.yml`, `.github/workflows/remote-desktop-runtime.yml`, `knip.config.ts`, `package.json`, `bun.lock`, `scripts/check-image-assets.ts`, `tools/agent-context/budget.json`, `src/backend/agent/thread-lifecycle.ts`, the e2e scripts named above, `src/renderer/src/fork-card-media.test.tsx`, and the files whose exports or optional types changed (no behaviour change).
- **Tests (local, macOS, same commands as `ci.yml`):** `knip:check`, `check:assets`, `check:doc-links`, `check:desktop:static` (UI check, Biome, typecheck, build, preload), `check:migrations`, `i18n:check`, `types:ratchet`, `check:agent-context`, `whats-new-translations`, `check:app-minimum-version` PASS; `released-schema-fixtures.ts --check` PASS with the tags up to v0.76.1 (FAIL with v0.77–v0.80 present, which is why CI drops them). `test:desktop` shard 1 and 2 PASS, no unhandled error (main's `fork-card-media.test.tsx` alone reproduces the `matchMedia` error). `test:team-legacy-clients`, `test:agent-create-retry`, `test:provider-exit-session`, `test:workflow-folders`, `test:antigravity-quota-failover` FAIL on `main` and PASS now; `test:antigravity-restart-keeps-subagents` (#92) still 3/3 PASS. All 156 `bun` steps of the Team API routes, Team API and reverse actions and Provider fakes E2E jobs, run one after another: 155 PASS; `test:mcp-member-access` fails only on macOS (Devin's sign-in relay is Linux only, `devinSignInPageRelayed`) and passes in CI.
- **Check:** after the push, the CI run on `main` is green and no "Run failed" notice arrives.

## 134. Team board

- **Get:** a lead agent's chat header shows a team button (👥 and the number of teammates, an orange dot when one needs attention) that opens a **Team** pane in the side panel: one row per teammate the lead sent a request to in the last 24 h, with its current request, a state chip (Queued, Working, In progress, Partial, Done, Replied, Blocked, Needs input, Failed, Overdue), whether it works right now (live dot and "Working now" / "Messages waiting" / "Idle"), its last report with status, next step and age, and how many earlier requests never got a report. Rows that need the lead come first. A row opens the teammate's chat; **Message** opens it with the composer focused. A teammate's reply shows its status as a chip on its "Update from …" row in the chat, with the next step on hover. Teammates can pass `status` (done, progress, partial, blocked, needs-input) and `next` on `openbot.send_message` when they answer; the lead gets "Status: … / Next: …" first in the text. The lead has `openbot.team_board` (direct tool) to read the same board instead of keeping a PLAN.md.
- **Why:** Vegapunk (Gemini) leads ~15 teammates and kept their state in a hand-edited `leads/PLAN.md` that drifted (Apoo "working" 24 min after it sent "Status: done", 27 Nov 2026) and forgot teammates (#130). Research `lab/RE-swarm-gaps` theme T3 (R1-1, R2-1, R1-2, R1-7, R3-7): Claude Code agent-team task list, OpenMausBot Team Board, Codync task list.
- **How (portable):**
  - No store: `buildTeamBoard` (`src/backend/fork-team-board.ts`) derives everything from the mailbox (`MailboxStore.forkTeamBoardMessages(since)`: agent-sent messages outside channels, with reply target resolved from a delivery id, `expectsReply`, and each delivery's recipient and status). A request is a message from one agent to another with `expectsReply` not false. Per lead and teammate the newest request in the window is current; a report is any message from the teammate to the lead; it answers the request it replies to, or (no reply id, or one that names none of the lead's requests) the newest request sent before it. State of the current request: its newest report's status line (`progress` while the delivery still runs is `working`), an OpenBot failure note (`answerFailedRequest`'s "OpenBot note, not written by this teammate") is `failed`, a question back (expectsReply, no reply id) is `needs-input`, else `replied`; without a report: delivery failed/interrupted/cancelled → `failed`, completed (turn ended without a result) → `overdue` after 2 min, older than 60 min (`OPENBOT_FORK_TEAM_BOARD_OVERDUE_MS`) → `overdue`, else `working`/`queued`. Earlier requests without any report and not cancelled count as `earlierOpen`. Hidden, deleted, being-deleted and being-duplicated agents are left out, as leads and as teammates. Live activity: `#runsTurn` or a starting/running delivery → working, a queued delivery → queued, else idle.
  - Status lives in the text: `parseReportStatus` (`packages/contracts/src/fork-team-board.ts`) reads a `Status: <word>` line among the first 5 non-empty lines (markdown, bullets and emoji ignored; done/complete/finished, in progress/working, partial, blocked, needs input/needs you/waiting/question) and a `Next:` / `Next step:` line after it. `send_message`'s `status`/`next` are validated (`reportFields`: known status, `next` needs `status`, one line ≤ 300 chars) and only allowed on a reply to a request (`replyToMessageId`, or the turn answers a request from the recipient); `withReportStatus` writes the lines first and drops the text's own leading status/next line. So the mailbox schema and the Team API wire are unchanged, and the lead reads the status in its prompt.
  - App: IPC `agent.forkTeamBoard({ leadId, hours? })` (local server only; a remote server answers an empty board), decoded by `decodeTeamBoard`. Renderer `useTeamBoard` (`src/renderer/src/features/team-board/team-board-store.ts`) shares one read per lead between the header button and the pane, reads again at most every 400 ms on `queue-changed`, `queue-invalidated`, `turn-started`, `turn-completed`, `agents-changed`, `hidden-agents-changed`, and every 20 s; ages tick every 30 s. The pane is side panel pane `team` (`SIDE_PANEL_PANES`), offered only for an agent that leads someone (or while it is open). The marker chip comes from `chatActionMarker` (`report` on `AgentMessageMarkerModel`; an OpenBot note shows `Failed`).
  - Tool: `openbot.team_board { hours? }` (direct, like `list_agents`) returns `counts` and per teammate `name, id, state, live, request, requestId, askedMinutesAgo, report {status, next, text, minutesAgo, messageId, forEarlierRequest?, writtenByOpenBot?}, earlierRequestsWithoutReport?`. Guidance for `send_message`/`list_agents`/`team_board` tells teammates to pass `status`/`next` and leads to read the board instead of a status file.
- **Here:** `packages/contracts/src/fork-team-board.ts` (new), `src/backend/fork-team-board.ts` (new), `src/backend/mailbox-store.ts` (`forkTeamBoardMessages`), `src/backend/agent-service.ts` (`forkTeamBoard`, `#forkTeamBoard`, `forkHub.teamBoard`), `src/backend/fork-hub.ts`, `src/backend/openbot-tools.ts` (`status`, `next`, `team_board`), `src/backend/agent/openbot-tool-router.ts`, `src/backend/agent/tool-catalog.ts`, `src/backend/agent/tool-guidance.ts`, `packages/contracts/src/ipc-endpoints.ts`, `src/main/ipc/agent-handlers.ts`, `src/main/ipc/agent-inputs.ts`, `src/preload/index.ts`, `packages/ui/src/features/conversation/TeamBoard.tsx` + `styles/team-board.css` (new), `ConversationHeader.tsx`, `SidePanel.tsx`, `ChatActionMarker.tsx`, `packages/ui/src/data.ts`, `src/renderer/src/features/team-board/` (new), `src/renderer/src/features/conversation/ConversationHeader.tsx`, `ConversationPanels.tsx`, `src/renderer/src/app-message-projection.ts`, `src/renderer/src/preview/mock-openbot.ts`, `conversation.team.*` / `conversation.sidePanel.pane.team` (English; other locales in `untranslated.json`); fake `[hold-turn]` in `scripts/provider-fakes/agents/antigravity-subagent.ts`.
- **Tests:** `src/backend/fork-team-board.test.ts` (21: queued/working/live activity, status + next, blocked/needs-input/replied, a question back, progress while running and after, failed by delivery and by OpenBot note, overdue by ended turn and by age, superseded requests with `earlierOpen` (answered and cancelled ones do not count), a report without reply id, hidden/deleted lead and teammate, old mail, info messages, grouping and attention order, one request to several teammates, decoder round trip, the tool's compact result; status parsing variants, `withReportStatus`, excerpts; `reportFields` and `hours` validation). `src/renderer/src/features/team-board/TeamBoard.test.tsx` (7: the pane's rows, ages, report and next, open/message clicks incl. repeats, loading and empty, the shared throttled read and unsubscribe, the marker's report status incl. OpenBot note, the chip and its title). `bun scripts/fork-team-board-e2e.ts` (real agent service, fake Codex calling OpenBot tools: a reply with `status: blocked`/`next` reaches the lead as "Status: blocked\nNext: …" first, a plain result is `replied`, `status` outside a reply and `next` without `status` are refused and send nothing, `team_board` and the app's board agree). Live in a dev mock (lead + 5 teammates, mixed states; `lab/RE-swarm-gaps/board-134`): board opens in ~90 ms, 6 fast toggles end open, a double-clicked row opens the chat once, Message focuses the composer, a teammate starting work shows on the open board in ~0.4 s, nothing overlaps or leaves the pane at 1100 px.
- **Not done:** no sidebar-folder entry and no all-leads view in the app (the IPC takes `leadId: null`); no claim, dependencies or tasks of their own (R1-1's L part); the board does not read channel work; a remote server's board is empty.
- **Check:** have a lead agent send two teammates requests; open the lead's chat: the 👥 button in the header opens the Team pane; when a teammate answers with "Status: done", its row turns Done and its update row in the chat shows a Done chip.
